The chain reconstructs the request from the durable record, verifies/restores the request file, accepts the response through the manifest module (malformed/foreign/undeclared → `invalid_response`), publishes the response file, and dispatches with the accepted digests and action id only; the routing target is never accepted from the caller and a caller-supplied target field is structurally impossible (ignored). An already-answered wait accepts only the identical response (same action id and response digest), restores both files if needed, and dispatches nothing — recognized through the durable record, so it works across restarts. Failure mapping is by class and typed fields, never by message text: manifest validation → `invalid_request`/`invalid_response`, wait-store conflict → `wait_conflict`, other wait-store failures (incl. `durability_unknown`) → `wait_storage_failed` with zero dispatch, reducer rejections → `invalid_state` after the idempotency check, sink `not_committed` → `state_persist_failed` with the published manifest kept as an orphan and no automatic second dispatch, sink `durability_unknown` → `state_persist_failed` with the adopted candidate snapshot (the sink poisons itself, no further dispatch; a fresh sink later recognizes the durable record as idempotent success). Published manifests are never removed on state failures; diagnostics are content-free; unexpected errors propagate unchanged. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_wait_controller.test.ts` (39 tests) covers both happy paths with serialize→`validatePipelineV2RunState` round-trips, hostile extra caller fields, publication failures with zero dispatch, `not_committed` orphans with exact-retry byte reuse (inode/mtime), durability-unknown adoption with zero further dispatch, durable re-entry without a second command, conflicts, tampered/missing request files for responses (tampered forbids, missing is restored), malformed/undeclared/foreign/unknown responses, response publication conflicts, response `not_committed`/durability-unknown with orphan and candidate adoption, two sequential wait cycles keeping the first record byte-identical, Promise-barrier concurrency (identical/different requests and responses) with exactly one durable commit per pair, the dispatch-capture mutation barrier, poisoned-sink and run-id-mismatch pre-filesystem rejections, deep-freeze/mutation isolation, canary-free diagnostics, byte-identity outside the sink protocol, a no-message-parsing source scan, and the export surface, plus the structured-action battery (structured ≡ raw canonical bytes/digest/state, one dispatch per path, request-only target derivation with hostile target fields ignored, undeclared action with zero writes/dispatch, two action ids sharing one target differing by digest, a Proxy over the options reading only the four validated members, structured idempotent retry after the raw answer, and a different-action conflict without writes). Still unwired: P01 validation, budget grants beyond the intervention commands, model replacements, API/T3, and migrations. Concurrent responding processes are serialized by the inter-process run mutation lock at the `respond` entrypoint; the wait store's exclusive-link idempotency and the sink's in-process dispatch serialization remain the inner defenses.
- Read-only pipeline v2 runtime context restoration (implemented, consumed by the coordinator resume entrypoint wired into `orchestrator resume`): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_resume_context.ts` rebuilds, from the trusted `ResolvedPipelineV2`, the durable state schema v7 and the fixed orchestrator-owned `<runRoot>`, exactly the runtime objects a future resume needs. Two public entrypoints share ONE private core (`restoreContextCore` — one provenance gate, one state validator, the parameterized boundary policy, one identity/run-root binding, one compiled transition replay, one execution-role verification, the layout checks, the input restoration and the one accepted-history reconstruction/verification) and differ only in the boundary policy and the final projection — there is no second restore mechanism: `restorePipelineV2RuntimeContext(pipeline, state, runRoot) -> RestoredPipelineV2RuntimeContext` — a deep-frozen, content-free `{state, run_inputs, accepted_outputs, cursor, next_execution_index}` where `state` is the normalized deep-frozen snapshot returned by the single existing `validatePipelineV2RunState` (never the caller object), `cursor` is a copy of the validated durable cursor, and `next_execution_index = executions.length + 1` counts decision executions too (agent activations share the global index, so activation directory numbers may skip after a decision); and `restorePipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext(pipeline, state, runRoot) -> RestoredPipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext` — the same shared chain with the planning-acceptance boundary policy and the projection `{state, run_inputs, accepted_outputs, cursor, planning_execution_index}` (the unbound planning execution's global index; no proposal, output value, plan/task manifests, candidate or compiled plan is returned, no acceptance window is classified, and no plan artifact or store is read). The API is strictly read-only: zero mkdir/write/chmod/link/rename/unlink/rm, no activation leaf checked or reserved, no Session, no sink call, no wait-manifest change, and the original user input bindings and project source are never re-read (after the run started they no longer exist as a source of truth). Trust boundary and order (fail-closed): `requireResolvedPipelineV2Provenance(pipeline, "pipeline v2 runtime context restoration")` is the very first action (before it the `state` is not read, the `runRoot` is not touched, no filesystem call happens, and a Proxy pipeline causes no getter or trap invocation); then `validatePipelineV2RunState` — the single state validator; the narrower resumable-boundary policy (never a second validator); the exact pipeline identity comparison against `pipelineV2RunPipelineIdentity` (nested schema version, canonical bundle root, execution snapshot digest, entry state, transition budget — no partial compatibility, no relocation tolerance; an entry-state mismatch is caught first by the loader's cursor replay as `invalid_state`) plus the run-id binding `basename(runRoot) === state.run_id`; the compiled-history verification replays the durable journal as the joint graph-transition + answered-wait cursor replay the state loader proves positionally — on every boundary the wait journal moves the replay cursor to the selected action's target (a state the compiled pipeline must declare) before the boundary's graph transition, resolved through the single `compiledTransitionFor` engine resolver — all before any filesystem access; the run-root layout (absolute, canonical, real non-symlink directory; `project`/`data`/`data/inputs` real non-symlink directories canonically resolving to their declared paths inside the canonical run root, with `data` and `data/inputs` keeping the orchestrator-owned mode 0700 while worker-writable `project` content and mode are never fixed or repaired); the run-input snapshot restoration; the accepted-history reconstruction and its full validation; only then the context. Resumable boundaries are clean boundaries without unfinished work only — active (`status: "active"`, `phase: "running"`, no terminal/run outputs/failure, `executions.length === transitions.length`, every execution settled — agent `cleanup_completed`, decision `evaluated — and therefore bound to its committed transition, and the last wait (if any) already answered; this includes the state right after `create_run` with an empty history) and waiting (`status`/`phase` both `"waiting"`, the last wait record open, all executions settled and bound, no terminal/run outputs/failure) — a waiting run's context is restorable but the restore itself continues nothing. Rejected as `invalid_state`: any in-flight agent or decision execution, a settled execution without its `transition_committed`, `publishing_outputs`, a reached terminal, the `success`/`failed`/`cleanup_failed` final states, and incoherent wait/cursor/history shapes. The planning-acceptance entrypoint accepts exactly one additional boundary: active running, clean, every execution settled, `executions.length === transitions.length + 1`, the unbound execution an agent `cleanup_completed` `execution_role === "planning"` execution without an `iteration_index` sitting exactly on the durable cursor, and the last wait (if any) already answered — the compiled correspondence of the recorded planning role stays in the shared execution-role chain (a durable planning role against a compiled stage/control assignment is `pipeline_mismatch`, not a boundary rejection), and the honest reducer path cannot even reach several of the forged shapes (a wait cannot be entered while an execution is unbound; a second unbound execution cannot be created), so the boundary checks are defense-in-depth on loader-valid documents. Typed closed failure contract `PipelineV2RuntimeContextRestoreError` with reasons assigned by validation phase, never by message text: `invalid_state` (schema v7 validation or non-resumable boundary), `pipeline_mismatch` (durable pipeline/input/output declaration metadata, run-id binding or compiled-transition-history mismatch — the durable journal is replayed through the single `compiledTransitionFor` engine resolver, including the answered-wait cursor relocation, before any filesystem access), `run_layout_invalid` (runRoot/project/data/inputs structure), `run_input_modified` (a fixed input object, kind, digest, JSON or schema), `accepted_output_modified` (a fixed accepted output object, kind, digest, JSON or schema); unexpected programmer errors propagate unchanged; diagnostics are content-free (no file/JSON bodies, no facts/prompts/env/credentials, no original user paths, no malformed-JSON parser fragments). The runtime data plane gained three narrowly scoped restoration helpers (all inside `pipeline_v2_runtime.ts`, no second registry, no general minter, no test seams): the exported `runInputSnapshotDigest` mirrors `acceptedOutputDigest` for the `pipeline-v2-input` domain (the internal digest loop of `verifyRunInputsSnapshot` was extracted into the shared `recomputeRunInputDigest` with byte-identical behavior), `mintRestoredRunInputsSnapshot` verifies every fixed `<runRoot>/data/inputs/<id>` object with the same fail-closed contract the activation path uses (kind, canonical self-resolution inside the canonical run root, clean trees, the exact input digest) plus a full JSON re-parse and revalidation against the declaring input's compiled schema — a matching digest alone never mints provenance for fabricated durable state with invalid JSON — and only then builds and registers the exact frozen `RunInputsSnapshot` in the same module-private `runInputSnapshotProvenance` registry `snapshotRunInputs` uses, so the existing provenance gates keep rejecting hand-built, cloned, spread, `structuredClone`d or proxied look-alikes; and `verifyRestoredAcceptedHistory` runs the single existing accepted-history chain (never a second resolver) over the records reconstructed from `state.executions` only — for every agent execution with durable outputs the record set must exactly equal the declared output ports of that state in declaration order (`pipeline_mismatch` otherwise; decision executions add nothing), every record including old non-winning ones is resolved to its fixed activation path, digest-verified with the exact `pipeline-v2-output` framing, and — via the new opt-in `jsonSchemaRevalidation` phase inside `resolveAcceptedHistory` (inert for the established consumers, whose runner-owned records were validated at acceptance time and whose typed failure reasons `run_output_invalid`/`decision_input_invalid` must not move to `accepted_output_modified`) — re-parsed and revalidated against the declaring output port's compiled schema. Reconstruction order is execution order × declaration order; types and paths are derived only from the trusted pipeline and the fixed layout and never enter the returned records (`{state, output, activation_index, digest}` exactly). `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_resume_context.test.ts` (68 tests) covers the full battery: the clean active boundary right after `create_run`, the waiting open boundary, the active boundary after a wait response (self-target; the explicit non-self-target relocation battery lives in the compiled-history battery), agent→decision→agent history with the global next execution index, repeated agent activations with deterministic accepted ordering, the restored snapshot accepted by both `prepareActivationData` and the decision adapter, clone/spread/`structuredClone`/Proxy snapshot look-alikes rejected by the existing provenance gate, Proxy pipelines rejected before any state read (zero state-proxy traps, sentinel untouched) or filesystem effect, deleted original sources never re-read, every pipeline identity field mismatch (the entry-state case documented as a loader-replay `invalid_state`), run-id/basename mismatch, durable input metadata mismatches (order/type/protected → `pipeline_mismatch`; digest → `run_input_modified`), missing/extra durable inputs, missing/symlink/wrong-kind/digest-mismatch inputs, directory inputs carrying symlinks or FIFOs, malformed and schema-invalid JSON inputs with matching forged digests, multi-activation accepted histories, decision executions adding no records, missing/extra/reordered/duplicate durable outputs (duplicates documented as loader rejections), phantom leaves/outputs, symlinked/wrong-kind/escaped output locations, corrupted old non-winning outputs, winning digest mismatches, malformed/schema-invalid JSON outputs with forged digests, all in-flight agent phases, in-flight decisions and settled-but-unbound executions, terminal/publishing/success/failed/cleanup-failed final states, zero filesystem mutation proven by a full-tree fingerprint (relative paths, kinds, modes, inodes, bytes) across success and every failure group, deep-freeze with caller-mutation isolation, deterministic repeated restoration, canary-free diagnostics, a no-message-parsing source scan, and the export surface (the class + the two restore functions only), plus the compiled-history battery (13 tests): a valid agent→decision→terminal and a valid cycle/repeated-state history restore; a structurally coherent wrong target / wrong `transition_index` / foreign-index declared outcome / undeclared selected outcome passes `validatePipelineV2RunState` (the loader/reducer accepts a self-coherent journal) and is rejected by restore as `pipeline_mismatch` — the layer boundary proven without touching loader or reducer; damage in an old non-last transition is detected; the mismatch wins over the run layout (stripped run tree still fails `pipeline_mismatch`, byte-identical tree); diagnostics are content-free (transition ordinal + safe ids only); input state and pipeline are never mutated; and the stage-lifecycle restore battery (8 tests) through real stage-template pipelines: the contract-order closure boundary with a same-boundary `normal_close` and with `exhausted` both pass the loader and the real restore verifier, the touching same-boundary close/open pair restores by membership in the admissible candidate set on both sides of the combination, and the touching generations keep the template-filtered exact lookups unique (the old and the new generation's executions under different templates; a reused template across plan stages passes restore as membership in the shared candidate set while the exact lookup returns `null` for the ambiguous boundary; an execution whose recorded index matches no template-matching candidate rejected as `pipeline_mismatch`). The coordinator's `resumePipelineV2Run` consumes exactly this context (its refusal battery lives in `tests/pipeline_v2_coordinator_resume.test.ts`), and the coordinator's plan-ready suspension seam consumes exactly `restorePipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext` (the same shared core, the planning-acceptance boundary policy). Still unwired: wait-state continuation, budget grants, profile replacement, P01, API/T3, and migrations.
- Planning-acceptance restore (implemented, read-only, **production-reachable directly through the coordinator's plan-ready seam**): `restorePipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext` in `pipeline_v2_resume_context.ts` accepts exactly the settled-but-unbound planning acceptance boundary through the SAME shared core as the ordinary resume restore (one provenance gate, one state validator, one identity/comparator, one compiled transition replay, one execution-role verification, one layout/input/accepted-history chain; the boundary policy is the only per-entrypoint difference besides the projection). It refuses the ordinary resume boundaries (the old API's exact messages are preserved byte-for-byte), accepts every fresh/partial/completed plan-acceptance window identically (the task/plan ledgers are not interpreted), and carries `planning_execution_index` instead of a next-execution index. Its battery (P1–P10 in the same test file) proves: the old restore's refusal and the new acceptance on the honest initial boundary, the replanning boundary (3 executions / 2 transitions) after a restart, the 0/1/2 durable task revisions and the fully durable target plan revision all accepted identically, the exact execution×port projection feeding `readAcceptedJsonOutput`, every non-planning/in-flight/failed/decision/stage/two-unbound/forged-cursor/open-wait/terminal/final shape typed `invalid_state`, the compiled role mismatch keeping `pipeline_mismatch`, the damaged layout/input/output reasons unchanged with a byte-identical tree, the Proxy pipeline rejected with zero state traps, and the source scan pinning exactly one validation chain, one accepted-record reconstruction, one shared verification call and no foreign imports (the planning-output composition is the acceptance layer's consumer and the anchored reconstruction of the plan acceptance itself). Still unwired: migrations/API/T3.
- Shared durable snapshot store substrate + pipeline v2 store/sink (implemented, wired into the production runner, not wired into the CLI): `orchestrator/src/run_snapshot_store.ts` is the schema-agnostic substrate for revisioned JSON snapshot stores, extracted from the former `pipeline_state_store.ts` with byte-identical v1 behavior — it owns the `<state-root>/pipeline-runs/<run-id>/state.json` layout, the injectable `PipelineStateIo` seam (real implementation plus deterministic test fault injection), 0700 directories / 0600 state and temp files, the `O_EXCL` temp file in the run directory, the full write → file fsync → close → atomic rename → directory fsync → close protocol, temp unlink on pre-rename failure, the state root is the trusted boundary and every store-owned component below it is verified separately — `load()` creates nothing and requires `pipeline-runs` and the run directory to be real non-symlink directories (missing at any level returns null), while `create`/`commit` build the tree component by component (`pipeline-runs` created 0700 and verified, then the run directory created 0700 and verified, and only then the temp file and commit protocol; no recursive creation of the full run directory before `pipeline-runs` is checked), with symlink refusals for the run directory and the state target, `create` with overwrite refusal, `commit` with on-disk revision verification and the exactly-+1 rule, load through an injected parser (parser/validator errors propagate unchanged, never masked as I/O errors), and in-process commit serialization; multi-process locking stays out of scope. `pipeline_state_store.ts` is now the v1 compatibility adapter over that substrate: `PipelineStateStore`, `PipelineStateStoreError`, `PipelineStateDurabilityError`, `pipelineRunStatePath`, and the re-exported seam keep the same public names, messages, and observable semantics (the unchanged v1 suite passes). `pipeline_v2_state_store.ts` is the v2 typed adapter (`PipelineV2RunStateStoreError` `not_committed` / `PipelineV2RunStateDurabilityError` `durability_unknown` with the exact candidate `PipelineV2RunState`) that validates the run id before any filesystem I/O with the single state-schema-v7 safe-id grammar (`pipeline_v2_state.ts`'s exported `expectSafeId`: `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,127}`, no `..`) shared by the store constructor, the sink, `pipelineV2RunStatePath()` (which validates itself), and the reducer's `create_run`, and loads through `parsePipelineV2RunState`. `pipeline_v2_state_sink.ts` adds `PipelineV2RunStateSink({stateRoot, runId, io?, now?})` with `dispatch(command)` over the existing `PipelineV2RunCommand` union — current snapshot → `reducePipelineV2RunCommand` → atomic store create/commit → adopt the candidate only after the commit outcome is known; `create_run.runId` must match the sink's run id; reducer rejections write nothing; `not_committed` keeps the previous revision (the caller may then dispatch the normalized `run_failed: state_persist_failed` when the reducer accepts it); `durability_unknown` adopts the visible candidate, poisons the sink, and propagates; a poisoned sink refuses every dispatch before the reducer and before any filesystem I/O; dispatches of one run are serialized; no lifecycle, worker-error, or terminal policy and no automatic finalize. Opening an already durable run is implemented as the explicit async factory `await PipelineV2RunStateSink.open(params)` — the single way to continue an existing run after a process restart: it loads exclusively through the existing `PipelineV2RunStateStore.load()` and its single state-schema-v7 parser/validator, refuses a missing state document or a document whose `run_id` differs from the sink's run id with a typed `PipelineV2RunStateStoreError` (never a fresh sink), installs the exact normalized loaded snapshot as the initial `snapshot` so the next `dispatch` performs an ordinary commit from the loaded revision, and performs zero filesystem mutations at open (no `create_run`, no rewriting, chmodding or repairing); the fresh constructor and all its observable semantics are unchanged, and reducer/store-protocol/durability-poisoning are not duplicated — the factory returns only a fully initialized sink. Fault-injection coverage lives in `tests/pipeline_v2_state_store.test.ts` and `tests/pipeline_v2_state_sink.test.ts` (extended `state_io_test_helpers.ts`: file `close`, `dirfsync`, `dirclose` fault steps, stacked counting io) — full commit matrix, permissions, symlinks, revision mismatch/gap, concurrent dispatch serialization, and the unchanged v1 regression suite; the open battery lives in `tests/pipeline_v2_state_sink_open.test.ts` (loaded-snapshot commit continuation, reopen round-trips, typed missing-state and foreign-run-id refusals creating nothing, parse-error propagation with byte-identity, resumed-sink durability poisoning, safe-id validation before any I/O, and the fresh constructor semantics beside open). Still unwired: `agent-smoke` (the v1 diagnostic path).
- Multi-state execution and activation/session order (implemented): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_runner.ts` (`runMultiStatePipeline`) drives `executePipelineGraph`; `pipeline_runner.ts`'s `runActivation` is the per-activation executor and owns only the agent run. Per agent-state activation, in strict order: durable `activation_started` → runtime input existence/regular-file/containment checks (`RuntimeInputError` → `runtime_input_missing`, before Session creation) → fail-closed activation control-tree preparation → re-resolution of every declared protected input against its recorded baseline (canonical target + `dev`/`ino` + digest, `WorkspaceInputError` → `protected_input_modified`, before Session creation) → a final synchronous `gate.checkAbort()` with no await before `createChildSession` (a signal recorded during the durable `start_activation` or the filesystem preparation can no longer create a Session) → create child Session → immediately durable `session_created` → pull image (non-fatal) → durable `agent_running` → verify the expected `result.json` is absent (`ControlPathError` → `control_path_invalid`) → `docker-helper run` with the state's `timeout_seconds` (signalable, SIGTERM at the deadline) → re-verify the full filesystem identity of all protected inputs (declared path re-resolved fresh; canonical target, `dev`, `ino`, digest) → verify the result identity (`run_id`/`state_id`/`activation_index`/`attempt`), schema, artifact confinement, and protected-input aliasing (against the confirmed baseline identity) → read `result.json` without following symlinks (regular non-symlink file inside the exact activation leaf, O_NOFOLLOW read) → durable `result_accepted` (digest + artifacts, never the summary) → delete the child Session exactly once → durable `session_cleanup_completed` → return only the validated outcome; the engine's transition-commit hook then records transition + cursor in one commit before the next callback. Sessions are never reused between states or revisits; the bearer stays in memory. Launcher authority is checked once per pipeline run; all profiles load before it. On failure the active Session is still deleted exactly once; a cleanup failure stops the pipeline with its prior priority; without a successful cleanup outcome no engine outcome is returned and no transition is applied; a signal blocks the next Session creation, and a signal during Session create lets the create finish, deletes the Session, and never runs the worker. Signal/timeout first-wins is shared with plain `smoke` through the `RunCauseGate` in `lifecycle.ts` (failure/cleanup boxes + the synchronous run-level cutoff before the single final state write); plain `smoke` keeps its existing behavior.
- Activation control tree (implemented): all orchestrator-side activation paths are built from the fixed canonical workspace recorded before Launcher auth — never from the raw CLI workspace alias — via `prepareActivationControlTree`: every parent component (`<ws>/.pipeline-agent-smoke`, `<runId>`, `activations`) is a real non-symlink directory (created 0700 if missing), the activation leaf `<index>-<stateId>` and its `attempt-1` child must be absent (an existing leaf, symlink, or unexpected object is a `ControlPathError` before any Session is created), the leaves are created as new directories with `mkdir` (no recursion, `EEXIST` rejected), and `execution.md` is written with `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` so a pre-placed file or symlink is never followed or overwritten. The whole tree must still resolve inside the canonical workspace after preparation. Before the worker starts the expected `result.json` must be absent; after the run it must be a regular non-symlink file inside the exact activation leaf and is read through an `O_NOFOLLOW` file descriptor (`readResultFileNoFollow`); no external sentinel is ever created, modified, or read through a substituted control path.
- Protected-input binding (implemented): a baseline protected input keeps the declared pipeline path, the canonical target, `dev`, `ino`, and the digest as separate fields; the durable `protected_inputs[].path` is the declared path, not a rewritten canonical-relative alias. Directly before every Session and again after every worker run (before the result can be accepted), `verifyProtectedInputs` re-resolves the declared path fresh and requires canonical target, `dev`, `ino`, and digest to match the baseline; a retargeted in-workspace symlink, a replaced inode with identical bytes, a changed file type, a disappearance, or a content change fails as `protected_input_modified` (`WorkspaceInputError`), and a pre-activation mismatch creates no Session. An unchanged internal symlink remains acceptable. Artifact direct/symlink/hardlink checks run against the confirmed baseline identity of all protected inputs.
- CLI: `bun src/main.ts smoke|agent-smoke`. `agent-smoke` takes `--config-root ABSOLUTE_PATH` and optional `--pipeline-root ABSOLUTE_PATH` (default `/opt/orchestrator/pipelines/default`), `--workspace`, `--launcher-id`; `--profile` and `--task` are removed — the execution profile and the workspace input paths are selected only by the pipeline's agent states — and `--image` stays rejected (the image comes only from the selected profile). Plain `smoke` keeps `--image` and is unchanged. `agent-smoke` needs the docker-helper socket and a **Launcher** credential (never a Principal/admin credential) at `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/docker-helper/credential.token`. Pipeline/plan/profile/input validation happens before Launcher auth: failures exit 1 with `runId: ""` and create no session. `orchestrator run` takes `--pipeline-root` **optional**: an explicit absolute path keeps the previous behavior, and the absence selects the bundled pipeline v2 default (`DEFAULT_PIPELINE_V2_ROOT` = `/opt/orchestrator/pipelines/default-v2`); the first successful `run` returns the `planReady` outcome and the next manual step for the default bundle is `resume-plan --stage-id stage-1 --initial-budget 1`. The default selection is parser-owned: the parser passes the already-resolved absolute `pipelineRoot` to the runner — no second default-path mechanism exists in main/runner/loader. The CLI now also exposes `orchestrator run` — the production pipeline v2 command wired through `runCli` in `main.ts` (see the CLI wiring bullet): it parses arguments, resolves the state-root projection from the environment, invokes `runPipelineV2` exactly once and returns its `exitCode`; `agent-smoke` remains the v1 diagnostic command with unchanged behavior.
- Agent worker input: the pipeline prompt travels only inside an orchestrator-owned per-activation execution document (`<run-dir>/activations/<activation-index>-<state-id>/attempt-1/execution.md`, non-secret: run id, state id, activation index, attempt, workspace-relative input/result paths, allowed outcome, prompt body, exact result format) written inside the existing run directory; the OpenCode command receives a short static instruction pointing at that document. Each activation's `result.json` lives next to it; a revisit cannot see a previous activation's result as its own. Prompt and input bodies never appear in argv, env, state, or orchestrator diagnostics; the pipeline bundle and config root are not mounted into the worker (workers get the workspace only). Each state's inputs are checked right before its activation (existence, regular file, inside the canonical workspace); unprotected inputs may be created by earlier states; a missing runtime input fails the run before that state's Session is created. After every agent run the digests of all protected inputs are re-verified and artifacts aliasing any protected input (direct, symlink, or hardlink) are rejected. The state's `timeout_seconds` is enforced only on the signalable worker `docker-helper run`: the runner sends SIGTERM at the deadline, marks the result timed out, and the run fails normally (exit 1, single cleanup); user SIGINT/SIGTERM keeps exit 130/143.
- docker-helper interaction: `session create/delete` and `pull`/`run` go through the **official CLI 2.2.0-rc.10**, spawned as an argv array (`Bun.spawn`, no shell); the narrow `GET /auth` check stays a direct unix-socket call (the CLI has no equivalent Launcher-authority command). `session create/delete` run with a minimal filtered env (`HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `DOCKER_HELPER_CONFIG`); `pull`/`run` run with the child Session bearer only (`DOCKER_HELPER_SESSION_TOKEN`), which is repeated as `--env` so the worker receives its Session capability. Worker env values are passed as separate `--env KEY=VALUE` arguments; until docker-helper issue #3 is implemented, resolved worker env (including secrets and the OpenCode config content) is visible in the `docker-helper run` process argv — a consciously accepted temporary risk. They never appear in state files or the orchestrator's own diagnostics. Worker output is inherited from the CLI process: docker-helper 2.2.0-rc.10 prints the (already mixed) container operation output on its stdout, and the CLI's own warnings/errors go to stderr; raw worker output is treated as untrusted (never a safe audit log or authoritative state); the orchestrator implements no polling, log cursor, or operation-API parsing.
- Cancellation contract: the first SIGINT/SIGTERM is recorded by the lifecycle; a running `docker-helper run` process receives the same signal (a bounded synchronous best-effort cancel inside docker-helper 2.2.0-rc.10 — the orchestrator never confirms a terminal operation state) under a first-wins rule: the first terminal cause of the active signalable worker — runner `timeout` or `user_signal` — is recorded once; a user signal that wins suppresses the timer (`timedOut` stays false, exit 130/143), and a user signal that loses to the runner timer is neither delivered nor forwarded to the lifecycle (the run stays a timeout failure, exit 1). `killActive` returns whether the signal may still be recorded as a lifecycle abort; signals outside a worker run keep the plain lifecycle semantics. `session create`, `pull`, and `session delete` always run to completion. Cleanup (child Session delete) happens only in the single lifecycle `finally`, after the active hook settles — no fire-and-forget cleanup. The signal cutoff sits synchronously after cleanup and before the single authoritative final state write: causes are snapshotted and signal acceptance closes with no await in between, then exactly one `finalize` writes the already-decided terminal status; a signal delivered after the cutoff — including while the final write is in flight — is late and can no longer change the recorded outcome or the exit code, and the terminal run status is never rewritten.
- `--workspace` is the absolute host workspace path used by docker-helper; it must also be visible at that same absolute path inside the orchestrator container (the proven dual-mount pattern: mount the Session workspace twice — canonical host path + `/workspace`). Workers mount the Session workspace at `/workspace` (`source "."`); the host path need not be reproduced inside workers. A helper-launched container cannot receive the docker-helper socket (workspace-relative sources only), so a containerized orchestrator must be launched by the operator (plain `docker run` with socket + credential + dual mounts); this docker-helper limitation blocks the helper-launched UAT path until helper gains trusted socket projection.
- Decision-table substrate (pure, implemented, **not yet used by the production runner**): `orchestrator/src/decision.ts` implements a declarative decision-table format (YAML, `schema_version: 1`, exact-field validation), a fail-closed loader/compiler into a deep-frozen engine-owned snapshot, and a pure deterministic evaluator. Expressions are a closed boolean DSL (`{fact, equals}` / `{all}` / `{any}` / `{not}`) with static limits (64 facts/decisions/relations/constraints/rules, depth 16, 512 nodes per expression, 4096 total, 128-char ids). Evaluation order: fail-closed fact validation (missing/extra/non-boolean facts throw `DecisionModelError`, never `uncovered`) → relations in declaration order (`inconsistent_facts` with all violated ids in order) → allowed set narrowed by active hard constraints in declaration order (`only` intersects, `forbid` removes) → first rule whose `when` matches and whose decision is still allowed (matched-but-forbidden rules never terminate the search) → `uncovered` as a normal result (no fallback). Loader containment reuses the shared helpers from `orchestrator/src/bundle_file.ts` (extracted from `pipeline.ts` unchanged; `pipeline.ts` passes its error class and scope wording): absolute real-directory root, clean `.yaml`-only bundle-relative path, internal symlinks allowed, symlink escapes and lexical traversal rejected, regular files only. `pipelines/default/decisions/architect.yaml` is the runtime decision model mechanically transferred from `docs/pipeline-oracle/decision-table.json` (11 facts, FC1–FC6, HC1–HC5, 7 rules in original priority order, exact decision ids, no automatic fallback); source line references, explanatory text, stage effects, state writes, recovery semantics, and P01 are deliberately not part of it — P01 remains the next policy layer above the base evaluator. `orchestrator/tests/decision.test.ts` proves equivalence with the unchanged oracle over all 2048 boolean assignments in `bit_order` (1952 inconsistent, 82 selected with exact frozen decisions + rule ids via an independent transcription, 14 uncovered, exact distribution) plus generic unit tests (arbitrary non-legacy ids, limits, containment, snapshot immutability, repeated-evaluation determinism). `pipeline.yaml`, `pipeline_engine.ts`, `pipeline_runner.ts`, `agent-smoke`, the durable state schema, and the CLI do not reference any of this yet; wiring it into production is a later increment.
- Pipeline schema v2 data ports + activation layout planner (pure, implemented, **not yet used by the production runner**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2.ts` implements the `schema_version: 2` compile branch as a separate, self-contained module. `pipeline_v2.ts` also fixes the v2 agent-state contract: every agent state declares exactly one transition with outcome `completed` (the target is the user's choice and may lead to an agent, decision or terminal state) and `max_attempts` exactly 1; zero transitions, several transitions, duplicate `completed` transitions, any other outcome, and `max_attempts` 0/non-integer/>1 are rejected with a `PipelineError` during the trusted load before provenance registration — content-based branching belongs to a decision state, agent failures are not transitions, and retries are not implemented yet (state schema v7 supports only `attempt: 1`). This is the current executable semantics of schema v2, not a default-pipeline rule. v2 describes data flow as named ports instead of workspace paths: run-level `inputs` (logical run inputs; host paths are bound later by CLI/API, never declared in the document) and run-level `outputs` (data handed to the user after completion, `required: true` outputs fail the run if absent at the terminal, optional outputs may be absent); every agent state declares local `inputs`/`outputs` ports; each port source has exactly one form (`{pipeline_input: ID}` or `{state_output: {state, output}}`); port types are `file`/`directory`/`json`; `json` ports require a bundle-relative JSON schema file (loaded through the same realpath containment, parsed as a JSON object, no generic JSON Schema validation). Pipeline inputs and agent outputs are the only places that declare a type or schema; agent input ports and run outputs derive both from their source — a run output is exactly `{id, required, source}` (declaring `type`/`schema` on it is rejected), and a stricter output contract is a separate transforming state, not a re-interpretation of the same value. The JSON contract travels by data flow: a run-level json input resolves and keeps its `schemaPath/schema`; an agent input port sourced from it (directly or through `state_output`) receives the derived type and the immutable schema snapshot value; schema paths stay with the declaring sites. Runtime semantics fixed in the module doc: a `state_output` value is the last successfully accepted output of the named state from an earlier activation; if none exists the run fails closed without fallback; self-references and cycles between state outputs compile (value availability is a runtime question); no user port paths, no mount targets, and `image`/`env`/`mounts`/`command`/docker/helper/session options remain forbidden; the activation completion envelope is orchestrator-owned and work products are the declared outputs only (no free-form artifact list). `loadPipelineV2` returns an engine-owned deep-frozen snapshot; `planActivationLayout(pipeline, stateId)` is a pure deterministic planner producing an immutable plan: ordered input ports (`source`, `type`, optional deep-frozen schema snapshot without `schemaPath`, fixed target `/pipeline/inputs/<id>`, `read_only: true`), ordered output ports (`type`, optional parsed schema snapshot, fixed target `/pipeline/outputs/<id>`, `read_only: false`), project mount `/workspace` RW, `reject_undeclared_outputs: true`, and only logical/structural data (no bearers, credentials, env values, host paths, or schema paths). Provenance is fail-closed before any target path is built: `planActivationLayout` first checks membership in a module-private `WeakSet` registry that only the fully resolved, deep-frozen snapshot returned by `loadPipelineV2` is registered in; hand-built objects, casts, shallow/deep clones (`structuredClone`), Proxies and corrupted objects are rejected with a stable `PipelineError` before any field is read (getters never run), and there is no second validation pass — structural correctness is owned once by the compiler. `stateId` remains a plain planner input (safe id + agent state of the trusted snapshot). `pipeline.ts` shares exact-field validators + `checkGraphShape` with v2 and rejects **any** YAML document with `schema_version: 2` at the production loader with exactly `pipeline schema version 2 is not executable yet` before Launcher auth and before any Session (no "genuine v2" shape sniffing). Session capability contracts are fixed next to the planner, unwired: Execution Session (`type: "execution"`, scope run root, orchestrator-only bearer, never passed to a worker in any form), Tool Session (`type: "tool"`, scope project only, bearer handed to the worker, nested containers cannot reach pipeline inputs/outputs; no wide-Tool-Session workaround exists), and the separate immutable Worker Launch contract (`WORKER_LAUNCH_CONTRACT`: worker launched via the Execution Session receives a projected helper socket — the socket is transport, the Tool Session bearer is the worker's authority); worker mounts project `/workspace` RW, prepared activation inputs `/pipeline/inputs` RO, activation outputs `/pipeline/outputs` RW; docker-helper#8 (allowed-roots RO/RW) is not required by this increment. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2.test.ts` covers v1 regression, v2 compilation, exact-field/union validation, duplicate/unknown references, schema propagation through `state_output`, schema containment, frozen snapshots, planner determinism/targets/flags, plan secret/host-path/schema-path absence, planner fail-closed regressions (forged port ids, hand-built/cloned/proxied objects, getters never invoked, cyclic schemas, duplicate state casts), socket-transport/Tool-authority separation, and the production rejection before auth/session. The production runner, durable state, lifecycle, signal handling, helper transport, and the default bundle are untouched by this substrate; wiring v2 execution into `agent-smoke` is a later increment.

- Pipeline schema v2 compiled orchestration metadata (pure, implemented; consumed by the production planning chain and the restore verifier — the coordinator's state-type dispatch remains role-agnostic): a v2 pipeline may declare an optional top-level `orchestration` section — the single, complete and trusted compiled source for the execution role of every agent/decision state (`planning | control | stage`) and for stage templates (`stage_templates[]` with exactly `{id, entry_state}`; `execution_roles[]` where planning entries are exactly `{state_id, role, plan_output, stage_wait}` (the declarative plan-output binding — implemented, compiled, consumed at runtime through the planning chain: `compiledExecutionRoleFor` → `readAcceptedJsonOutput` → the run-plan proposal — plus the mandatory trusted stage-wait policy `stage_wait: {reason, actions}`: the wait `reason` and the declared intervention actions for entering the intervention wait when the coordinator reaches this planning state after a completed stage execution while its iteration is open; exact own enumerable fields at every level with inherited properties never satisfying the required shape, the `reason` carrying the same scalar contract the wait request manifest accepts — the shared `isPipelineV2SafeId` predicate of `pipeline_v2_scalar.ts` (the manifest's private `expectSafeId` is only its error-formatting wrapper), no second normalization or wider grammar — `actions` a non-empty list of unique ids from the closed `PIPELINE_V2_STAGE_WAIT_ACTION_NAMES` vocabulary (`continue_stage | revise_task`) preserved in declaration order (never sorted), no routing targets in the metadata (a `continue_stage` target is derived later from the open generation's compiled stage entry, a `revise_task` target from the planning cursor), control/stage roles rejecting `stage_wait` as an unknown field, no defaults and no inference from `plan_output`, transitions, stage templates or stage order; stage-wait diagnostics are content-free in the wait-manifest sense — unknown field names are never named and no hostile value is ever echoed), control entries exactly `{state_id, role}`, and stage entries exactly `{state_id, role, stage_template}`). There is no default classification and no inference from profiles, state names, prompts, model paths or content; terminal states never carry a role. Structural invariants checked once at trusted compile (`compilePipelineV2Spec`, after `checkGraphShape`): either list may be empty (a pipeline without agent or decision states — e.g. a terminal-only pipeline — declares no roles, and one without stage templates declares no templates; the lists' non-emptiness follows from the pipeline's states, not from a structural minimum); template ids, template entry states and role state ids unique; every role names a declared non-terminal state of a matching kind (planning → agent, control → decision, stage → agent or decision); every agent/decision state is listed exactly once; every stage role references a declared template; each template owns at least one stage state; every template entry state exists and carries that template's stage role; a planning role's `plan_output` names exactly one declared JSON output port of its own agent state (unknown and non-JSON outputs rejected at trusted load inside the same orchestration structural check — no second parser or validator). Template topology (also compile-time, per template): all stage states reachable from the template entry along transitions staying inside the template; transitions between stage states of different templates forbidden; a transition from a state outside the template into a stage state allowed only into that template's entry state; exits to planning/control/terminal states allowed; the template entry itself need not have an incoming graph transition (a future wait response may set the cursor there) and the run's initial cursor position stays governed by `checkGraphShape`. Declaration order of the section's entries is not semantic: the resolved metadata is normalized (stage templates sorted by id, execution roles sorted by state_id — code-unit order), so permuting equivalent entries yields the identical resolved metadata, execution snapshot JSON and digest, while changing a role, membership, template entry, `plan_output` value or any stage-wait policy field (reason, action set, action order) moves the digest. `ResolvedPipelineV2` carries the metadata as the optional deep-frozen `orchestration?: ResolvedPipelineV2Orchestration` field (resolved entry shapes as the discriminated union above); bundles without the section compile byte-identically to before (the snapshot has no `orchestration` key, not null/empty). The pure read-only resolver `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_orchestration.ts` exposes exactly `PipelineV2OrchestrationError`, `compiledExecutionRoleFor(pipeline, stateId)` and `compiledStageTemplateFor(pipeline, templateId)` (returning `{id, entry_state, state_ids}` with state ids in the normalized role order): provenance (`requireResolvedPipelineV2Provenance`) fires before the id check and any content read (clones/spreads/`structuredClone`/Proxies rejected with traps never invoked), missing metadata/unknown state/unknown template/unsafe ids are typed errors, results are deep-frozen and built only from the normalized metadata (a planning role resolves to exact deep-frozen `{state_id, role, plan_output, stage_wait}` with the nested `stage_wait` and its actions array frozen fresh per call), caller objects are never mutated or frozen, and diagnostics name safe ids only. No role/template registry is added to the durable `PipelineV2RunPipelineIdentity` — `execution_snapshot_sha256` stays the single durable anchor. The execution snapshot includes the normalized orchestration when present (`pipeline_v2_digest.ts` embeds the already-normalized resolved field; no second serializer). Durable state and the coordinator's state-type dispatch are untouched; the compiled roles/templates are consumed at runtime by the resume-restore verifier, the stage-iteration controller and the production planning chain (the compiled planning `plan_output` feeds the run-plan proposal). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_orchestration.test.ts` (45 tests — including the `plan_output` binding matrix, the exact planning shape and the no-inference/no-fallback proof that a lone JSON output without `plan_output` does not make a bundle load, plus the stage-wait battery: minimal/full policies and both action subsets with both full-set permutations, the exact-field and own-property batteries, the reason/actions validation matrices without value echo, `stage_wait` rejected on control/stage roles, declared-order preservation, recursive freeze and input-mutation isolation, the exact compiled shape, the provenance battery, content-free diagnostics, and the single-chain source scan) and `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_digest.test.ts` (42 tests, `plan_output` digest sensitivity included, plus the stage-wait digest battery: reason-only, action-set-only and action-order-only mutations each moving the digest, and an identical policy digest-deterministic across loads and bundle locations). The stage-wait policy is digest-bound, compiled and consumed by the production wait entry: the coordinator recognizes the exact stage→planning boundary after its own committed transition (and on the restart path before the engine continues) and enters the wait through the public `enterPipelineV2Wait` with the policy-derived reason/actions.
- Compiled run-plan projection over the orchestration metadata (pure substrate, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_compiled.ts` binds one prepared run plan candidate (the exact deep-frozen `PreparedPipelineV2RunPlanCandidate`) to the trusted compiled orchestration metadata of a `ResolvedPipelineV2` — the single prepared projection the run plan acceptance controller consumes. Runtime export surface is exactly `PipelineV2CompiledRunPlanError`, `compilePipelineV2RunPlanCandidate(pipeline, candidate)` and `compiledPipelineV2RunPlanStageFor(compiledPlan, stageId)`; types are erased. Provenance order is fixed: the pipeline gate (`requireResolvedPipelineV2Provenance`) first, then the candidate gate through the existing boolean `hasPreparedRunPlanCandidateProvenance` (a registry lookup — the candidate's getters/Proxy traps never fire), and both before any manifest field is read; a rejected candidate is the typed `PipelineV2CompiledRunPlanError("invalid_candidate")` with a fixed content-free diagnostic. The projection is content-free and deep-frozen: `{run_id, plan_revision, plan_sha256, origin_execution, stages: [{id, template, entry_state, state_ids, tasks: [{id, revision, sha256, depends_on}]}]}` — stages keep the plan manifest's semantic declaration order, tasks/`depends_on` keep the manifest's normalized order, `entry_state`/`state_ids` come exclusively from the trusted `compiledStageTemplateFor` (template reuse cached per compile; missing orchestration and unknown templates propagate as the unchanged `PipelineV2OrchestrationError`, never masked or re-classified), `origin_execution` is transferred as the normalized number only (durable verification is the future controller's), and no task bodies, canonical JSON, filesystem paths or prepared-object references enter the result (fresh arrays, no aliasing). The compiled plan is registered in a module-private `WeakMap`-based registry (internal module `pipeline_v2_run_plan_compiled_internal.ts`, exactly three keys `registerCompiledRunPlanIdentity`/`hasCompiledRunPlanProvenance`/`compiledRunPlanOriginIdentity`) that binds every projection to the immutable snapshot of its originating pipeline's durable run identity, built exclusively by the existing `pipelineV2RunPipelineIdentity` construction point immediately before the successful return; the identity is not part of the public projection shape or its bytes. `compiledPipelineV2RunPlanStageFor` gates on that registry before reading any field (hand-built/spread/`structuredClone`/Proxy rejected with zero traps, `invalid_plan`), validates `stageId` against the shared scalar safe-id predicate without echoing an invalid value (`invalid_stage_id`), returns the exact frozen stage of the projection, and reports an unknown stage as typed `stage_not_found` naming only validated safe ids. No second candidate registry, no public registry/minter/test seam, no own topology validator, no message-text classification. Out of scope (untouched): state schema v7, execution roles in durable executions, lifecycle journals, filesystem publication (the run-plan store owns it); `origin_execution` verification and controller/coordinator/runner consumption are implemented; still unwired: grants/effective-budget policy, wait/replanning policy, migrations/API/T3. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_compiled.test.ts` (27 tests: additionally the hidden-identity binding — frozen, equal to a fresh `pipelineV2RunPipelineIdentity` result, never an alias of a caller object — mutation impossibility, the no-hidden-identity projection shape/bytes, registry non-membership of lookalikes, the internal registry surface pin).
- Compiled run-plan acceptance boundary (pure substrate, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_acceptance.ts` adds the controller-owned compiled validation that decides whether a prepared run plan candidate is ready for the future durable acceptance; the invariant is that the candidate's `plan.origin_execution` names the LAST successful settled-but-unbound agent execution of the current run and the compiled execution role of its state is `planning` (resolved only through `compiledExecutionRoleFor`; the role is never inferred from execution type, profile, state name, prompt or graph position). Runtime export surface is exactly `PipelineV2RunPlanAcceptanceError` (closed reasons `invalid_state | pipeline_mismatch | candidate_mismatch | origin_execution_mismatch | origin_role_mismatch`, immutable non-writable `reason`, never classified from message text) and `verifyPipelineV2RunPlanCandidateForAcceptance(pipeline, state, candidate)` — types erased. Fixed gate order: pipeline provenance gate first (before the state or candidate are read; Proxy state/candidate traps stay at zero) → `compilePipelineV2RunPlanCandidate` (candidate provenance + template resolution; forged candidate → the compiled layer's `invalid_candidate`, missing orchestration/unknown template → the unchanged `PipelineV2OrchestrationError`, both before the state is touched) → `validatePipelineV2RunState` as the single state validator (`PipelineV2StateError` → `invalid_state`) → the exact durable pipeline identity via the single shared structural comparator (`pipeline_v2_identity_compare.ts`, also used by the byte-identical resume verifier; `pipeline_mismatch` per field) → the acceptance boundary (`invalid_state`: active+running, no terminal/run_outputs/failure, no open wait, `executions.length === transitions.length + 1`, the LAST execution is the single settled-but-unbound one, agent + `cleanup_completed`; no own cursor replay — the loader already proved cursor coherence) → candidate/run binding (`candidate_mismatch`) → origin = the last array element only, never a searched older execution (`origin_execution_mismatch`) → compiled role must be `planning` (`origin_role_mismatch`). Success returns the exact provenance-backed compiled plan object from the verifier's single internal `compilePipelineV2RunPlanCandidate` call (no copy, no second registry; the stage lookup accepts it). Loader/reducer unchanged; the run plan acceptance controller is the production consumer of this verifier (via the planning-output composition, reached from the runner and the CLI). Still unwired: wait/replanning policy, grants, migrations/API/T3. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_acceptance.test.ts` (23 tests) plus the resume-context identity-mismatch diagnostics pinned byte-identical in `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_resume_context.test.ts`.
- Run plan acceptance controller (production-neutral, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_controller.ts` (public) + `pipeline_v2_run_plan_controller_internal.ts` (internal core) is the single layer that binds the existing run-plan layers into one crash-safe order — full compiled/state validation through the single acceptance verifier → reducer pre-check of the whole missing durable sequence on a local snapshot → publication of the task manifests (candidate order, strictly sequential) → publication of the plan manifest as the filesystem commit marker → durable `task_revision_accepted` for the missing task records → durable `plan_revision_accepted` as the state commit marker. Runtime export surface is exactly `PipelineV2RunPlanControllerError` (closed reasons `invalid_state | candidate_conflict | state_persist_failed`, immutable `reason`, last authoritative `state`) and `acceptPipelineV2RunPlanCandidate({pipeline, runRoot, sink, candidate})` over the structural `PipelineV2RunPlanControllerSink {snapshot, poisoned, dispatch}` seam (the production `PipelineV2RunStateSink` satisfies it); the deep-frozen result is `{compiled_plan, state}` — the exact provenance-backed compiled plan object from the verifier's single compile (never copied, never re-compiled) and the last authoritative sink snapshot; prepared manifests, canonical JSON, task bodies, filesystem paths and the caller candidate never enter the result or the content-free diagnostics. No second state validator, identity comparator, candidate compiler or serializer and no own successor rules: the acceptance verifier, the reducer (`reducePipelineV2RunCommand` — the whole missing sequence pre-checked before any filesystem effect; the authoritative state comes exclusively from the sink), the candidate publisher (`publishPipelineV2RunPlanCandidate` — called exactly once; tasks before the plan marker; store errors keep their original class and identity) and the schema v7 ledgers are reused unchanged, and the acceptance/orchestration/compiled/manifest/binding/publication errors are never masked or re-classified. Reconciliation (structural, fail-closed, before any publication or dispatch): a candidate task is already durable exactly when the ledger carries the exact `task_id`+`revision` pair with the candidate's sha256 and previous_sha256 AND that pair is the latest durable revision of the task id — an earlier revision exists only as a chain predecessor, and a durable revision newer than the candidate's is a downgrade `candidate_conflict` (never re-dispatched); the same pair with a different digest/chain, the same task id at a different revision, or a missing task revision above 1 (a user-response revision cannot be created on the active planning boundary) are `candidate_conflict` failures with zero writes; missing revision-1 tasks are dispatched in candidate order; the durable plan record matching the candidate revision/sha256/previous_sha256/origin_execution exactly is idempotent durable success only when it is the last durable plan revision, with every candidate task required durable (a missing one is a conflict); a plan record with different content, a durable ledger that has moved past the candidate revision (stale candidate), a candidate revision ahead of the next expected ledger revision (ahead/gap), or a candidate chain not linking the durable ledger are conflicts; durable task revisions for tasks outside the candidate never conflict by themselves. Dispatch: after the publication, the missing task records in candidate order then the plan record; after every dispatch the authoritative sink snapshot is re-read and must structurally carry exactly the expected record (identity, digest, chain, origin — a hostile resolve-without-change fails closed); a reducer rejection after a racing identical dispatch is idempotent success only when the snapshot now carries the exact expected record. Durability: a sink `not_committed` returns `state_persist_failed` with the previous snapshot authoritative and the published manifests as orphans (no automatic second dispatch); a `durability_unknown` adopts the visible candidate, poisons the sink, stops every further dispatch and returns `state_persist_failed` with the adopted state; a fresh retry reuses the published artifacts (idempotent adoption), recognizes the already durable prefix and dispatches only the still-missing records; nothing is ever rolled back. Capture boundary: every options field is read exactly once, the sink's `poisoned`/`dispatch`/`snapshot` accessors each exactly once in the synchronous prefix as opaque references (no field of the pipeline/candidate/state documents is read before the pipeline/candidate provenance gates — the acceptance chain is the first validation of pipeline/candidate/state contents after the fail-closed sink poison latch: pipeline provenance, candidate provenance/compile, and only then the state validation and the identity/boundary checks; unexpected sink-getter errors propagate unchanged), `dispatch` bound once before the first await; the per-call ops (`verifyCandidateForAcceptance`, `publishCandidate`) with the single frozen production ops object and no mutable module-global seam. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_controller.test.ts` (32 tests) covers the revision-1 happy path through the real publisher and real sink (manifest bytes, candidate-order task ledger, plan ledger last, loader round-trip after every durable step), the multi-stage deterministic order, the revision-2 plan skipping the durable user-response task revisions, compilation/acceptance failures with zero filesystem and dispatch effects, the reducer pre-check failure, task/plan publication failures with orphans and zero dispatch, the partial durable task prefix retry, `not_committed` on task/plan with fresh retries, `durability_unknown` on task/plan with the adopted state and the poisoned sink, the exact full retry with zero dispatch and preserved manifest identities, digest/chain/origin conflicts, the stale repeated-candidate conflict (a repeated plan r1 after a linked r2), the ahead/gap conflict, the task-downgrade conflict, a candidate at the durable latest task revision beside unrelated records, the hostile sink, the poisoned sink, the one-time option/accessor capture, the provenance/Proxy trap battery with zero snapshot/candidate traps before the gates, unexpected getter errors propagating unchanged, deep-freeze/caller isolation, canary-free results and diagnostics, both export-surface pins, and the source scan (single-layer reuse; no second validator/comparator/compiler/serializer; no message parsing; no coordinator/runner/CLI/Docker imports; no mutable seam). Production-reachable transitively through the planning-output composition (runner → handoff). Still unwired: wait/replanning policy, automatic resume, multi-process locking, API/T3.
- Run plan restoration after a restart (production-neutral, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_restore.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_run_plan_restore_internal.ts` (internal core) is the single official path that rebuilds the provenance-backed `CompiledPipelineV2RunPlan` of the existing chain after a process restart — the object `openPipelineV2ContinuedStage` demands and that otherwise exists in memory only immediately after `acceptPipelineV2RunPlanCandidate`. Public API is exactly `restorePipelineV2AcceptedRunPlan({pipeline, runRoot, state}) -> {compiled_plan, state}` (runtime export surface: `PipelineV2RunPlanRestoreError` + the function; types erased), the deep-frozen result carrying the exact compiled object (never a clone) and the normalized `validatePipelineV2RunState` snapshot. The reconstruction chain is entirely the existing one — `requireResolvedPipelineV2Provenance`, the single state validator, `pipelineV2RunPipelineIdentity` + `comparePipelineV2RunIdentity`, the read-only store loaders `loadPipelineV2PlanRevision`/`loadPipelineV2TaskRevision` (provenance-backed prepared manifests), then the existing `preparePipelineV2RunPlanCandidate` and `compilePipelineV2RunPlanCandidate`; no second parser, compiler, serializer, digest builder, registry or minter, and no direct provenance-registry access. The internal ops seam is exactly the two read-only loaders in one frozen production object (`productionRunPlanRestoreOps`); fault injection is per-call through the internal core, so an injected call cannot influence a parallel production call. Capture order before the first await: the options shape, `pipeline`/`runRoot`/`state`, the injected-ops record shape and both ops getters each exactly once, the run root as a non-empty string, then the pipeline provenance gate — before any field of the durable state or of the pipeline is read. Then: the single state validation, the exact durable pipeline identity (shared comparator), `basename(runRoot) === state.run_id` before any store load, at least one durable `plan_revision`, and the authoritative revision is strictly the LAST durable plan ledger record — the filesystem never selects it and orphan artifacts are ignored. Fixed strictly sequential read order (no `Promise.all`): current plan manifest → exact match against the ledger record (run id, revision, sha256, previous_sha256, origin_execution) → the immediate predecessor plan manifest read+verified only above revision 1 → per task pointer in the manifest's normalized semantic order: the exact durable task record (task_id + revision, exactly one, digest and chain binding), the current task manifest verified against pointer and record, the immediate predecessor task manifest read+verified only above revision 1 (revision 1 reads no predecessor) → the durable protected input named by `plan.root_task.input_id` (exactly one, `protected === true`, digest exact; no input artifact is read) → the existing candidate preparation and compilation. Only the current plan, its immediate predecessor, the pointered current task revisions and their immediate predecessors are ever loaded. The typed failure contract is `PipelineV2RunPlanRestoreError {reason, state}` over exactly `invalid_options | invalid_state | pipeline_mismatch | artifact_missing | artifact_mismatch` (malformed options or injected-ops record shapes are `invalid_options` at the capture boundary; malformed state/no accepted plan; identity or run-root binding; absent required exact plan/task/predecessor artifact; ledger/pointer/chain/root-task divergence; hostile malformed loader results are typed, never `TypeError`s), with store/manifest/binding/compiler errors passing through by class and identity, content-free diagnostics and unexpected errors propagating unchanged. Strictly read-only: no filesystem writes, no durable dispatch, no recovery or repair. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_restore.test.ts` (29 tests) covers both happy paths through real reducer state and the real acceptance controller (the exact ordered load log `plan:1, task:task-a:1` / `plan:2, plan:1, task:task-a:2, task:task-a:1`; the stage-new-task case reading `task-b` without a predecessor), orphan-artifact tolerance, the four-case missing matrix, the mismatch matrix (plan digest/previous/origin, predecessor plan digest, task digest/id, predecessor task digest, root-task input id/protection/digest, run-root and foreign-pipeline `pipeline_mismatch`), the malformed loader-result matrix through injected ops (null/primitive/array/wrapper/wrong-kind/projection → typed failures; loader errors by object identity), the malformed injected-ops matrix (null/undefined/primitives/array/missing/non-function loaders → typed `invalid_options` at the capture boundary, before the provenance gate, state validation, identity/run-root binding and any store load, without reading a hostile extra property), the one-time capture battery (Proxy options/ops, zero state reads and store calls for a Proxy pipeline through the existing gate, caller mutation after a pending load), the read-only fingerprint proof, the export surface (two facade keys, three internal runtime keys), the source scan (no writer/reducer/sink/controller/coordinator/runner/CLI imports, no second parser/serializer/digest builder/registry), and the integration bridge proof: the honest continued-stage prefix through the real facades (real run, real planning execution, real plan acceptance, real generation/iteration, real stage execution, real wait, real continue-stage intent acceptance), the simulated restart, the restoration of the accepted plan from the durable ledger plus the immutable manifests, and the exact four-command suffix `iteration_grant_recorded → stage_iteration_closed → wait_response_recorded → stage_iteration_opened` through the public `openPipelineV2ContinuedStage` consuming exactly the restored compiled plan (generation 1 / grant-closed iteration 1 / open iteration 2, exact cursor target and grant, loader round-trip). Production-reachable transitively through the planning-run-plan handoff (Branch B) and the revise-task intervention. Still unwired: automatic resume, retries, and migrations.

- Planning-output → accepted-run-plan composition controller (production-neutral, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_planning_run_plan_controller.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_planning_run_plan_controller_internal.ts` (internal core) is the single restart-aware layer that turns the settled-but-unbound planning acceptance boundary of an existing durable run into the accepted run plan in one fixed sequence: capture `{pipeline, runRoot, sink}` → the pipeline provenance gate (before any state read or filesystem access) → ONE authoritative `sink.snapshot` read → `restorePipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext` → the exact last execution by the restored `planning_execution_index` → `compiledExecutionRoleFor` (the exact planning role + safe `plan_output`) → `readAcceptedJsonOutput` over the restored accepted history and the exact execution index (the proposal value read once from the fixed orchestrator-owned output location) → `preparePipelineV2RunPlanProposal` (the existing proposal chain is the only validation; no re-hash, no re-parse) → `constructPipelineV2RunPlanCandidateFromProposal` (the anchored, retry-aware construction) → the existing public acceptance verifier (`verifyPipelineV2RunPlanCandidateForAcceptance`) only as the provenance/binding gate and the source of the expected compiled projection (never a second compiler) → `acceptPipelineV2RunPlanCandidate` (the existing crash-safe acceptance) → the defensive verification of the acceptance result and the exact downstream result returned by identity — no new envelope. Public API: the caller passes no state, no execution index, no state id, no output id, no proposal, no manifests, no candidate, no revision and no digest; everything is derived from the durable snapshot and the trusted pipeline. Runtime export surface exactly `PipelineV2PlanningRunPlanControllerError` (own reasons only `invalid_options | invalid_result`) + `acceptPipelineV2PlanningRunPlan`; all composed layers' typed errors and unexpected errors pass through unchanged by object identity. Production ops are exactly the eight existing public facades/resolvers in one frozen object (`productionPlanningRunPlanOps`: restore/compiled role/reader/proposal/construction/verifier/acceptance/compiled-stage selector); every member is read exactly once before the first await; no store/reducer/runtime internals directly; no fs, no second parser/compiler/digest/serializer/registry/minter. Defensive boundaries: the restored context's exact own-key shape with the planning index bound to the last durable execution, the cursor projection bound to the state's cursor and the run bound to the run root (never swapped); the compiled role bound to the execution's state id with `role === "planning"` and a safe `plan_output`; the reader result's exact five-key shape with the exact state/output/activation binding and a lowercase SHA-256 digest; the verifier's expectation verified (exact compiled shape) BEFORE the acceptance is ever called; the acceptance result's exact two-key shape with the compiled projection equal to the verifier's expectation, the compiled plan's provenance through the existing public stage selector, and `state === sink.snapshot` by identity after the success; malformed fake results are the layer's own `invalid_result` with the next facade never called and never a leaked `TypeError`; diagnostics are content-free. The controller's own snapshot reads are exactly one initial (before the restore) and one post-acceptance verification read; an acceptance failure performs no post-failure snapshot reads (the error passes by identity, never masked). Capture order: the options shape → `pipeline`/`runRoot`/`sink` each exactly once → the ops record shape + all eight members once → the non-empty run-root scalar → the structural sink check (no snapshot getter read) → the provenance gate; mutation after the first await cannot change the captured references; a cloned or Proxy pipeline is rejected by the gate with zero pipeline traps, zero state reads and zero facade calls. - Planning-run-plan handoff controller (restart-aware, production-neutral, implemented, **not directly wired but production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_planning_run_plan_handoff_controller.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_planning_run_plan_handoff_controller_internal.ts` (internal core) is the single restart-aware layer that moves an existing durable run from its planning boundary onto the caller-selected stage of the newly accepted plan and commits the planning transition — the full `revise_task` chain `accepted revise task → replanned closure → revise_task response → settled planning execution → accepted next plan revision → old generation closed by replanned → selected new-plan generation opened → iteration 1 opened → planning transition committed`, plus the INITIAL branches over the new initial stage transition controller (`openPipelineV2InitialStageTransition`, built on the shared transition-application kernel `applyStageTransitionCommit` that also serves the replanned transition controller): the initial chain is `accepted first plan revision → selected stage's generation 1 and iteration 1 opened → initial planning transition committed`. Initial Branch B (the exact committed initial boundary: the same planning execution with `executions.length === transitions.length`, the last transition binding exactly that execution and landing on the selected stage's entry, the cursor on its target, exactly the one open generation with its open iteration 1, no waits/grants) runs `restorePipelineV2AcceptedRunPlan` → verification → the stage resolution → the ensure's zero-dispatch W3 recognition → verification → `openPipelineV2InitialStageTransition` alone, accepting only the exact C1 zero-dispatch result; a replayed different budget on the committed boundary is the ensure's typed `PipelineV2StageIterationControllerError` by identity. the wait must be the last journal entry, answered `revise_task`, with its exact intent; arbitrary unchanged historical revise waits and generations are allowed and never participate in the target uniqueness; a later wait — even on the same anchor — is progressed state) → the exact caller-stage resolution (`compiledStageFor(compiledPlan, stageId)` before any durable stage write — separate from the provenance probe; Defensive verification: the acceptance result's exact two-key shape, the compiled plan's provenance through the public stage selector probe, the plan revision/digest/origin equal to the last durable plan record, the state identity `=== sink.snapshot`; the restore result verified against the durable plan ledger INCLUDING a structural comparison of its `state` with the initial authoritative snapshot (deep-equal accepted, no false identity requirement); the stage result's exact 13-key shape bound to the intent/wait anchor, the caller stage/budget/position/template (the position derived from the compiled plan's declaration order — the compiled stage projection carries no position field), the accepted plan, the previous generation (the generation carrying the target wait's replanned closure — the same generation every composed form closed or recognized) and the opened generation (whose own record is bound to the selected stage position/template, plan, budget and anchor); the transition result's exact 14-key shape bound to the committed transition record, the execution, and the open generation/iteration read from the authoritative state's durable journal — every flat `generation_index`/`iteration_index`/`previous_generation_index` is bound to the durable predecessor/new generation/open iteration, never merely type-checked; every result's `state` compared STRUCTURALLY (a local recursive own-key comparator, never `JSON.stringify`) against the authoritative post-call snapshot read exactly once per phase — the proven nuance honored: the production C1 result's state is deep-equal but not identity-bound to the sink snapshot, and no false identity requirement is imposed. Capture order: the options shape → `pipeline`/`runRoot`/`sink`/`stageId`/`initialBudget` each exactly once in contract order (hostile extras never read) → the ops record shape + all nine members once with function checks → the non-empty run root, the safe stage id and the positive safe budget → the provenance gate → the structural sink shape check (`invalid_options` for a non-record sink, a missing or non-function `dispatch`; only the three contract members ever read) → the single snapshot read (`invalid_state` for a missing or primitive snapshot; `invalid_state` for a poisoned sink) → the branch classification; downstream typed errors (planning acceptance, plan restore, intent loader/store, replanned stage, transition controller, compiled resolver) and unexpected errors pass through unchanged by object identity; diagnostics are content-free (no proposal/task/output bodies, no hostile field values, no digest canaries, no filesystem paths).
