The third corrective battery (tests 28–32) proves the planning-edge gate on the real production chain: an incompatible caller stage (existing in the accepted plan, its template entry differing from the completed-edge target) is refused with the controller's own `invalid_state` before the intent load and before ANY stage write — the acceptance suffix alone becomes durable (S0: delta +3, no `stage_generation_closed`/`stage_generation_opened`/`stage_iteration_opened`/`transition_committed`, the state loader-valid on the planning acceptance boundary, a compatible explicit-stage repeat continues the same run; S2: total zero dispatch with the revision and the full run-tree fingerprint unchanged and the error state the authoritative accepted snapshot by identity), a real zero-candidate plan leaves no wrong durable generation, both same-entry stages are still accepted on independent prefixes (no declaration-first policy), the thrown engine-resolver error propagates by identity with zero facade calls, the malformed edge-result matrix (15 shapes) calls no stage/transition facade, the ops Proxy pins exactly nine member reads in the capture contract order, and the source scan requires the `compiledTransitionFor` import. - Planning-run-plan handoff → coordinator resume composition controller (production-neutral, implemented, **wired into the production runner**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_planning_run_plan_resume_controller.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_planning_run_plan_resume_controller_internal.ts` (internal core) composes the completed planning-run-plan handoff followed by the coordinator's resume entrypoint in one fixed sequence: `applyPipelineV2PlanningRunPlanHandoff` → the defensive verification of the successful handoff → `resumePipelineV2Run`. Public API is exactly `resumePipelineV2RunAfterPlanningRunPlanHandoff({pipeline, runRoot, sink, runtime, control, stageId, initialBudget})` returning the exact verified `PipelineV2ResumeCoordinationResult` by object identity (no new envelope); the run id is never a caller field (derived exclusively from the verified authoritative state), and no proposal, compiled plan/stage, wait intent, plan digest/revision, generation/iteration/transition index or entry state is accepted from the caller. Runtime export surface is exactly `PipelineV2PlanningRunPlanResumeControllerError` (own reasons only `invalid_options | invalid_result`, last verified authoritative `state`) + `resumePipelineV2RunAfterPlanningRunPlanHandoff`; internal core: the error class, the frozen `productionPlanningRunPlanResumeOps` (exactly the two existing facades by identity) and `applyPipelineV2PlanningRunPlanResumeWithIo`; no reducer/state validator/store/fs/parser/serializer/digest/registry imports, no manual manifest/intent/proposal reconstruction, no second retry classifier and no mutable module-global seam. The controller owns no durable side effect of its own, never opens the sink and never loads the pipeline (the caller opens the sink and loads the pipeline from the durable `state.pipeline.bundle_root`; after a process crash the caller reopens and repeats the whole facade), and interprets no signal itself (acceptance/cutoff stay coordinator-owned through the captured control functions). Handoff-result verification: the post-handoff snapshot is read exactly once and verified as the completed handoff boundary FIRST — active/running with no terminal/publication/failure projection, cursor and both journals exactly at the committed planning transition with `executions.length === transitions.length` (no successor started), the last transition bound to the last settled planning execution (role `planning`, `cleanup_completed`, no `iteration_index`) and leading to the selected stage's compiled template entry state through the trusted `compiledStageTemplateFor`, the last open generation bound to the caller stage/budget and the last accepted plan record and anchored to the answered wait, the actual last replanned iteration closure — never a historical one — anchoring the last `revise_task` wait, whose single declared `revise_task` action's target is the planning execution's state; a snapshot that is not a record or not a valid boundary yields the layer's own `invalid_result` with error state `null` and the resume never starts. Then the result's exact 14-key shape, the flat field types, the structural `state` equality (a local recursive own-key comparator, never a serialization; identity not required) and every flat binding (caller policy, the last plan record, the opened generation and its open iteration, the answered wait, the committed transition and its execution) are verified with the verified snapshot as the error state — a hostile or malformed result is the layer's own `invalid_result`, never a leaked `TypeError`, and the resume is never called. - Continue-stage intervention → resume handoff controller (production-neutral, implemented, **wired into the production runner**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_continue_stage_resume_controller.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_continue_stage_resume_controller_internal.ts` (internal core) is the unwired composition of the full `continue_stage` handoff — the restart-aware intervention followed by the coordinator's resume entrypoint in one fixed sequence: `applyPipelineV2ContinueStageIntervention` → defensive verification of the successful handoff → `resumePipelineV2Run`. The verification is targeted, defensive and total for malformed values (own `invalid_result`, never a leaked `TypeError`): the intervention's flat result must bind exactly to the captured provenance-backed intent (run id, wait index, intent digest, additional iterations, request digest, the fixed `continue_stage` action id and its routing target), to the caller budget through the durable granted generation's `initial_budget`, and to the authoritative durable state — which the real intervention result carries as the exact sink snapshot object, proven by identity (a dedicated test drives the honest prefix, the ordinary restart and the real facade and pins `intervention.state === sink.snapshot`); the targeted completed-boundary bindings read only from that snapshot: status/phase, the cursor at the declared action target on the wait anchor, the transition/execution journals at the anchor, the answered target wait (the last and only record of its index with the exact accepted intent, declared request digest and exact response digest), the exact single grant of the granted pair, and the granted last open generation bound to the intent's stage, the expected plan digest and the caller budget with the exact grant closure and the open successor. Options are exactly `{pipeline, runRoot, sink, runtime, control, intent, initialBudget}` — `runId` is never a caller field (derived exclusively from the verified intent), `compiledPlan` is not a caller field (only the restore obtains it), and stage id/action/budget policy is never applied here. Capture and preflight, all before the first await and before any durable intervention: the options shape → the seven fields each read exactly once (fixed order, hostile extras never read) → the ops record shape and its two members each read exactly once with function checks → a non-empty absolute `runRoot` (`node:path` `isAbsolute`, the coordinator's own contract), a structural sink (a record with a `dispatch` function; the `snapshot`/`poisoned` getters stay owned by the composed layers and the coordinator), a record runtime/control, a prepared intent record and a positive safe-integer budget → the pipeline provenance gate and the intent provenance gate (the existing registry, strictly the `continue_stage_intent` kind) plus the intent's contract fields, with the provenance gate's own typed error propagating unchanged → the policy fixed as captured scalars → the runtime and control contract functions read and bound exactly once into stable deep-frozen adapters the coordinator alone consumes, so a mutation of the caller-owned runtime/control objects during the pending intervention cannot change the resume; the signal lifecycle stays coordinator-owned through the captured control functions and this layer interprets no signal itself. The intervention facade always runs first: any of its typed errors passes through by object identity and the resume is never started after one. The authoritative snapshot is read exactly once per verification phase (immediately after the intervention, and after the resume — the read-count test pins exactly one read per phase); the real intervention result's state object identity with the sink snapshot is the verification's binding anchor. The coordinator's returned union is verified defensively and accepts ONLY the exact runtime shapes of `PipelineV2ResumeCoordinationResult` — a success carries exactly the own enumerable keys `ok`,`state` with a state record exactly the sink snapshot; a refusal carries exactly `ok`,`refused`,`reason`,`state` with `refused === true` and a reason from the coordinator's refusal vocabulary; an ordinary failure carries exactly `ok`,`reason`,`state` with no own `refused` field and a reason from the canonical `PIPELINE_V2_FAILURE_REASONS` (imported from `pipeline_v2_state.ts` — the only runtime state-module import, no reducer/validator call); failure and refusal states are null or exactly the sink snapshot — and returned unchanged by object identity without a new envelope: refusal, worker failure, signal failure and persistence failure stay coordinator-owned classifications. Retry and crash windows (the honest boundaries only): C0 — the intervention has not started; the fresh call runs the exact five-command suffix `plan_intent_accepted → iteration_grant_recorded → stage_iteration_closed → wait_response_recorded → stage_iteration_opened` on the reopened run and then the resumed successor execution. C5/crash seam — the intervention is already fully durable while the resume has not started (for example after a resume-side crash); a fresh call's intervention recognizes the exact completed boundary with zero dispatch through the intervention facade's own progressed-retry reconciliation and runs the resume exactly once, with the durable grant/wait/generation/closure/open projection never rewritten between the boundary and the final durable state. A resume that already began durable execution has no new convergence guarantee here (the coordinator's existing continuation contract stays the only owner of that semantics); unrecognized progressed states and downstream lifecycle errors pass through by identity. the malformed resume results over every union branch — including the exact-shape battery: an ordinary reason in a refusal branch, a refusal-only reason in the ordinary branch, unknown reasons, extra fields on success/refusal/failure and a `refused: undefined` own field are all rejected), the foreign caller budget refused by the composed layers with zero resume calls, the mutation-after-await immunity of the captured policy/runtime/control with mutated replacements never called, the exact capture order/read counts (the seven options then the two ops members, the two runtime and two control functions, one snapshot read per verification phase) with hostile extras never read, invalid runtime/control before any facade call, pipeline/intent clone and Proxy provenance failures with zero facade calls, valid coordinator outcomes returned by `toBe` without reclassification, the table-driven exact-vocabulary proof (all nine refusal reasons and all `PIPELINE_V2_FAILURE_REASONS` values each with a null and with the authoritative state, plus the success union — every valid object returned by identity), both export surfaces, and the source scan (only the two facades plus provenance/scalar/state/types; a stale plan artifact as the restoration's typed `artifact_mismatch`), the hostile successful-result matrices (malformed restore/acceptance/completion results, healed deltas, forged and foreign compiled plans — every own `invalid_result` with zero downstream calls, never a `TypeError`), the hostile racing reconciliation, the acceptance-window racing barrier (a table-driven R0/R1/R2 battery over honestly built exact states proving the racing recovery never accepts an acceptance window — the completion canary never fires and the original sentinel is re-thrown by object identity), the genuine racing positive controls (an exact R3 progressed state runs the completion and records only the response; an exact R4 progressed state is the zero-dispatch completed retry; both converge to the same completed boundary), the provenance/capture battery (a spread-cloned pipeline and a Proxy pipeline with zero traps and zero effect; options read exactly once in the fixed contract order with hostile extras unread; ops members read exactly once; a missing ops member refusing before any sink read; caller mutation after a pending restore), the durability windows (all four not-committed faults and the task/response durability-unknown faults with fresh-retry suffix convergence to the same final revision, the adopted candidate and the poisoned sink pinned), publication pass-through by identity, identical concurrency convergence, the exact two-key public and three-key internal export surfaces with the frozen ops identities, the source scan (the five facades only; no reducer/validator/serializer/digest/fs/store/coordinator/runner/CLI imports; no message parsing; no mutable seam), and the invalid-options battery.
- Revise-task intervention → resume handoff controller (production-neutral, implemented, **wired into the production runner `revisePipelineV2Task`**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_revise_task_resume_controller.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_revise_task_resume_controller_internal.ts` (internal core) is the composition of the full `revise_task` handoff that the production runner invokes — the restart-aware revise-task intervention followed by the coordinator's resume entrypoint in one fixed sequence: `applyPipelineV2ReviseTaskIntervention` → defensive verification of the completed handoff → `resumePipelineV2Run`. Options are exactly `{pipeline, runRoot, sink, runtime, control, runId, waitIndex, taskId, taskBody}` — no intent, candidate, digest, stage, plan or budget field is accepted from the caller (every one is derived from the durable data by the intervention facade). The controller owns no durable side effect of its own: it dispatches nothing, publishes nothing, loads nothing, never opens the sink and never calls the reducer, validator, store, manifest loader, serializer or digest machinery, and interprets no signal (acceptance/cutoff stay coordinator-owned through the captured control functions); the caller's `taskBody` is a captured content input passed unchanged to the intervention, re-hashed nowhere here, and never enters the result, the durable state or any diagnostic. Capture and preflight, all before the first await: the options shape → the nine fields each read exactly once in the fixed order (hostile extras never read) → the ops record shape and its two members each read exactly once with function checks → the pipeline provenance gate (before any field of a durable state is read; its typed error propagates unchanged) → the scalar/structural validations (non-empty absolute `runRoot`, a structural sink with a `dispatch` function, record runtime/control, safe `runId`/`taskId`, positive safe-integer `waitIndex`, non-empty `taskBody`) → the runtime/control contract functions read and bound exactly once into stable deep-frozen adapters the coordinator alone consumes. The intervention facade always runs first: any of its typed errors passes through by object identity and the resume never starts after one; after a successful intervention the authoritative snapshot is read exactly once — the real result carries it as the exact sink snapshot object (identity, proven against the real facade on both the C0 and the R4 zero-dispatch paths), and a hostile or malformed success result — wrong flat fields, a structural-clone state, a hostile durable boundary — is the layer's own `invalid_result` with the last authoritative snapshot, never a leaked `TypeError`, with the resume never started. The targeted completed-boundary bindings read only from that snapshot: `run_id`, active/running, the target wait the last and only record of its index with the exact request/intent/`revise_task` response digests, the declared action routing exactly to `action_to`, the cursor on that target at the wait transition boundary with the journals on the same boundary, exactly one wait-bound task revision matching the flat result with the exact own twelve-key result shape enforced before any value check (its schema-owned `index` at its actual append-only ledger position, a revision above one, exactly one predecessor revision of the same task at `revision - 1` whose recorded digest the record's `previous_sha256` chains to, no later revision of the revised task; the predecessor need not be the adjacent global record), the last plan record matching the open generation's plan binding, the last open generation with the intervention's indexes and its last iteration closed by the exact `{by: "replanned", wait_index, closed_transition_count}` closure with no open iteration, and no new execution/transition/wait/plan/generation beyond the boundary. The coordinator's returned union is verified defensively, identically to the continue-stage sibling (success exactly `ok`,`state` with the state identical to the sink snapshot; refusal exactly `ok`,`refused`,`reason`,`state` with a reason from the coordinator's typed refusal vocabulary; ordinary failure exactly `ok`,`reason`,`state` with a reason from the canonical `PIPELINE_V2_FAILURE_REASONS`; the controlled waiting branch exactly `ok`,`waiting`,`state` and the controlled plan-ready branch exactly `ok`,`planReady`,`state`, each with the state identical to the sink snapshot, returned by identity; failure/refusal states null or exactly the sink snapshot) and returned unchanged by object identity without a new envelope; a thrown resume error passes through by object identity. Retry and crash windows (honest boundaries only): C0 — the fresh call runs the exact four-command intervention suffix on the reopened run and then the resumed planning execution; R4/crash seam — the intervention is already fully durable while the resume has not started; a fresh call's intervention recognizes the exact completed boundary with zero dispatch through its own progressed-retry reconciliation and runs the resume exactly once, the projection never rewritten; a resume that already began durable execution has no new convergence guarantee here; unrecognized progressed states and downstream lifecycle errors pass through by identity; there is no reopen inside this layer (after a process crash the caller opens a fresh sink and repeats the whole facade). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_revise_task_resume_controller.test.ts` (14 tests) cover the honest C0 through the public facade (the exact four-command suffix then the seven-command resume `start_agent_execution {architect, planning, no iterationIndex} → agent_failed → run_failed`, execution index 3, the ordinary `worker_failed`, one session pair cleaned tool-first exactly once, revision +11, the durable projection and the loader round-trip), the crash seam/R4 window (sentinel by identity before any resume effect, fresh call's zero-dispatch R4 recognition, only the seven resume commands recorded, the projection never rewritten), the intervention error identity with zero resume calls, the resume thrown-error identity, the malformed intervention-result matrix (mutated flat fields over the authoritative snapshot, bare-record and structural-clone identity violations) and the durable-binding near-miss matrix through a hostile sink snapshot (foreign run id, waiting status, cursor off the target/boundary, foreign `continue_stage`/response/intent/request digests, shifted/duplicate/new wait records, mismatching/later task revisions, closed generation, grant-closed iteration, open successor iteration, foreign generation plan binding, new plan revision, new execution/transition, malformed shapes — every case own `invalid_result` with zero resume calls), the malformed resume-union matrix over every branch plus the positive vocabulary table (every refusal reason and every `PIPELINE_V2_FAILURE_REASONS` value with a null and with the authoritative state, plus the success union — every valid object returned by identity), the corrective battery (a correct result carrying one extra own enumerable field rejected by the exact twelve-key shape; the wait-bound task revision's mutated `previous_sha256` or ledger `index` rejected by the ledger-position/predecessor-chain checks — every case the layer's own `invalid_result` with zero resume calls, never a `TypeError`, content-free diagnostics), the positive multi-task control (a ledger where the revised task's predecessor is not the adjacent global record is accepted and the verified coordinator result returned by identity), the capture/read-order battery (the nine options then the two ops members, exactly once each in order, hostile extras never read), the exactly-one-snapshot-read-per-verification-phase proof, the mutation-after-await immunity, the pipeline spread-clone/Proxy provenance failures with zero facade calls and zero traps, invalid runtime/control with zero facade calls, both export surfaces, and the two-file source scan (the single imports of the two composed facades; no reducer/validator/store/filesystem/manifest machinery of its own; no second parser/serializer/digest builder/registry; no message parsing; no mutable module-global seam). Wired: the runner entrypoint `revisePipelineV2Task` and the CLI `orchestrator revise-task` are exactly one call of this facade (the runner passes only the verified options), so this controller and every composed layer below are production-reachable. Still unwired: the default pipeline bundle, the revise-task selection policy (the runner passes the caller's taskId/taskBody), the architect/replanning branch beyond the intervention (the next plan revision, generation reopen, transition and successor start), automatic resume, schema/reducer changes, migrations/API/T3 and multi-process locking.
- Production planning-run-plan runner API (implemented, **wired into the CLI as `orchestrator resume-plan`**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runner.ts` adds `resumePipelineV2PlanningRunPlan(options: PipelineV2PlanningRunPlanOptions, deps: PipelineV2RunnerDeps)` with `PipelineV2PlanningRunPlanOptions {runId, stageId, initialBudget, configRoot, launcherId?}` — the external parameters are exactly the run id and the two explicit caller policy scalars `stageId` (safe id) and `initialBudget` (a positive safe integer with no default) plus the standard resume configuration; automatic stage/budget selection, the default-pipeline policy and the CLI wiring stay out of this entrypoint, and no automatic planning loop exists. The entrypoint uses the single existing private existing-run core `runExistingPipelineV2` — no second runner, no second preflight: the shared preflight order (capture/validate options and deps, one `RunCauseGate`, read-only state-root/run-root verification, read-only `PipelineV2RunStateSink.open`, the pipeline loaded only from `state.pipeline.bundle_root`, the profiles from the caller `configRoot`, one Launcher authority check, one runtime adapter, one composed controller call, the existing coordinator-result → `PipelineV2RunOutcome` mapping) is unchanged. `ExistingRunCallerPolicy` gained the fourth discriminant `planning_run_plan {runId, stageId, initialBudget}` (frozen at capture; the original caller objects are never read afterwards) with the capture order `runId → stageId → initialBudget → configRoot → launcherId`, each read exactly once. There is deliberately NO runner-level planning-boundary classifier and no post-open derivation: the initial/replanned Branch A/B classification, the latest-replanned-closure derivation, the selected-stage binding, the budget ownership and the crash retry belong entirely to the existing composition controller `resumePipelineV2RunAfterPlanningRunPlanHandoff`, whose single call is the entrypoint's `runCoordinator` body (`{pipeline, runRoot, sink, runtime, control, stageId: policy.stageId, initialBudget: policy.initialBudget}`) — no proposal, accepted output, compiled plan/stage, wait index, intent, plan revision/digest, generation/iteration/transition index or run root is ever passed through the runner; a typed downstream error (including a wrong caller stage/budget on the completed boundary) passes through by identity to the ordinary runner mapping (exit 1 without a reason field, the durable state untouched, no reclassification, no own retry logic). `resumePipelineV2`, `continuePipelineV2Stage` and `revisePipelineV2Task` keep their observable behavior, texts, capture orders, signal semantics and outcome mapping unchanged (their suites are unmodified beyond the two export-surface pins gaining the fifth key). commands then the seven resume commands, revision +14 —, the successor execution on the selected stage's entry with role `stage`/iteration 1, the ordinary `worker_failed`, one session pair cleaned once each tool-first, the loader round-trip and the no-in-memory-reuse proof via the prefix helper returning coordinates only), the C1 crash retry (the handoff durable through the public facade, reopen, Branch B/C1 zero dispatch, only the seven resume commands, the handoff projection byte-identical), the capture battery (the options Proxy reading the five fields exactly once in contract order with hostile extras unread, mutating `stageId`/`initialBudget`/`runId` getters keeping the validated policy and the first validated run only, a mutation delivered during the pending auth and a replacement `deps.now` never reaching the composed call), the invalid-options matrix (non-record, unsafe run/stage ids, zero/negative/fraction/overflow/undefined budget, relative/empty config root, invalid launcher id — every refusal the ordinary pre-run-root outcome with zero filesystem/auth/session/controller effects and the durable state byte-identical), the existing-run precedence battery (missing state document, an unsafe pre-existing run-root mode rejected unchanged, a broken bundle, a semantically valid but different bundle failing the durable identity inside the composed controller, a missing profile root, a failing authority — all through the shared boundaries with zero Sessions and zero handoff dispatch), the wrong-caller-policy battery on the completed boundary (a foreign stage id and a foreign budget each refused by the handoff's own typed verification, mapped without reclassification), the signal battery (SIGINT 130 / SIGTERM 143 at the authority boundary with the actual run id/root/authoritative planning-boundary state and zero durable writes), and the export/source pins (exactly five runtime keys; one existing-run core with the single new composed call; no direct planning-acceptance/stage/transition facade call and no second store/reducer/parser/serializer/digest/registry machinery). Still unwired: the automatic stage/budget selection policy, schema/reducer changes, migrations/API/T3 and multi-process locking. (The bundled default-v2 carries the trusted `plan_ready` policy and the plan-ready continuation is automatic — see the trusted automatic plan-ready continuation bullet; the manual `resume-plan` contract is unchanged.)

- Replanned-generation controller (production-neutral, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_replanned_generation_controller.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_replanned_generation_controller_internal.ts` (internal core) is the durable bridge that closes the previous stage generation after the next plan revision has been accepted — the full revise-cycle order is now `revise intent/task accepted → iteration closed by replanned → revise_task response → planning execution → next plan accepted → old generation closed by replanned`, from which a future increment opens the new plan's generation and iteration. The single durable command is `stage_generation_closed {generationIndex, by: "replanned"}`, built only from the durable records and dispatched through the structural `PipelineV2ReplannedGenerationControllerSink {snapshot, poisoned, dispatch}` seam (the production `PipelineV2RunStateSink` satisfies it without an adapter); the reducer stays the single successor authority. The controller performs no filesystem work: it never runs the architect, never builds or accepts a plan candidate, never publishes any manifest, never opens a new generation or iteration, never selects a stage or a budget, never commits a graph transition, never resumes the run, and is not wired into the coordinator, the runner, the CLI or the default pipeline. Capture and provenance ordering (fail-closed, tested): the options shape → `sink` → `intent` → `compiledPlan` each read exactly once → the sink's `poisoned`/`dispatch`/initial `snapshot` members each captured exactly once as opaque references with `dispatch` bound to the sink before the first await → the poison latch (`invalid_state`) → the existing manifest-registry provenance gate of the prepared intent (no intent field read before it, Proxy traps never invoked) with the strict `revise_task_intent` kind → the existing compiled-plan provenance gate, equally before any field of the compiled plan is read and with zero Proxy traps → the single `validatePipelineV2RunState` (a missing or invalid initial snapshot is a typed `invalid_state` with `state: null` and a fixed content-free diagnostic; unexpected causes propagate unchanged) → the hidden compiled-plan pipeline identity comparison through the single existing `comparePipelineV2RunIdentity` → the durable bindings → the reducer pre-check → the dispatch. Exact prerequisite boundary (the fixed check order, tested): active/running with no terminal/run outputs/failure → the target wait the last wait-journal record and the ONLY record of its index (a full defensive journal pass: every viewed entry a record, the target index exactly once, the last position) → the durable wait intent equal to the prepared intent's digest (`invalid_intent`) → the wait answered with exactly the `revise_task` response → the intent manifest bound to the durable state (the task ledger is the binding authority for task id, predecessor and new-task digests) → the accepted task revision read exclusively from the ledger (exactly one wait-bound record of ANY task, exact contract fields, a positive safe revision above 1, no later revision of the same task; absent → `invalid_state`, several/conflicting/later → `revision_conflict`) → the old generation the LAST durable generation bound to the previous plan digest without an `open_iteration`, with at least one iteration and the last iteration as the exact replanned closure of the target wait, the historical iteration prefix well-shaped → the new accepted plan exactly the last durable plan revision (`plan_revisions.length === compiledPlan.plan_revision`, revision above 1, the preceding plan record at revision −1 whose digest equals the new record's predecessor AND the old generation's plan digest — stale/foreign/non-successor → `plan_conflict`) → the compiled plan projection carrying the revised task exactly once with the exact accepted id/revision/digest — the task may live in any stage (absent/duplicated/stale → `plan_conflict`) → the declared `revise_task` action with the cursor on its declared target and the cursor transition count and the transition journal exactly at the wait boundary (an advance past it → `lifecycle_conflict`) → the settled planning execution (`executions.length === transitions.length + 1`, the last execution exactly `compiledPlan.origin_execution` at its journal position, role strictly `planning`, type strictly `agent`, phase strictly `cleanup_completed`, state id on the declared action target, and no committed transition, new wait or new execution beyond the boundary). Reconciliation is ONE internal classification point: C0 — the generation still open: the single closing command pre-checked through the single `reducePipelineV2RunCommand` on the local validated snapshot (a rejection is a typed `invalid_state` with zero dispatch; defense-in-depth — the verified boundary is strictly stronger than the reducer's preconditions), dispatched exactly once, and the authoritative snapshot re-read and fully verified; C1 — the exact durable replanned closure already on the last generation: an idempotent zero-dispatch success built from the already verified snapshot, with no snapshot re-read after the classification and no state restoration; a partially matching closure is never an idempotent success; C2 — the narrow crash-recovery retry form needed by the replanned-stage composition controller after a fault window inside the stage-iteration opening: the exact closed old generation as the direct predecessor of exactly one still-open generation of the CURRENT compiled plan (the new generation is strictly the LAST durable record, the old replanned generation strictly the second-to-last one at `lastPosition - 1`, an arbitrary unmodified historical prefix before the predecessor is admitted unchanged — the loader owns its coherence — and nothing sits between predecessor and new generation; `index === predecessor.index + 1`, the accepted plan digest, exactly one compiled stage of that stage id, the exact stage position and template, the wait-boundary anchor, a positive initial budget) in one of the two immediate opening forms (the bare generation with `iteration_count === 0`, or exactly iteration 1 open on the same anchor with an exact `open_iteration` projection) — also a zero-dispatch success whose result still describes the old closed generation and its replanned iteration; a generation of the current plan without the exact replanned predecessor closure, a wrong stage position/template/plan digest/anchor, a closed new generation, more than one new generation after the closed old one, a closed or second iteration, and any lifecycle advance past the wait boundary are `lifecycle_conflict` with zero dispatch, and C2 is never a general historical search. Post-dispatch verification is the same targeted comparison on the normal resolve path and the racing `PipelineV2StateError` path: the revision delta is exactly +1 on the strict closure shape and exactly +2/+3 on the racing C2 suffix (the bare new generation / its first iteration open, with the arbitrary historical prefix admitted unchanged); the run id and the durable pipeline identity unchanged; status, phase, cursor and the boundary journals unchanged; the wait journal, the task ledger and the plan ledger positionally unchanged; the generation journal length unchanged with historical generations and the historical iteration prefix unchanged; the target generation keeping its index and identity bindings and its target iteration with the exact replanned closure; the single new field the exact generation closure with `closed_transition_count === wait.transition_count`; the comparisons are defensive contract-owned helpers with `Array.isArray`/record guards — a malformed hostile snapshot yields a typed controller error, never a `TypeError`; nothing is compared by serialization and there is no recursive deep comparator. Durability: a `PipelineV2RunStateDurabilityError` adopts the exact closure candidate visible on disk, poisons the sink, stops every further dispatch and fails `state_persist_failed` with the adopted state (a fresh retry with a reopened sink recognizes the exact C1 closure with zero dispatch); a plain store error (`not_committed`) keeps the previous open-generation state authoritative and a fresh retry dispatches the closing command again; unexpected errors keep their identity; errors are never classified from message text. The closed reason set is `invalid_intent | invalid_state | revision_conflict | plan_conflict | lifecycle_conflict | state_persist_failed` with the last authoritative state (`null` until the initial snapshot validated); diagnostics are content-free. Runtime export surface is exactly `PipelineV2ReplannedGenerationControllerError` and `closePipelineV2ReplannedGeneration` (public) / `closePipelineV2ReplannedGenerationInternal` (internal); no planner, pre-check, comparator or test seam is exported and there is no mutable ops seam. The deep-frozen content-free result is `{wait_index, generation_index, iteration_index, intent_sha256, task_id, task_revision, task_sha256, previous_plan_revision, previous_plan_sha256, plan_revision, plan_sha256, origin_execution, state}` — no manifest, canonical JSON, paths, bodies, prepared intent, compiled plan or other caller-owned objects. unexpected getter and dispatch error identity, content-free diagnostics, both export surfaces, the prefix-C2 battery on an old generation with index > 1 built through the real reducer/controllers (a closed generation-1 prefix: C2-bare and C2-open zero-dispatch retries, the racing extended suffix, and a hostile extra generation), and the source scan (one validator called twice, the reducer only for the pre-check, no filesystem/store/publisher/serializer/digest-builder/coordinator/runner/CLI imports, no mutable seam and no new registry) — 46 tests. Production-reachable transitively through the replanned-stage composition controller (reached from the planning-run-plan handoff, the runner and the CLI). Still unwired: the action/intent selection policy, the architect worker output parsing, stage selection and the initial-budget policy, automatic resume, the default-pipeline bundle, migrations/API/T3, multi-process locking.
- Replanned-stage transition controller (production-neutral, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_replanned_stage_transition_controller.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_replanned_stage_transition_controller_internal.ts` (internal core) commits the single planning transition that follows a successful replanned-stage composition — the full revise-cycle order ends `... selected new-plan generation opened → iteration 1 opened → transition_committed {from: declared revise target, outcome: "completed", to: the selected stage's entry state, transition_index: the declared edge's index, execution_index: the settled planning execution}`. Runtime export surface is exactly `PipelineV2ReplannedStageTransitionControllerError` (closed reasons `invalid_options | invalid_state | lifecycle_conflict | state_persist_failed`, last authoritative `state`) and `openPipelineV2ReplannedStageTransition({pipeline, sink, intent, compiledPlan, stageId, initialBudget})`; the internal core exports exactly the error and `openPipelineV2ReplannedStageTransitionInternal`. Input carries `initialBudget` because the transition binding must prove the preserved generation under the caller-selected budget, and two stages may share one entry state so the step alone never proves the stage binding. Capture order: options shape → the six fields each read exactly once → the sink's `poisoned`/`dispatch`/`snapshot` members each once with `dispatch` bound to the sink before the first await → poison latch → intent provenance gate → compiled stage through the single trusted `compiledPipelineV2RunPlanStageFor` (position from the declaration order; resolver errors pass by identity) → positive safe `initialBudget` → the single `validatePipelineV2RunState` → the hidden originating-identity comparison (`compiledRunPlanOriginIdentity` + `comparePipelineV2RunIdentity`) → the durable bindings. C0 bindings before any dispatch: active/running with no terminal/publication/failure; the target wait exactly once keeping the exact accepted intent and answered exactly with `revise_task`; the last durable plan revision equal to the compiled plan (revision/digest/origin) with a predecessor record; the OLD generation (the predecessor of the last) closed exactly `by: "replanned"` with `closed_transition_count === wait.transition_count`, bound to the PREDECESSOR plan digest, and its LAST ITERATION bound to the wait by the exact `replanned` closure (`wait_index`/`closed_transition_count` exact — the generation's `closed.by` alone is never sufficient); the NEW generation (strictly the last) open with the exact stage id/position/template/current-plan digest and the caller-selected budget, opening on the wait anchor with exactly iteration 1 and the exact `open_iteration` projection; the last execution the settled planning execution (`cleanup_completed`, at the declared target, `index === compiledPlan.origin_execution`). The PREMATURE call (before the composition — the old iteration already closed `by: "replanned"` while the old GENERATION is still open and no next generation of the accepted plan exists) is typed `invalid_state` before any dispatch and is never described as a stage execution inside the old iteration. The step is derived only from the durable cursor (C0) or the exact durable transition (C1), the last planning execution and `compiledTransitionFor(pipeline, from, "completed")` (the single engine-owned edge resolver; `unknown_outcome`/`missing_state` pass by identity); `to` must equal `compiledStage.entry_state` (`lifecycle_conflict` otherwise). The single command is pre-checked through the one reducer before the dispatch (rejection → `invalid_state`, zero dispatch), then dispatched exactly once. C1 recognizes only the exact durable transition by all five fields with the cursor at `to` and no started next execution (a changed field is `lifecycle_conflict`, never an idempotent success); anything beyond the boundary is `lifecycle_conflict`. Post-dispatch verification: revision exactly +1 with the run identity pinned, the exact new transition record and moved cursor; every other durable field unchanged except the routine `updated_at` refresh — a dispatch resolving without this exact change is `invalid_state`, never a success; a reducer rejection because a racing dispatch has already recorded a different transition for the same execution stays `lifecycle_conflict`. Durability: `not_committed` keeps the previous snapshot authoritative (a fresh retry re-dispatches); `durability_unknown` adopts the visible candidate, poisons the sink and a fresh reopened sink recognizes the durable transition with zero dispatch; nothing is ever rolled back. Diagnostics are content-free; unexpected causes propagate by identity. What remains policy: the moment of the call, the caller-selected `stageId`/`initialBudget`, and the next stage execution. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_replanned_stage_transition_controller.test.ts` (17 tests) cover the premature refusal with zero dispatch and a byte-identical snapshot, the exact C0 command/result/loader round-trip, the foreign stage with a shared entry state and the foreign budget refused before dispatch, the C1 exact retry, a changed transition field, a resolve-without-change dispatch, both durability windows with fresh-retry continuations, the provenance battery (hand-built intent, cloned compiled plan, Proxy pipeline with zero traps and pass-through typed errors), and the export/source scans; the post-dispatch full-verification battery adds a changed historical agent execution `profile`, a changed `schema_version`, a changed schema-owned input field (the protected input digest), a changed historical grant on a real grant fixture, and hostile historical decision executions/results (an injected `iteration_index`, changed result scalar fields and both id lists) — every `invalid_state`. Runtime export surface is exactly `PipelineV2StageIterationControllerError` (closed reasons `invalid_options | invalid_state | lifecycle_conflict | state_persist_failed`, immutable `reason`, last authoritative `state` — `null` when none exists), `ensurePipelineV2StageIteration({compiledPlan, stageId, initialBudget, sink})` and `closePipelineV2StageIteration({compiledPlan, stageId, iterationCloseReason, generationCloseReason?, sink})` over the structural `PipelineV2StageIterationControllerSink {snapshot, poisoned, dispatch}` seam (the production `PipelineV2RunStateSink` satisfies it); the deep-frozen results are `{compiled_stage, generation_index, iteration_index, state}` and `{compiled_stage, generation_index, iteration_index, generation_closed, state}` where `compiled_stage` is the exact frozen object of the existing compiled projection (never a copy). Trusted binding order: every options field and the sink's `poisoned`/`dispatch` members are read exactly once as opaque references (the authoritative `snapshot` is read once at capture and is then re-read after every dispatch and on its typed failures — never memoized, so the per-dispatch verification and the durability mapping always take the sink's authoritative state) → the fail-closed poisoned latch (`invalid_state` before anything) → `compiledPipelineV2RunPlanStageFor` as the single compiled-plan provenance gate and stage lookup (its own errors keep their classes) → only then `validatePipelineV2RunState` as the single state validator → the hidden originating-identity comparison (`compiledRunPlanOriginIdentity` + the single `comparePipelineV2RunIdentity`: any mismatch of the five durable identity fields is `lifecycle_conflict`, so a compiled plan compiled from a foreign pipeline is never accepted even when run id, plan revision and plan digest coincide) → the durable bindings (`state.run_id === compiledPlan.run_id`, and the last durable plan revision exactly `compiledPlan.plan_revision`/`plan_sha256` — a foreign run, a stale/non-current plan or a digest/revision mismatch is `lifecycle_conflict`) → the run boundary check (active/running, no terminal/run outputs/failure, no open wait, no in-flight and no failed last execution — a failed execution is never a clean lifecycle boundary, the only successor of a failure is the run failure finalization — `invalid_state`) → the reconciliation. Every durable value is derived, never accepted from the caller: the stage position from the compiled plan's declaration order, the transition anchor from `state.cursor.transition_count`, the generation index from `generations.length + 1`, the iteration index from `generation.iteration_count + 1`. Reconciliation table: no open generation → the sequence `stage_generation_opened` then `stage_iteration_opened` (iteration 1 of the new generation); an open generation matching stage id/position/template/plan digest/initial budget exactly with an open iteration → W3 idempotent success with zero dispatch; the same match without an open iteration → `stage_iteration_opened` of the next iteration index (W2 after the generation-only crash window; the next policy-authorized iteration after the previous one was closed); any field difference → `lifecycle_conflict` with zero dispatch (a foreign generation is never accepted, closed or rewritten). Durability: sink `not_committed`/`durability_unknown` are `state_persist_failed` with the last authoritative (adopted) state; a fresh retry recognizes the already durable prefix and dispatches only the still-missing steps. The same module adds the active-boundary closure API `closePipelineV2StageIteration({compiledPlan, stageId, iterationCloseReason, generationCloseReason?, sink})`: the caller has already decided why the iteration closes (`normal_close` | `exhausted`) and whether the generation closes (`next_stage` | `final_stage`); the wait-bound reasons (`grant`/`replanned`), the generation `replanned` reason, wait indexes and caller-supplied indexes, anchors, positions, templates, digests or targets are not part of the API. Both public APIs share one capture/validation path (single capture → poison latch → compiled-plan gate → state validator → hidden identity → durable bindings → stage position) and one pre-check/dispatch machinery inside the module — no second controller, comparator, replay or registry. The closure boundary is the contract hook order `settled stage execution → stage_iteration_closed → optional stage_generation_closed → transition_committed`: active/running with no terminal/outputs/failure/open wait, exactly one settled-but-unbound execution (`executions.length === transitions.length + 1`) whose durable `execution_role` is exactly `stage` (never inferred from the profile, the executor name or the state id) in the agent phase `cleanup_completed` or the decision phase `evaluated` exactly, whose `state_id` is one of the compiled stage's state ids, and whose recorded `iteration_index` resolves — through the single existing exact resolver `pipelineV2StageIterationAt` at the execution's start boundary (derived only from the durable data: the global execution index k starts at committed transition count k − 1) — to exactly one projection matching the generation and iteration this call would close (`generation_index`, `iteration_index`, `stage_id`, `template_id`); zero and ambiguous resolutions (a reused template with the same iteration index across touching generations) are both `lifecycle_conflict` with zero dispatch — no first/last/current resolution is ever selected, and the membership resolver is deliberately not used here because a closure mutates a specific generation and demands a unique exact binding; the last durable generation must match the compiled stage's id, declaration position, template and current plan digest. Reconciliation: an open iteration → `stage_iteration_closed` (no wait index) plus the optional `stage_generation_closed`; the iteration already closed at this boundary → an exact match of the durable close reason and anchor (plus the generation's state for a requested closure) is an idempotent zero-dispatch success, a requested-but-open generation is completed by the single generation close dispatch, and any other close reason or anchor, or a generation closed while the call requested only the iteration closure, is a `lifecycle_conflict` with zero dispatch — no candidate heuristic, no historical generation guess. - Pipeline v2 run-input snapshots + activation data layout (pure host-side substrate, used by the production runner through the coordinator, no Session/container launch itself): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runtime.ts` is the v2 data plane. Run layout: `<runRoot>/project` is the shared project directory of the whole run — the coordinator prepares the run-owned copy from the caller's source directory via `prepareRunProject(projectSourcePath, runRoot)`, and every activation mounts exactly this one directory at `/workspace` read-write, so changes persist across activations; there is no per-activation project directory. `prepareRunProject(projectSourcePath, runRoot) -> PreparedRunProjectWithProvenance` (deep-frozen `{run_root, project_root, manifest, binding}`; the source path is deliberately not part of the object): the caller hands over only the source directory path — the source is never modified, renamed, cleared or read beyond this copy, its path never enters the pipeline, execution document, worker env/argv, durable state or results, and the internal run-root layout is not presented to the user. Since the project snapshot provenance increment (state schema v10) the preparation also produces the run's immutable provenance manifest: the single copy traversal records the capture of exactly the objects and bytes it read and copied, the manifest is published through the immutable-document substrate BEFORE the staged workspace is renamed into `<runRoot>/project`, and the published workspace is verified against the manifest's workspace baseline before the preparation returns — see the project snapshot provenance bullet. `runRoot` must be an existing absolute canonical real non-symlink directory (`realpath(runRoot) === runRoot`; never created or removed by the function); `<runRoot>/project` must be absent as an object of any kind (a repeated call fails closed and never overwrites); the source must be an absolute real non-symlink directory whose canonical path is fixed before copying, and source/runRoot must not overlap in either direction. The copy is staged in an exclusive hidden staging directory inside the canonical run root — staging creation fixes the created directory's `dev`/`ino` identity immediately after the exclusive `mkdir` and only then runs the fallible post-creation checks, so every failure after identity fixation cleans the own staging tree while a failed primary identity fixation never removes blindly (the staging may remain and the original sanitized error surfaces) — and published with one `rename` after a fresh absence re-check — supporting real directories (0700), regular files (0600, or 0700 when the source carries any execute bit), symlinks copied as symlinks through `readlink` (target text verbatim, never resolved, permissions not normalized), hidden entries including `.git` and empty directories, in deterministic relative-path code-unit sorted order; uid/gid, timestamps, xattrs, ACLs and hardlink identity are not preserved (hardlinks become independent regular files — the documented current file-copy contract); regular files are read `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, destinations created `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW`, every block is fully written by an internal write-all loop (a partial write advances the buffer offset and the file position; a zero-progress, negative or impossible `bytesWritten` fails the operation after exactly one attempt — no loop), streamed without shell/`cp`/`tar` and fsynced before publication; FIFOs, sockets, devices and unknown kinds are rejected, as is an object that changed kind or inode between scan and open; every expected filesystem failure (source lstat/realpath, readdir, readlink, source open/read/close, destination open/write/fsync/close, staging create/chmod/inspect, target absence checks, final rename) is a `PipelineV2RuntimeError("run_input_invalid", <sanitized diagnostic>)` — close errors never replace an already failing copy and a close error after an otherwise successful copy is the typed failure itself; diagnostics never contain the absolute source path, absolute paths of its descendants, file contents or raw system error messages — source entries are named only by their `JSON.stringify`-encoded relative path plus a safe operation class, errno codes are rendered separately (`(errno EACCES)`), and only the limited system-code form `[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,31}` is accepted as an errno suffix (anything else — paths, spaces, newlines, lowercase, oversized or punctuated codes — renders no suffix, and reading `cause.code` is getter-safe so a throwing getter can never replace the original failure or leak its text). The copy core with its fault-injection types lives in the explicitly internal module `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_project_copy_internal.ts`: the production wrapper `prepareRunProject` always passes the single fixed immutable `realProjectCopyIo` to the per-call core `prepareProjectCopy(io, projectSourcePath, runRoot)`, tests inject their own IO object per call, and there is no mutable module-global IO and no installer — a fault-injected test call cannot influence a parallel production call (shared generic filesystem checks live in `orchestrator/src/fs_checks.ts`). Any failure before the rename removes exactly the created staging tree, and only after an ownership proof succeeds: the recorded `dev`/`ino` must still identify a real non-symlink directory canonically resolving to the expected path inside the canonical run root — a vanished tree is left alone, a substituted object is never removed, and a cleanup failure never replaces the original operation failure (a leftover staging tree stays the documented absence of crash recovery); existing objects and the source stay byte-identical, and every expected failure reason stays `run_input_invalid` — the project source is a run-level input; no new failure reason and no state schema change. Honest boundaries (unchanged): the portable `rename()` can replace a concurrently created empty directory at the target; no protection against a trusted host process mutating the source during the read; no crash recovery; after the rename the copy is authoritative and is never removed by this function even on later run failures. `snapshotRunInputs(pipeline, bindings, runRoot)` accepts the trusted `ResolvedPipelineV2` (provenance-checked via `requireResolvedPipelineV2Provenance` — no second compiler), the full binding list `{id, path}`, and an absolute orchestrator-owned `runRoot` that must already be a real directory (symlinked final component rejected; symlinked ancestors canonicalize through `realpath`) whose `project/` subdirectory exists as a real non-symlink directory. Every declared pipeline input is bound exactly once (missing/duplicate/unknown rejected before any mutation), each binding path must be absolute, its real object kind must match the declared `file`/`directory`/`json` type (`json` = regular file with valid JSON; the value must additionally conform to the loader-compiled Draft 2020-12 schema when the input declares one — the immutable schema snapshot already carried by the resolved pipeline is used, schema paths are never re-read), a symlink binding is rejected, and a binding resolving inside the canonical run root is rejected. Every input is copied into the orchestrator-owned snapshot `<runRoot>/data/inputs/<input-id>`: file/json byte-for-byte, directories recursively in deterministic relative-path code-unit order with only real directories and regular files allowed (symlink/FIFO/socket/device entries rejected fail-closed). Each input digest is a deterministic SHA-256 over the declared type, relative paths and content only (8-byte big-endian length framing, fixed domain tag `pipeline-v2-input\0`; directory entries contribute their kind tag `directory\0`/`file\0`, their length-framed UTF-8 relative path, and the length-framed content for files — names, entry kinds, and empty directories all change the digest; `sha256` hex) — never host paths, inodes, permissions or timestamps. File/json snapshots are written to a temporary name inside `data/inputs` and published atomically via `link()` (EEXIST-safe); directory snapshots are built in a temporary directory and published with one `rename()` (never replaces a file/symlink/non-empty directory). On failure the operation removes exactly the objects it created (tracked, best-effort, containment-guarded), so no partial snapshot becomes authoritative and existing snapshots (any pre-existing target object) are never overwritten; on success the returned deep-frozen metadata (`RunInputsSnapshot`: canonical `run_root`, `inputs_root`, canonical shared `project_root`, ordered per-input `{id, type, protected, snapshot_path, digest}`) is the run's only data source and user source paths are never read again. Honest boundaries: no protection against a trusted host process mutating a source during the read; a crash (SIGKILL) mid-copy leaves leftovers that make a retry fail closed; files are fsynced but no crash-durability claim is made. Snapshot provenance: the frozen snapshot object is registered in a module-private `WeakMap` together with the exact trusted pipeline object and the canonical run/project roots, only after the full snapshot succeeded; `prepareActivationData` accepts only that exact object (identity check against the same `ResolvedPipelineV2` object it was handed) and rejects hand-built objects, casts, spread/`structuredClone` clones, Proxies (getters never invoked) and snapshots of another pipeline with a stable `PipelineError` before any field is read — there is no second structural re-validation pass, and a future resume loader could mint trusted runtime state through the same registration path. `prepareActivationData(pipeline, runInputs, acceptedOutputs, stateId, activationIndex)` prepares a fresh `<runRoot>/activations/<index>-<stateId>/data/` tree with `inputs/` and `outputs/` only — there is **no per-activation project directory**; the shared `<runRoot>/project` directory is mounted at `/workspace` RW by every activation and is never created, cleared, copied or removed by the runtime. `inputs/` is copied per declared port strictly in declaration order (pipeline inputs only from the run-owned snapshot; state outputs only from the explicitly passed runner-owned accepted records `{state, output, activation_index, digest}` — no user paths and no types are accepted: the type is derived from the declared output port and the object must exist at the fixed orchestrator-derived path `<runRoot>/activations/<index>-<state>/data/outputs/<output>` with real non-symlink parents, matching real kind, and canonical containment; before anything is prepared the run-input snapshot is re-verified in full at its fixed orchestrator-owned paths (`verifyRunInputsSnapshot`: every entry a real non-symlink object of its declared kind resolving exactly to its recorded snapshot path inside the canonical run root, directory snapshots containing only real directories and regular files, every digest recomputed with the `pipeline-v2-input` framing and compared — a modified, relocated or escaped snapshot fails before the accepted history is parsed and long before the activation leaf is created; the original user binding paths are never read again), then the whole accepted history is validated — every record, including old and non-winning ones, is resolved to its fixed location and its digest is recomputed with the exact output-domain framing and compared, and only after the whole history checks out is the winning record per `state`/`output` pair selected by highest activation index independent of list order; so an accepted output that changed after acceptance — content, entry name, empty directory, kind or JSON bytes — fails the next activation before its leaf is created; missing/forward/first-visit self references fail before anything downstream; duplicate records and one activation index spanning two states are rejected; for every recorded `{activation_index, state}` pair the record set must be exactly the declared output ports of that agent state, so a partially constructed runner history is rejected as incoherent before any location is resolved; records with an index not below the current activation index are rejected; digests must be lowercase SHA-256 hex), `outputs/` (an empty 0700 directory pre-created for `directory` outputs; `file`/`json` output paths deliberately absent until a worker run). The activation index is globally unique within the run: before the leaf is created, any existing `activations/` entry with the same `<index>-` prefix (any state id, any object kind, never followed, unmodified) rejects the request. The returned immutable layout contains exact mount descriptors — shared project `/workspace` RW, inputs `/pipeline/inputs` RO, outputs `/pipeline/outputs` RW — and may carry canonical host paths (orchestrator-internal; they never enter the compiled pipeline, prompt, envelope or durable state), plus no bearer/credentials/env/helper endpoint/Docker options/argv. Since the runtime-adapter increment every successful preparation also writes the deterministic orchestrator-owned **execution document** at `<inputsRoot>/.orchestrator/execution.md` (container path fixed at `/pipeline/inputs/.orchestrator/execution.md`; `.orchestrator` created exclusively as a real non-symlink directory 0700, the file `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` mode 0600; any pre-placed object of either path is a typed `activation_prepare_failed` and existing external objects are never modified). The document is the only carrier of the pipeline prompt body between orchestrator and worker and is fully deterministic from the trusted state + activation index: state id, global execution/activation index, the verbatim prompt content, the `/workspace` note that project sources are the working product and not part of the pipeline output-port namespace, input/output ports in declaration order (id, type, fixed container path, read-only for inputs) with the canonical JSON schema value for json ports, and the rules to create only declared top-level outputs and to finish only after all declared outputs are written; it never contains host paths, profile env, credentials, bearer, helper endpoint, model config or input file contents. `PreparedActivationData` gains only the `execution_document {host_path, container_path}` pair — never the prompt body — and `prepareActivationExecutionDocument` is exported for deterministic trap testing (pre-placed directories/symlinks/files are rejected, external sentinels untouched); the failure cleanup still removes exactly the created activation leaf. The prepared layout is registered in a module-private `WeakMap` (pipeline + canonical run root) after success; only that exact object can later be passed to `acceptActivationOutputs` for the same pipeline object. Accepted records are a trusted runner-owned input (agent envelopes, stdout and worker files can never create records; the production link to durable `result_accepted` is not implemented); nothing accepts an agent-declared "output is ready". Directories 0700, files 0600; worker-UID compatibility of the output tree is an explicit limitation for the next runtime increment (no 0777 workaround). `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_runtime.test.ts` covers the exact binding set, type mismatches for all three types, json run-input schema violations rejected before any mutation, snapshot immutability after source change/deletion, digest determinism and framing, declaration-order processing, symlink/FIFO/socket rejection, pipeline-input activation, state-output handoff through acceptance-minted records, highest-index-wins under record permutation, missing/future/current and first-visit self outputs, global activation index uniqueness, accepted-record shape/declaration/index/fixed-location validation, arbitrary contained paths never satisfying records, symlink traps + sentinel preservation, forged snapshot batteries (clone, forged paths/digest/protected, duplicate entries, other-pipeline snapshot, getter/Proxy never invoked, rejection before filesystem mutation), accepted-history digest verification (readable-then-unreadable outputs), the shared project root across activations, frozen results, failure cleanup, v1 regression, and the unchanged production v2 rejection before auth/session; the run-owned project copy battery lives in `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_project.test.ts` (27 tests: the full tree, ownership/isolation in both directions, overlap, source-kind rejections, pre-existing project kinds, FIFO/socket with no staging leftovers, verbatim symlinks, mid-copy failure cleanup, repeated call, contract modes, deterministic traversal, frozen metadata, run-root validation, content-free diagnostics, run-root ownership, one block written through several partial writes with a byte-exact copy and zero cleanup calls on success, zero-progress/NaN/impossible write counts failing after exactly one attempt without looping, cleanup `rm`/`realpath` faults never replacing the original failure, vanished/replaced/relocated staging trees never removed with sentinel preservation, an unreadable source file as a sanitized `(errno EACCES)` failure, a fault-injected internal core blocked on a barrier cannot affect a parallel public `prepareRunProject` call on the same run root, a chmod failure after the staging identity is captured removing the own staging tree, a failed primary staging identity fixation never removing blindly with sentinels untouched, a failing staging cleanup during creation never replacing the original failure, the errno suffix accepting only the closed `[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,31}` form with getter-safe `code` reads, and the export-surface proof that the runtime module has no mutable IO seam while the internal real IO is frozen).
- Durable project snapshot provenance (state schema v10, implemented, wired into the production runner): every fresh run now owns ONE immutable provenance manifest describing both the source observation and the workspace baseline, produced by the single owner of the project copy during the same traversal that copies it — no second source scan ever exists. Module: `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_project_provenance.ts` (pure serializer/parser/binding owner) + `pipeline_v2_project_provenance_store.ts` (public store facade over the shared immutable-document substrate) + `pipeline_v2_project_provenance_store_internal.ts` (internal core: publication, read-only loads, the initial workspace baseline verifier). The manifest artifact lives at the fixed run-root location `<runRoot>/project-provenance.json` — outside the worker-mounted `<runRoot>/project` tree (the worker mounts the run root read-only, so the artifact is readable but never writable by a worker) — as one immutable canonical JSON file mode 0600 (exact canonical bytes, no trailing newline; exclusive temp + file fsync + exclusive `link()` publication, never a replace-capable rename; byte-exact adoption on an exact retry; typed conflict on anything else; `durability_unknown` with a frozen candidate after the link). Envelope: `{schema_version: 1, kind: "pipeline_v2_project_provenance", copy_policy_version: 1, entries[]}` with exact own keys at every level. Copy policy version 1 (unchanged copy contract, now durably recorded): real directories 0700, regular files 0600 (0700 when the source carries any execute bit), symlinks verbatim (target text never resolved), uid/gid/timestamps/xattrs/ACLs not preserved, hardlinks split into independent regular files — the split is no longer silent: every regular-file entry records `multiple_links` plus a canonical per-manifest `hardlink_group` (1-based, assigned in deterministic sorted-entry order over the shared copy-time `dev`/`ino` identity, which is stripped from the manifest and never serialized — no host inode numbers ever enter the document); a future Apply increment must resolve `multiple_links` explicitly instead of silently destroying the link. Entries are in deterministic code-unit order of their relative paths; paths obey the safe relative-path grammar (non-empty, not absolute, no NUL, no empty/`.`/`..` component, spelling preserved); entries are unique with coherent parent/child structure (every proper ancestor a directory entry). Each entry records the source observation AND the workspace baseline: directories `{path, kind, source_mode, workspace_mode}`; regular files `{path, kind, source_mode, workspace_mode, byte_count, sha256, multiple_links[, hardlink_group]}` — `sha256` is the plain SHA-256 of the exact copied bytes; symlinks `{path, kind, target, targets_identical: true}` — the verbatim target plus the copy-time confirmation that the created workspace symlink's target text equals the source target byte for byte. No absolute source/project/run paths, uid/gid, timestamps, xattrs, host inodes, file contents, diagnostics or credentials ever enter the manifest. Digest: SHA-256 over the dedicated closed domain `pipeline-v2-project-provenance\0` followed by the manifest's canonical JSON — the digest binds the whole document including order, modes, kinds, targets and file digests. The state binding is the minimal immutable triple `{digest, entry_count, copy_policy_version}` stored in the revision-1 `create_run` snapshot (see the state schema v10 bullet) — a run cannot exist without it. Copy-time consistency (honest contract): regular files are read with a copy-time coherence proof — the opened object must still be the scanned one (kind, `dev`/`ino`, permission mode, hardlink count, size, content timestamps) and is re-statted after the read (the same checks) with the read byte count required to equal the file size, so a source object written or touched while it is being read fails the whole preparation closed (best-effort at millisecond timestamp granularity, not a guarantee against an adversarial same-millisecond same-size rewrite; a coherent copy is still never a whole-tree atomic snapshot without project-level cooperation); the manifest describes exactly the objects and bytes the copy used, so a source change after the copy is visible as a future comparison conflict, never silently absorbed. Publication order (crash-safe by construction): the manifest is published BEFORE the staged workspace is renamed into `<runRoot>/project`, so the window "project published, manifest invisible" is unreachable; the mirrored window (manifest durable, workspace absent, nothing bound — no state document yet) fails closed on any retry: a fresh preparation refuses when a manifest already exists (the source is never re-read to rebuild provenance, and a changed source can never rebind the run id to another baseline), and the leftover staging tree stays the documented absence of crash recovery. The preparation verifies the published workspace against the manifest's workspace baseline (entry set, kinds, workspace modes, byte counts, content digests, verbatim symlink targets) BEFORE it returns, so no `create_run` — and therefore no worker activation — can ever exist for a run whose published workspace diverges from its provenance baseline; a `durability_unknown` manifest publication fails the preparation closed without binding. The restore verifier (`restorePipelineV2RuntimeContext` chain, wired into `orchestrator resume` and every existing-run flow) refuses any run whose stored manifest is missing (`artifact_missing`), non-canonical, digest-mismatching, symlinked, wrong-mode or foreign to its durable binding (`artifact_modified`), and — only while `executions.length === 0` (before the first worker activation) — additionally verifies the initial workspace against the baseline; after a first activation the workspace is worker-mutable by contract and the manifest stays provenance, never a workspace invariant. After the terminal the manifest remains byte-identical (worker add/modify/delete/mode/type changes never touch it); run success does NOT mean project delivery — the operator project stays read-only, no Apply, no RW authority and no manual diff exists in this increment; the read-only change-set projection over the manifest is implemented (next bullet). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_project_provenance.test.ts` (15 tests — envelope, ordering determinism, digest domain/framing, empty project, digest sensitivity per field class, mode normalization recording, hardlink groups, safe-path grammar, invalid-entry/value-free batteries, coherence, canonical-parse, binding validation/matching, content scans, freeze/isolation) and `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_project_provenance_store.test.ts` (18 tests — publication/layout/modes/canonical bytes, exact-retry adoption, conflicts, read-only loads, binding-verified loads, tamper refusal, run-root ownership, layout refusals, the C4 durability-unknown window with the visible candidate, the workspace-baseline verifier battery (matching tree, empty baseline, modified content/byte count/mode, type substitutions, changed symlink target, missing entry, symlinked root, determinism), the source scans (one digest-domain owner, one publication substrate, no second source scan, manifest-before-rename order, no public projection in inspect/events/HTTP/routing/client/UI), plus the provenance batteries in `pipeline_v2_project.test.ts` (tests 28–40: mode/byte recording, hardlink pair split + canonical group, symlink verbatim + identical-targets confirmation, deterministic order + workspace verification, empty project, the audit indistinguishability broken by the manifest, source-mutation-during-read and mode-change-during-read fail-closed batteries, the C5 corrupted-write gate, the C1 publication-failure retry, the C2/C3 rebind prevention with a mutated source, and the C6 worker-changes battery) and in `pipeline_v2_resume_context.test.ts` (PP1–PP5: missing/tampered/foreign/symlinked/wrong-mode manifest refusals with zero writes, the pre-activation workspace mismatch, and the worker-mutable workspace after the first activation).
- Versioned read-only Project Changes API (implemented, wired into the CLI, the control-plane routing/client, the loopback HTTP service and the Browser UI v6 panel): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_project_changes.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_project_changes_internal.ts` (internal core) is the single production owner of the **run workspace changes relative to the durable project baseline** for one finalized pipeline v2 run. The projection compares the OBSERVED current content of the final run workspace (`<runRoot>/project`) against the durable schema-v10 provenance and the observed current operator project — it deliberately does NOT claim the final workspace is a sealed worker-authored artifact (it is not sealed by an immutable artifact and remains technically reachable by a trusted host operator), and `source_relation` is an observation, never an Apply authorization (there is no `safe_to_apply` field; a future Apply must re-verify the project immediately before writing). The whole path is read-only: no run mutation lock, no Docker Helper/Launcher/Session access, no temporary file, no write/chmod/repair on any path, and a repeated read over unchanged trees is byte-identical. Reuse is total — the shared read-only durable-run load chain `loadDurablePipelineV2Run` (the single loader also serving `inspect`/`events`/`logs`), the shared finalized-status predicate (`isPipelineV2FinalizedRunStatus` added to the state module as the single status-vocabulary owner; running/plan-ready/waiting/resumable and every terminal recovery window before the final status refuse `changes_not_ready` with zero writes; failed terminal runs are supported equally with succeeded), the single provenance parser/digest/binding owner through `loadPipelineV2ProjectProvenance` + `requireProvenanceBindingMatch` (missing → `artifact_missing`; non-canonical/symlinked/wrong-mode/conflicting → `artifact_modified`, matching the restore verifier's mapping) and the provenance scanner's read-only tree-observation IO seam `ProjectWorkspaceVerifyIo` (no second filesystem abstraction; the copy traversal keeps its single-pass provenance contract untouched). The internal core scans both trees (`<runRoot>/project` and the CLI/registry-bound canonical operator project root) with one read-only scan: root identity fixation, leaf symlinks never dereferenced, regular files read through bounded chunks with kind/mode/identity/size/content-timestamp checks before and after each read, every directory's entry set re-read after walking (replacement during the scan is a typed unstable-tree failure — `workspace_unstable` for the run workspace, `project_unstable` for the operator project), FIFOs/sockets/devices typed `unsupported_project_entry`, deterministic code-unit entry order, and a missing/substituted/non-canonical operator root typed `project_invalid`. Change paths are the union of the provenance workspace-baseline paths and the observed final workspace paths (unchanged entries never appear; renames are never inferred — exactly one `deleted` plus one `added`); the closed change vocabulary is `added | deleted | content_modified | mode_modified | content_and_mode_modified | symlink_target_modified | type_changed` (regular files: digest/byte_count → content, final-workspace-mode vs workspace-baseline-mode → mode, both → combined; directories: mode only, changed descendants alone never change the directory; symlinks: verbatim target only, never dereferenced; kind differences: `type_changed`).
