The mode mapping is pinned: the copy normalization (source 0644 → workspace 0600, source 0755 → workspace 0700, directory 0750 → 0700) is never a change, a `content_modified` result keeps the original source mode, a mode-touched result projects the exact final workspace mode, an added entry's result is the final workspace node and a deleted entry's result is `null`. Baseline nodes carry the provenance source observation verbatim (including `multiple_links`/`hardlink_group`); result nodes carry the observed final workspace shape with the workspace's own canonical hardlink topology (1-based groups in sorted-path order — host dev/ino never serialize) and a hardlinked result's `matches_result` relation requires the operator topology to share exactly the result group's member paths. Every entry carries a closed `source_relation` (`matches_baseline` — the operator object corresponds to the source observation; `matches_result` — it already corresponds to the projected result, including absence for `deleted`; `diverged` — anything else, including an incompatible ancestor) computed against the full observed operator tree. Each full comparison carries an ephemeral **observation identity**: SHA-256 over the new closed domain `pipeline-v2-project-changes-observation\0` binding the API schema version, the run id, the durable provenance binding, the terminal revision, the FULL observed final workspace (kinds/modes/byte counts/digests/targets/hardlink groups), the FULL observed current operator project (the chosen semantics — pinned by a test: even an unrelated operator change on an unchanged path moves the identity), and the full ordered change set with its relations; it is never written to state, never a durable revision and never permission to Apply. Pagination: `after` counts already viewed entries (default 0; `after > 0` requires the exact previous observation, a mismatch is `stale_observation` with no partial page; `after > total` is `invalid_cursor`; `after == total` is an empty page with `nextAfter: null`; `nextAfter = after + page.length` only when a tail exists), default limit 100, maximum 200; the summary counts (`total`, the seven change kinds, the three relations) cover the whole comparison, not the page. CLI: `orchestrator project-changes --run-id SAFE_ID --config-root ABS --project ABS [--after N] [--limit N] [--observation SHA256] [--json]` — `--project` is the trusted operator surface (clean absolute path validated; the caller never passes a run root or provenance path); strict parser (both flag forms, duplicates/unknowns/positionals exit 2 with empty stdout; every registry/binding/auth/helper/state-root/internal flag rejected); success prints exactly one JSON document `{ok, exitCode: 0, schemaVersion: 1, runId, runRoot, revision, observation, summary, changes, nextAfter}` (runRoot only in the trusted CLI document — the control-plane client drops it), failures the established `{ok: false, exitCode: 1, runId, runRoot: string|null, reason}` envelope over the closed 14-reason vocabulary (`run_not_found | invalid_options | run_state_invalid | pipeline_mismatch | artifact_missing | artifact_modified | changes_not_ready | project_invalid | project_unstable | workspace_unstable | unsupported_project_entry | invalid_cursor | stale_observation | internal_error`), classified by typed causes only, never by message text, with no path/target/payload/credential in any failure document. Control-plane: the closed `OrchestratorCommand` kind `project_changes {runId, projectPath, after?, limit?, observation?}` routes to the exact CLI argv with the project path taken EXCLUSIVELY from the bound frozen registry project (the HTTP caller can never pass projectId/projectPath/configRoot/pipelineRoot/any artifact path); the subprocess client parses the exact own-key shapes (`schemaVersion === 1`, exit-code agreement, frozen projection, `runRoot` dropped, no stderr classification). HTTP: `GET /v1/runs/:runId/changes[?after=N&limit=N&observation=HEX]` flows `runId → durable binding → frozen registry project → subprocess client`; the strict query parser (no percent-encoding, bare keys, duplicates, empty values, sign, fraction, exponent or overflow; limit maximum 200); `invalid_cursor` maps to the request-layer 400 envelope and `stale_observation` to the request-layer 409 (both new members of the closed request-failure vocabulary and the one status table), every other correctly received changes failure — including `changes_not_ready` and the provenance-integrity refusals — follows the established failed command result at HTTP 200, transport failures stay 502, `binding_not_found` is 404 and a removed project stays `project_unavailable` (409); the success body is exactly `{schema_version: 1, kind: "project_changes", run_id, revision, observation, summary, changes, next_after}` (snake_case, no `run_root`, no host paths); Host/Origin/GET-only/no-body gates unchanged. Browser UI boundary: the read-only Project changes panel (Browser UI v6) is the only consumer of `kind: "project_changes"` — `ui/app.js` owns one dedicated changes parser/transport/viewer (`parseProjectChangesDocument`/`createProjectChangesTransport`/`createProjectChangesViewer`), the general JSON client keeps rejecting the changes kind for every other endpoint, and `tests/ui_model.test.ts` pins the fail-closed boundary; the panel is described in its own bullet below. Concurrency: a held run mutation lock never blocks a read (the read takes no lock and creates no project lock), two parallel readers agree byte-identically, a final workspace change or an operator change between pages is `stale_observation`, mid-scan instability is typed, and the whole run tree plus the operator project stay byte-identical across every read. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_project_changes.test.ts` (49 tests — eligibility and typed refusals with zero writes, the full classification battery including the four mandated mode-mapping cases and the no-false-positive normalization pin, hardlink metadata on both sides, failed terminal runs, provenance missing/tampered/wrong-mode/symlinked/binding-diverged refusals, pagination/observation batteries, scan stability through the injected seam, the CLI parser battery, the control-plane routing battery, the real CLI subprocess wire with the dropped run root, the UI fail-closed pins, the ownership source scans, the no-lock/no-write batteries), the CLI dispatcher battery (`tests/cli_main.test.ts`: exact single dispatch and options mapping, defaults, human/JSON reporting, resolver failure exit 2), the CLI fixture CliIo pins in `tests/pipeline_default_v2.test.ts` and `tests/pipeline_v2_runner_continue.test.ts`, the shared status-vocabulary export-surface pin in `tests/pipeline_v2_state.test.ts`, and the HTTP battery in `tests/control_plane_http_server.test.ts` (route parsing, the exact query grammar matrix, the 400/409/200/404/409/502 mapping, GET-only/no-body, the loopback gates and the leakage scan). Still unwired: Apply, any write to the operator project, RW project mounts, project-level locking, automatic application, a UI content/diff viewer, final-workspace durable sealing, and any state schema change (the bounded raw-byte content layer itself is implemented — see the next bullet).
- Output acceptance for one prepared activation (pure host-side, used by the production runner through the coordinator): `acceptActivationOutputs(pipeline, activation)` in `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runtime.ts` validates the completed `outputs/` tree of one prepared activation and returns one deep-frozen trusted record per declared output in declaration order — exact fields `{state, output, activation_index, digest}`, no path/type/schema/summary/timestamps/content. The argument must be the exact `PreparedActivationData` object a successful `prepareActivationData` call returned for the same `ResolvedPipelineV2` object (registered after success in a module-private `WeakMap` together with the pipeline and canonical run root; hand-built objects, casts, spread/`structuredClone` clones, Proxies with traps never invoked, and activations of another pipeline are rejected before any field is read and before any filesystem access). Acceptance creates, fixes, renames and deletes nothing — it only reads the finished tree. The outputs root is re-examined fresh (the worker held it read-write): it must be a real non-symlink directory resolving exactly to itself inside the canonical run root, its top-level entries must be exactly the declared output ports of the state (a missing declared output fails, any undeclared top-level entry fails, no duplicate/alias/fallback semantics; names, paths and types come only from the compiled pipeline and the prepared layout — the agent never reports them; `reject_undeclared_outputs: true` is a real enforced invariant since this increment), and each output must be a real non-symlink object of its declared type whose canonical path stays inside the outputs root. `file` outputs are existing regular files with non-symlink final components read through `O_NOFOLLOW`; `json` outputs must additionally be valid JSON conforming to the declared, loader-compiled Draft 2020-12 schema (no coercion, no defaults, nothing removed, the parsed value is never modified); `directory` outputs are real non-symlink directories whose recursion allows only real directories and regular files (symlink/FIFO/socket/device entries fail). All parents and canonical paths stay inside the canonical run root and the exact activation outputs location. Each record carries a lowercase SHA-256 digest over the separate domain `pipeline-v2-output\0` plus the declared type, with the same unambiguous framing as input digests (length-framed bytes for file/json; kind tag, length-framed UTF-8 relative path, length-framed file content in code-unit sorted order for directories — names, entry kinds and empty directories participate; host paths, inodes, permissions and timestamps never do). Accepted records remain a trusted runner-owned input; a changed accepted output (bytes, name, empty directory, kind, JSON bytes) is detected by digest recomputation before the next activation leaf is created. Diagnostics never contain file contents or JSON values. JSON Schema mechanism: `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_schema.ts` wraps the pinned Ajv Draft 2020-12 dialect (`ajv` 8.17.1, `orchestrator/package.json` + `orchestrator/bun.lock`; installed into the image by `Dockerfile_orchestrator` via `bun install --frozen-lockfile --production`). Schemas are compiled only at trusted `loadPipelineV2` time, before the resolved snapshot is registered (compile failure rejects the load); a fresh Ajv instance per compile keeps schemas hermetic; internal `$ref`/`$defs` are supported, remote/missing refs and unknown format keywords fail compilation, `strictRequired` is off (a required-only object schema is valid Draft 2020-12), no network, no remote loading, no executable callbacks, no coercion/defaults/removal. The dialect is synchronous-only: a schema that compiles to an async validator (`$async`) is rejected inside compile before registration (a `$async` subschema behind an internal `$ref` fails Ajv compilation outright), `validatePipelineJson` re-checks `$async` defensively before calling, and success is only the literal boolean `true` — `false` is an ordinary validation failure and a Promise/thenable result can never pass as success, so no unhandled rejection can be created. Validation errors are rendered value-free as `<instance path>: <message>`. Runtime `JSON.parse` diagnostics for user data values (json run inputs, json activation outputs) are normalized to the stable content-free form `<what> <path> is not valid JSON` — no parser message, offending token, line/column, or input fragment ever enters the message (schema-file parse errors in the trusted bundle keep their compile/load diagnostic). The same mechanism validates JSON run inputs in `snapshotRunInputs` and JSON agent outputs at acceptance; uncompiled schemas are rejected fail-closed. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_accept.test.ts` covers the acceptance battery (successful file/json/directory acceptance in exact declaration order with exact record fields, missing/undeclared outputs of every kind, symlink final components/substituted roots/broken parents, nested symlink/FIFO/socket inside directory outputs, invalid JSON, schema violations with value-free diagnostics, `$defs`/internal `$ref`, no coercion/defaults/mutation, digest determinism across locations/timestamps and digest change under bytes/name/empty-directory/kind changes, tamper detection for winning and old non-winning outputs, record permutation invariance, forged/cloned/proxied prepared activations, compile failures at load (including `$async` schemas), json run-input validation, the sync-only validator contract (Promise-like results never pass as success, rejected async validators are never registered), v1/production-v2 regression). Default bundle, v1 loader/runner, production `agent-smoke`, durable state, decision evaluator, profiles, lifecycle/signals, helper transport, Dockerfiles/CI are untouched by acceptance; Session creation, mounts and worker launch for v2, terminal state execution, and production wiring are later increments.
- Run-level output publication at the terminal (pure host-side, used by the production runner through the coordinator, no Session/container launch itself): `collectRunOutputs(pipeline, runInputs, acceptedOutputs)` in `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runtime.ts` materializes the declared run-level outputs after the graph runner has reached a terminal — deciding whether a terminal may be entered is the graph runner's job, and the function takes no terminal id, no user paths, no types, no mounts and no Docker options (arity 3: trusted pipeline, trusted run-input snapshot, runner-owned accepted history). Provenance gates: the pipeline must be the exact `loadPipelineV2` snapshot and the snapshot must be the exact `RunInputsSnapshot` a successful `snapshotRunInputs` returned for that same pipeline object (hand-built/cloned/proxied objects rejected before any field is read; no second structural compiler). The run root and shared project must still be real directories, the fixed `<runRoot>/outputs` path must be absent (any pre-existing file/directory/symlink fails closed before anything is created, so a repeated call after a successful publication is rejected and never overwrites), and the run-input snapshot is re-verified in full before anything is resolved or staged. The whole accepted history is then validated (shape, declared state/output, per-activation coherence, fixed-location resolution, digest recomputation of every record including old non-winning ones) before any winner is selected; there is no current-activation bound here — activation existence is proven by resolving each record's fixed location. Each declared run output is processed strictly in declaration order: `pipeline_input` sources resolve only from the verified run-input snapshot (a pipeline input counts as existing once its snapshot succeeded, and its digest is re-verified), `state_output` sources only from the runner-owned winner map (highest activation index wins). A present source is published regardless of `required`; a missing `required: true` source fails the whole operation before publication; a missing `required: false` source is listed as `{present: false}` with no filesystem entry — optionality never masks a damaged accepted history. `json` sources are re-parsed and re-validated against the same loader-compiled Draft 2020-12 schema carried by the resolved run output (content-free `<what> <path> is not valid JSON` diagnostics; the original JSON bytes are published, never a reserialization). Publication algorithm: the tree is staged in an exclusive temporary sibling directory inside the canonical run root (directories 0700, files 0600; only real directories and regular files — symlink/FIFO/socket/device rejected; no shell, no `cp`, no `tar`), every staged byte is hashed with the separate `pipeline-v2-run-output\0` domain (same unambiguous framing as input/output digests; host paths, inodes, permissions and timestamps never participate), the target is defensively re-checked absent, and one `rename()` publishes the finished staging tree atomically; a failure before the rename removes exactly the staging tree — run inputs, activations, accepted outputs and the shared project are never modified. The returned deep-frozen `RunOutputsSnapshot` (`run_root`, `outputs_root`, one discriminated entry per declared run output in declaration order; `snapshot_path` is the fixed orchestrator-owned path and the digest is taken over the actually published copy) is registered in a module-private provenance registry only after the atomic publish succeeded (no serializable markers; a future download/API layer can consume only registered snapshots). Honest limitations (unchanged): a trusted host process may mutate a source during the read; an adversarially created empty directory at the rename target could in principle be replaced; crash recovery and a directory `rename` no-replace primitive remain absent. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_outputs.test.ts` covers pipeline-input publication of all three types with byte-exact original bytes/modes, declaration-order processing (error names the first declared missing output, not the alphabetically first), highest-index winners with record permutation invariance, one source feeding several outputs, required-missing failure before publication, optional-absent snapshot entries without filesystem entries, whole-history verification before winner selection (including damaged old non-winning and optional outputs), incomplete per-activation record sets rejected before both the next activation and publication, modified/relocated/symlinked/FIFO/socket run-input snapshots rejected before activation leaves and staging, original user binding paths never re-read, pre-existing `outputs` objects of every kind, later-output errors never publishing earlier staged outputs, staging cleanup leaving project/inputs/activations intact, repeated publication rejected without overwrite, json re-validation with canary-verified content-free diagnostics, digest domain separation plus location/timestamp independence and bytes/name/empty-directory/kind sensitivity, deep-freeze with exact per-branch entry fields, empty-outputs publication, and v1/production-v2 regression. Production runner wiring, terminal state execution, durable state v3/resume, API/T3 download, decision evaluator integration, Sessions/mounts/worker launch, retries, quotas, and old-activation cleanup are not part of this increment; the production loader still rejects schema v2 before Launcher auth/session.
- Pipeline v2 deterministic `decision` state + pure evaluator adapter (compile-time substrate, used by the production runner through the coordinator): `pipeline_v2.ts` accepts a third state type `type: decision` with exact fields `id`/`type`/`model`/`inputs`/`transitions` (`profile`/`prompt`/`outputs`/`timeout_seconds`/`max_attempts`/`image`/`env`/`mounts`/`command`/`credentials`/`paths` are unknown fields); a decision state runs no container and no Session and declares no filesystem outputs. `inputs` is exactly one data port whose source must derive to type `json` (the user never re-declares the type); the port keeps the same data-port contract, so it also receives the immutable JSON schema snapshot from its source. During `loadPipelineV2` the `model` field (a clean bundle-relative `.yaml` path, extension checked at parse time with the state id in the message) is loaded through the single `loadDecisionModelResolved` chain in `decision.ts`: the declared path is validated, canonicalized, read, and compiled exactly once, the returned pair `{modelPath, model}` is atomic for the caller (`modelPath` is the canonical path the compiled model was actually read from — no symlink-retarget window between resolution and compilation), and the same `modelPath` keys the per-load cache, so states referencing one canonical model file (e.g. via an internal symlink) share one compiled snapshot — no global mutable cache, no second decision compiler/evaluator. Realpath containment is unchanged (internal symlinks allowed; symlink escapes, traversal, absolute paths, `~`, directories and missing files rejected, all with the state id in the message); the path-validation errors are `PipelineError` and model-content errors are wrapped as `PipelineError` carrying the state id. `loadDecisionModel` remains available unchanged as the root-canonicalizing wrapper over the same chain; the model is stored deep-frozen on the resolved state and never re-read afterwards. Reserved outcomes `uncovered`/`inconsistent_facts`/`invalid_facts` (`DECISION_RESERVED_OUTCOMES`) may never be declared as model decision ids; a decision state's transitions must be exhaustive and exact — exactly one per model decision id plus exactly one per reserved outcome, no extras, no duplicates, document order preserved, several outcomes may share a target — so every routable situation is declared explicitly (no fallback, no hidden terminal failure). The shared graph-shape layer (`checkGraphShape`) now treats agent and decision states as transition-bearing (terminals have none) and includes decision states in reachability/cycle checks; the v1 representation and the production engine are unchanged, and production `agent-smoke` never executes v2 decision states (the engine executes them only through the unwired `executePipelineV2Graph` adapter). The pure adapter `evaluatePipelineDecisionState(pipeline, stateId, facts)` (provenance-gated, decision states only) maps an already-parsed fact assignment through the existing `evaluateDecision` to a deep-frozen `PipelineDecisionStateResult` union (`selected` → `outcome` equals the decision id; `uncovered`; `inconsistent_facts`; `invalid_facts`). Only the shared evaluator's typed fact-validation error (`DecisionFactValidationError`, a `DecisionModelError` subclass distinguishable from model/compilation errors) maps to `invalid_facts`; any other unexpected error propagates fail-closed. The `invalid_facts` branch is structured and canary-free: `reason` is the stable code `not_mapping`/`unknown_fact`/`missing_fact`/`non_boolean_fact`, plus exactly where the code allows it the model-declared `fact_id` (`missing_fact`, `non_boolean_fact`) and the value's safe type name `actual_type` (`non_boolean_fact` only) — it never embeds a fact value or body, and for an unknown fact not even the provided property name (nothing is logged either); the evaluator's own error message is canary-free for the same reasons. The adapter selects no target state (targets come only from the transition table) and contains no timestamps, randomness, stdout, LLM, callbacks, expressions or user code. Filesystem fact resolution through the run-input snapshot/accepted history is now implemented as the pure host-side data adapter `evaluateDecisionStateFromData` (see the decision-state data adapter bullet below); production execution, `intervention`/pause-resume, P01, and engine-exhaustion-as-outcome policy remain later increments. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_decision.test.ts` covers compile shape (json derivation, transition order), pipeline-input/state-output sources, non-json rejection, zero/multiple inputs, unknown and forbidden fields, model containment (valid, internal symlink + shared compiled model, escape, traversal, absolute, home, wrong extension, missing, directory), modelPath↔read-model correspondence through a symlink, state-id error context, reserved-id collision, missing decision/reserved transitions, extra outcomes, graph reachability/cycles through decision states, decision states rejected as data-port sources, the adapter battery (exact result unions, invalid_facts for missing/extra/non-boolean/non-mapping facts with stable structured reasons and canary-free results/messages/diagnostics including an unknown-property-name canary, deep-freeze, repeated-evaluation identity, model-file/facts mutation immunity, provenance/unknown/non-decision-state rejection, agent-only activation layouts), arbitrary non-legacy state/decision ids, and full 2048-assignment oracle equivalence for the default `decisions/architect.yaml` through the adapter (1952 inconsistent / 82 selected / 14 uncovered, exact decision distribution, uncovered never falls back).

- Verified exact-activation JSON output reader (pure host-side, read-only, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runtime.ts` exports `VerifiedAcceptedJsonOutput` and `readAcceptedJsonOutput(pipeline, runRootCanonical, acceptedOutputs, stateId, outputId, activationIndex)` — the generic bridge from a verified accepted JSON output to a future consumer; the runtime owns no consumer semantics (the caller selects state/output/index from its own trusted metadata, e.g. a planning role's compiled `plan_output`). Fail-closed order: provenance gate before any pipeline field; safe ids + positive safe index; trusted state/output/type/schema resolution (the requested port must be a declared JSON output of the requested agent state with its loader-compiled schema — refused before any filesystem work, a hostile `acceptedOutputs` Proxy is never read); then the canonical run-root contract (`requireCanonicalRunRoot`, also before the history is read); the whole accepted history through the single existing `resolveAcceptedHistory` chain bounded below `activationIndex + 1` (current/future records rejected; old non-winning records verified like every other record); the exact winning record for the pair must carry exactly the requested index; then the deep-frozen five-key result `{state, output, activation_index, digest, value}` — no host path, schema, raw bytes or second digest. The chain itself gained one physical read per record during JSON revalidation: the digest phase captures every json record's raw bytes into a per-call cache on the single `readRegularFileBytes`/`O_NOFOLLOW` read (existing `readPortValueForDigest` with a new capture parameter), ALL records' digests are compared first (the established digest-before-schema priority: a digest mismatch anywhere still fails before any schema failure), and only then does the existing `verifyAcceptedOutputJsonSchemas` phase parse and validate the cached bytes through the existing `validatePipelineJson` — it never reopens a file (the shared `parseJsonRecordBytes` helper is the only parse path). Established consumers with `jsonSchemaRevalidation=false` keep behavior, reasons, winner selection and read counts unchanged; `verifyRestoredAcceptedHistory` keeps its public signature and semantics but no longer reads JSON records twice. No new parser, compiler, digest domain, serializer, registry, error class or failure reason; diagnostics stay content-free. The planning/proposal bridge is proved only by a test: the honest prepared-activation/acceptance battery takes `role.plan_output` from `compiledExecutionRoleFor`, reads the accepted `architect.plan` output, and hands `value` to `preparePipelineV2RunPlanProposal` (test-only caller); the runtime module still imports no proposal/plan-candidate/coordinator/runner/state module and carries no `plan_output` knowledge. Production-reachable transitively through the planning-output composition, which is the construction hook's owner (it reads the compiled planning `plan_output`, prepares the proposal, constructs and accepts the candidate). Still unwired: migrations/API/T3.
- Pipeline v2 decision-state data adapter (pure host-side, read-only, used by the production runner through the coordinator): `evaluateDecisionStateFromData(pipeline, runInputs, acceptedOutputs, stateId, nextActivationIndex): Promise<PipelineDecisionStateResult>` in `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runtime.ts` resolves a declared v2 `decision` state's single `json` input through the existing data plane and evaluates the compiled model via the existing `evaluatePipelineDecisionState` — creating nothing (no decision activation leaf, no `data/inputs`, no `data/outputs`, no Session, no container, no helper call, no env, no credentials), modifying and deleting nothing, and consuming no activation index (`nextActivationIndex` is only the strict upper bound every accepted record's index must stay below; the whole argument is a positive safe integer checked before the state id). Strict order: pipeline provenance → run-input snapshot provenance for the same pipeline object (both before any field is read; clones/casts/spreads/Proxies rejected with getter traps never invoked) → `nextActivationIndex` validation → safe state id + declared + `type: "decision"` → real run root and shared project root → full `verifyRunInputsSnapshot` of **every** run input (not only the decision's own; a modified input that does not feed the decision still fails first, and the original user binding paths are never read again) → the complete accepted history through the one shared `resolveAcceptedHistory` chain now used by `prepareActivationData`, `collectRunOutputs` and the adapter (exact record parse, per-activation coherence, fixed-location resolution of every record including old non-winning ones, digest recomputation, then highest-index winner selection — every record's index strictly below `nextActivationIndex`) → resolve the single input port by its declared source (`pipeline_input` only from the verified snapshot with the user binding path never re-read; `state_output` only from the verified winner map — missing/forward/first-visit references, kind mismatch, symlink or digest mismatch are `PipelineError`s before the evaluator) → read the JSON bytes from the fixed orchestrator-owned path through `O_NOFOLLOW` → `JSON.parse` with the content-free diagnostic `<what> <path> is not valid JSON` (no parser message, token, position or fragment) → validate against the port's loader-compiled Draft 2020-12 schema snapshot (same single compiler, value-free diagnostics) → call `evaluatePipelineDecisionState`. Malformed JSON and schema failures are `PipelineError`s and never become `invalid_facts`; only a schema-conforming value that fails the model's fact-assignment contract yields the existing typed `invalid_facts`. Raw JSON bytes, parsed facts and fact values are never returned, stored or logged; the result is the deep-frozen existing `PipelineDecisionStateResult`, carries no transition target, and is never written to state. Repeated calls with the same inputs return structurally identical results and leave the filesystem byte-identical. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_decision_data.test.ts` covers the pipeline-input and accepted-agent-output sources (including two accepted activations with highest-index-wins under record permutation), uncovered/inconsistent/invalid_facts exact shapes, malformed-JSON canary and port-schema failures rejecting as `PipelineError` before the evaluator, damaged old non-winning records, incomplete/incoherent histories, index-bound violations (including `nextActivationIndex` validation before state-id resolution), missing sources, tampered snapshots (content, symlink, FIFO, kind, relocation) and invalid accepted outputs (symlink, FIFO, kind, winning-record digest mismatch), non-decision run inputs verified too, user binding paths never re-read, forged pipeline/snapshot clones/casts/Proxies and other-pipeline snapshots, filesystem invariance with no decision leaf, determinism + deep-freeze, and exact key sets with no transition target. Still not implemented and unchanged: production wiring (the production loader still rejects schema v2 before Launcher auth), graph-engine integration, durable state/activation records for decision states, retries, resume, and P01.
- Typed pipeline v2 runtime failures (pure, implemented, used by the production runner through the coordinator): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runtime_error.ts` gives the future coordinator a stable machine-readable `reason` for every expected pipeline-v2 data-plane failure without message parsing. Public API (exactly these four members — no broad retagging or construction helper is exported): `PIPELINE_V2_RUNTIME_FAILURE_REASONS` (9 strings: `run_input_invalid`, `run_input_modified`, `activation_prepare_failed`, `activation_output_invalid`, `accepted_output_modified`, `decision_input_invalid`, `run_output_missing`, `run_output_invalid`, `run_output_publish_failed`), `PipelineV2RuntimeFailureReason` (compile-time subset of `PIPELINE_V2_FAILURE_REASONS` from state schema v7, asserted at compile time and re-checked by a test), `PipelineV2RuntimeError` (still an `instanceof PipelineError`, message byte-identical to the former diagnostic, `reason` immutable via a non-writable own property, unknown reasons rejected at construction), and `isPipelineV2RuntimeError`. The construction/wrapping helpers are module-private inside `pipeline_v2_runtime.ts` (`runtimeFailure(reason, what)` — message formed explicitly, no `cause?` diagnostic injection — and `withRuntimeReason(reason, operation)` — one narrow semantics-scoped region; converts only its own `PipelineError`s preserving the message byte-for-byte, passes already-typed errors through unchanged (first reason wins), and rethrows non-`PipelineError`s unchanged). Reasons are assigned by operation context, never by classifying error text, and the reason boundaries are narrowed so an expected failure and an internal-invariant guard are never wrapped by one region: `verifyRunInputsSnapshot` carries `run_input_modified` and the resolve/digest phases of the single `resolveAcceptedHistory` chain carry `accepted_output_modified` (record-shape/coherence parsing stays plain); `snapshotRunInputs` assigns `run_input_invalid` to its binding-matching, per-binding-validation and formation/publication regions, while the declared-input lookup, the prepared-binding pairing and the cached tree/content checks run at plain sites before the regions; `prepareActivationData` has two narrow `activation_prepare_failed` regions (index/leaf preparation; data tree + port materialization) while the trusted-state port resolution — missing snapshot entry, derived type mismatch (plain guards) and the missing accepted output (typed, constructed directly) — runs at a plain site between the regions preserving the established error order, and metadata/provenance registration happens outside any region; `acceptActivationOutputs` hoists the compiler-invariant guard (a json output port always carries a loader-compiled schema; unreachable, plain `PipelineError`) before its `activation_output_invalid` region and pairs the schema with the port so the region reads a non-optional value; `evaluateDecisionStateFromData` runs its trusted-state resolution (missing snapshot entry, derived type mismatch, missing compiled schema — plain; missing accepted output — typed `decision_input_invalid` constructed directly) outside the region, which covers only read/parse/schema validation, and runs the evaluator outside the region; `collectRunOutputs` types the pre-existing publication target, staging creation/writes and the atomic rename as `run_output_publish_failed`, a missing required source `run_output_missing`, and present-source read/scan/parse/schema failures `run_output_invalid`, while a run output without a compiled schema (unreachable compiler invariant) stays a plain `PipelineError` instead of `run_output_invalid`. Plain `PipelineError`s (never retagged): provenance/caller violations (forged provenance objects with getter traps never invoked), malformed runner-owned records, impossible trusted state/port/source/type/schema combinations, invalid caller-supplied indexes/state ids, run/project-root infrastructure failures and unexpected evaluator/compiler errors (the coordinator normalizes them to `internal_error` later); `invalid_facts` stays a normal result, and no runtime error object, stack or message ever reaches durable state. `run_output_invalid` is typed but unreachable in deterministic flows (present sources are digest-verified immediately before staging; only a trusted-host race can defeat verify-then-copy) and is covered at error-contract level. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_runtime_errors.test.ts` (30 tests) covers each reachable reason through the public API, context-dependent reasons for the same helper (input tampering → `run_input_modified` before preparation/decision/collection, accepted-output tampering → `accepted_output_modified` before next activation/decision/collection, corrupted old non-winning records), malformed JSON inputs/agent outputs/decision inputs with content-free diagnostics, missing required run output → `run_output_missing`, pre-existing `outputs` root → `run_output_publish_failed`, missing optional run output staying a success, missing state output at preparation, malformed accepted records staying plain, forged objects with Proxy getter counting, `invalid_facts` as the unchanged deep-frozen result, canary-free diagnostics, the reason-subset check, the exact export surface (namespace keys = the three runtime members, no helpers), non-`PipelineError` propagation by identity through a typed region (throwing Proxy trap), run/project-root infrastructure staying plain, and invalid caller indexes/state ids staying plain; `pipeline_v2_runtime.test.ts` gained one reason-identity extension test. Existing exact-message tests pass byte-for-byte (the type changed, the diagnostics did not).
- Pipeline v2 execution snapshot/digest + durable pipeline identity (pure, implemented, used by the production runner through the coordinator): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_digest.ts` connects the trusted `ResolvedPipelineV2` with the schema v7 run state. Public API: `pipelineV2ExecutionSnapshot(pipeline) -> ReadonlyJsonValue` (explicit normalized execution snapshot), `pipelineV2ExecutionSnapshotJson(pipeline)` (canonical JSON string), `pipelineV2ExecutionDigest(pipeline)` (lowercase SHA-256 with domain separation `pipeline-v2-execution-snapshot\0` over the canonical snapshot JSON as UTF-8), and `pipelineV2RunPipelineIdentity(pipeline)` — the single construction point of the exact `create_run` pipeline identity (`{schema_version: 2, bundle_root: pipeline.bundleRoot, execution_snapshot_sha256, entry_state, max_transitions}`); the future coordinator must not duplicate this mapping. Every function gates on the existing `requireResolvedPipelineV2Provenance` before reading any field (hand-built/cast/spread/`structuredClone`/Proxy rejected with the stable `PipelineError`, getters never invoked) and adds no second compiler or re-validation. The snapshot is built explicitly, never `JSON.stringify(pipeline)`: `schema_version`, `entry_state`, `max_transitions`, run inputs in declaration order (`id`, `type`, `protected`, and for json inputs the declaring-site bundle-relative `schema_path` plus the loaded `schema_value`), run outputs in declaration order (`id`, `required`, `source`, derived `type`, derived `schema_value`), and states in declaration order — agent states (`profile` name only, bundle-relative `prompt` + exact `prompt_content`, input ports with `source`/derived `type`/`schema_value`, output ports with `type` + declaring-site bundle-relative `schema_path` + `schema_value`, `timeout_seconds`, `max_attempts`, transitions as `{index, outcome, to}` where `index` is the engine's original per-state index, agent input ports carry the derived `schema_value` only), decision states (`model` bundle-relative path, `decision_model` as the full normalized snapshot of the already-compiled model — `fact_ids`/`decision_ids`/`relations`/`constraints`/`rules` with expressions normalized to `{kind: "fact", fact_index, expected}` / `{kind: "all"|"any", children}` / `{kind: "not", child}`, input port with `source`/`type: "json"`/`schema_value`, ordered transitions), and terminals (`id`, `type`, `result`). Derived ports (agent inputs, run outputs, decision inputs) carry the schema value only — the loader never gives them a path. Absolute host paths are banned from the snapshot: bundle files are represented by their bundle-relative path (computed relative to the canonical `bundleRoot` via the new shared helper `bundleRelativePathInsideRoot` in `bundle_file.ts`; fail-closed if unrepresentable) alongside the already-loaded content, so relocating the same bundle changes nothing in the snapshot or digest; `bundle_root` is recorded separately by the identity. The snapshot is deep-frozen; repeated calls are structurally identical. When the bundle declares an `orchestration` section, its normalized compiled metadata (roles, templates, entry states and every planning role's `stage_wait` policy) joins the snapshot verbatim, so any policy change moves the digest; a bundle without the section keeps the exact pre-orchestration shape. Canonical serialization was extracted verbatim into the neutral shared helper `orchestrator/src/canonical_json.ts` (`canonicalJson`, key-sorted, order-of-mappings-independent, array order preserved) and `pipeline_digest.ts` now re-exports it with unchanged v1 behavior (the v1 digest has no domain prefix). The v2 digest is `SHA-256("pipeline-v2-execution-snapshot\0" + canonicalJson(snapshot))` as UTF-8. The snapshot JSON is an internal execution artifact — durable state and diagnostics carry only the SHA-256, never the snapshot body; run input contents/digests, binding paths, accepted outputs, sessions, credentials, env values, `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`, helper endpoints, stdout, timestamps, run ids and mutable state are never part of it (profile name only). `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_digest.test.ts` (42 tests) covers relocation independence (identical bundles in two absolute directories → identical snapshot JSON + digest, differing `bundle_root`), determinism, deep-freeze, forged-input rejection for all four functions (hand-built/clone/spread/Proxy with getter traps never invoked, per-function stable names), exact compiled-semantics shapes with original transition indexes, exact identity form, `create_run` acceptance + `validatePipelineV2RunState` round-trip through the reducer, absence of absolute paths and of credential/env/session/runtime canaries, and digest sensitivity for every execution-relevant class (prompt content, profile name, timeout, port types/sources, protected/required flags, JSON schemas, decision model rule + added constraint, transition target/outcome rename/order, single agent transition target, entry state, budget, terminal result, input/state declaration order) — mutating an agent `max_attempts` to 2 or making the entry state unreachable is now rejected by the loader, so no snapshot is produced (list order preserved as execution-relevant). Unchanged: production loader v2 rejection, `agent-smoke`, production runner, graph execution, decision evaluation, data plane, store/sink semantics, profiles, Sessions, helper transport, Dockerfiles/CI, default pipeline.


- Production-neutral pipeline v2 coordinator (implemented, wired into the production runner `runPipelineV2`): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_coordinator.ts` assembles the existing v2 components — `executePipelineV2Graph`, the data plane, the decision evaluator, the execution snapshot/digest, durable state schema v7, `PipelineV2RunStateSink` and the typed runtime failures — into one coordination flow (`coordinatePipelineV2Run({pipeline, runId, runRoot, projectSourcePath, inputBindings, sink, runtime}) -> PipelineV2CoordinationResult`, deep-frozen, `{ok:true,state}` (only terminal `run_succeeded`), the waiting branch `{ok:false, waiting:true, state}` (exact own keys `ok`/`waiting`/`state` — no reason, no refusal, no failure/success envelope), the plan-ready branch `{ok:false, planReady:true, state}` (exact own keys `ok`/`planReady`/`state` — the settled-but-unbound planning acceptance boundary; no waiting/reason/refusal/envelope) and `{ok:false,reason,state|null}); every consumer checks `waiting` and `planReady` before the ordinary `ok:false` branches; no new failure reasons. The provenance gate (`requireResolvedPipelineV2Provenance`) is the first statement and runs before any side effect — before the sink getters, the runtime callback capture, any filesystem operation, any sink dispatch and any Session; a forged/cast/spread/`structuredClone`/Proxy pipeline returns `{ok:false, reason:"internal_error", state:null}` with Proxy traps and runtime/sink getters never invoked, and the pipeline is never re-compiled or re-validated. The coordinator prepares the run-owned project copy itself (`prepareRunProject(params.projectSourcePath, runRoot)` — a ready-made `PreparedRunProject` is never accepted; the caller hands over only the source directory path, the source is never modified, and its path never enters the pipeline, execution document, worker env/argv, durable state or results) before the run-input snapshot, so the fixed order is: validate/capture runtime contract -> `prepareRunProject` -> `snapshotRunInputs` -> `create_run` -> the unchanged graph execution. A project preparation failure returns `{ok:false, reason:"run_input_invalid", state:null}` with zero sink commands and zero runtime sessions; a project published before a later snapshot or `create_run` failure stays in the run root for diagnostics. The coordinator creates the run-input snapshot itself (`snapshotRunInputs` — a caller-provided snapshot is never accepted, so durable `create_run.inputs` rests on the object it just received), dispatches every command through the structural `PipelineV2CoordinatorStateSink` seam (the production `PipelineV2RunStateSink` satisfies it; tests inject fault-injectable equivalents), and drives the graph only through `executePipelineV2Graph` — no second graph loop, no manual transition search, no coordinator cursor; the `onTransitionCommit` hook is the only transition writer (`transition_committed` with the index of the last settled execution) and must complete before the cursor moves. The agent runtime boundary follows the two-session capability model: `PipelineV2AgentRuntime.createExecutionSession(view, activation) -> PipelineV2ExecutionSession` (orchestrator-owned, run-root scope, never handed to the worker; `sessionId`, `runAgent(toolSession): completed | {status:"failed", reason:"worker_failed"|"worker_timeout"}`, `cleanup()`) and `createToolSession(view, activation) -> PipelineV2ToolSession` (project scope, its bearer is the worker's only authority; `sessionId`, `cleanup()`); the concrete runtime keeps bearers in private state and the coordinator sees only opaque session objects, ids and lifecycle methods. `createExecutionSession`/`createToolSession` are captured exactly once before the first side effect; after each Session is created its `cleanup` is captured first, then the `sessionId`, then — for the Execution Session only — `runAgent`, all before the durable write, so a durably recorded session always has a captured cleanup and method reassignment during pending callbacks cannot change dispatch (user objects are never frozen or modified); invalid runtime/session contracts and unexpected runtime exceptions normalize to `internal_error`. Agent order: `start_agent_execution` -> execution index from `sink.snapshot` -> `prepareActivationData` -> `agent_data_prepared` -> `createExecutionSession` -> `agent_execution_session_created` -> `createToolSession` -> `agent_tool_session_created` -> `agent_running` -> `runAgent(toolSession)` -> `acceptActivationOutputs` -> `agent_outputs_accepted` -> cleanup Tool -> cleanup Execution -> `agent_cleanup_completed` -> accepted records join the runner-owned history -> void to the engine (the engine applies its fixed `completed`); no next agent/decision starts before the cleanup and the durable transition commit. The cleanup order is fixed — the Tool session first (it revokes the worker's authority), then the Execution session — each created Session is cleaned exactly once on every path, and the first cleanup error never blocks the second. If the Tool Session cannot be created, the Execution Session is still deleted and the worker never runs. If a durable session write is not confirmed, the actually created sessions are still cleaned; at `not_committed` the authoritative state never claims the session existed (slot `not_required` even when the real cleanup succeeded), at `durability_unknown` the visible candidate is adopted, the sink is poisoned and no further writes happen. Decision order: next execution index -> `prepareDecisionStateData` -> `start_decision_execution {inputDigest}` -> `evaluatePreparedDecisionState` -> `decision_evaluated` (content-free record without the duplicate `state_id`) -> only `result.outcome` to the engine; no activation directory, no Session. Terminal phase: `terminal_reached` -> `collectRunOutputs` -> `run_outputs_published` -> success terminal `run_succeeded`, failed terminal `run_failed {terminal_failed}` (outputs are published for both). **Trusted stage-wait entry (implemented)**: the coordinator owns the exact stage→planning boundary of the compiled `stage_wait` policy at two points with ONE private recognizer (`stageWaitBoundary` — structural checks only, never message parsing): the live path right after its own successful `transition_committed` (a new post-commit signal checkpoint preserves the previous signal semantics — a signal accepted during the worker run is caught there with the identical durable finalization, before the wait entry), and the restart path before the engine continues when the durable transition is already committed (after the resume restore; the engine can never start a planning callback before the recognizer). The boundary requires: active/running, no terminal/failure/run-outputs, no open wait, exactly the current open generation with its open iteration (`pipelineV2OpenStageIteration` — historical waits/generations never selected), `executions.length === transitions.length` with the cursor count matching the journal, the last execution agent/`stage`/`cleanup_completed` with the exact open `iteration_index`, the last transition bound to it (`from` = its state, `to` = the cursor, outcome `completed`), and the destination state resolving through `compiledExecutionRoleFor` to the exact planning role carrying the trusted compiled `stage_wait`. Not recognized (the previous semantics are preserved byte-for-byte): the initial planning boundary, a planning completion without an open iteration, stage→control (still `invalid_graph` with `run_failed`), a stage→terminal edge (still the ordinary success), failed/in-flight/settled-but-unbound executions, malformed/progressed boundaries and already waiting runs. The derivation uses no caller policy: the reason and the verbatim action order come from the trusted compiled `stage_wait`; the accepted compiled plan is restored through the single public `restorePipelineV2AcceptedRunPlan` (no manual artifact reads, no manifest parsing, no new cache/registry) and must bind the open generation exactly (plan digest, `stage_id` → the declaration position and template); the `continue_stage` target is the bound stage's compiled template entry state (never a declaration-first/template-first heuristic — the `stage_position` check proves the `stage_id` binding), the `revise_task` target is the destination planning state (the cursor), and an unknown action id — impossible after the trusted load — fails closed before any publication. The wait is entered through the single public `enterPipelineV2Wait` (the request publication stays the wait store's semantics, never a reducer command); after a successful entry the coordination suspends through the module-private `CoordinatorSuspension` sentinel (never exported, unforgeable by the runtime/worker/downstream; the closed discriminated private kinds `waiting` and `plan_ready`) that the outer catch recognizes by exact identity and maps to the waiting result (`state` = the authoritative deep-frozen snapshot after the committed `run_waiting`, `requireSnapshot()` — no false identity claim about the controller's own result object). **A wait-entry attempt never writes a durable `run_failed`**: request publication failures, `run_waiting` persist failures with an orphan request (the boundary stays active and retryable), `durability_unknown` (the adopted waiting candidate is returned with reason `state_persist_failed`, the sink is poisoned and never re-read; a fresh reopen sees the durable waiting state), typed wait-controller refusals/conflicts and unexpected downstream errors all return the ordinary failure result (`state_persist_failed` passes through by typed reason, everything else `internal_error`) with the last authoritative snapshot and zero further dispatches — orphan adoption and completed-entry retries belong to the wait controller, and the coordinator implements no retry machine of its own. After a completed waiting boundary the ordinary `resumePipelineV2Run` preflight still refuses (`invalid_state`): the operator must apply a declared intervention first — `resume` never becomes an automatic wait-response loop. **Trusted plan-ready suspension (implemented)**: the coordinator stops a fully successful planning execution after the durable `agent_cleanup_completed` and before the engine can apply the planning transition, when the planning state's declared `completed` transition targets a state whose compiled role is `stage` — the exact shape whose stage start would otherwise fail `invalid_graph` because the plan has not been accepted yet. The seam sits inside the planning `executeAgent` (step 15b, after `tracking.unfinished = false`) and carries one plan-ready signal checkpoint (a signal accepted up to it wins with the previous 130/143 outcome and no transition is recorded; non-planning executions gain no new checkpoint — their signal semantics are unchanged and caught at the next callback start). Before the suspension the coordinator applies the single existing public `restorePipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext` to the authoritative `sink.snapshot` and verifies the documented result defensively (the exact five-key shape, the structural state correspondence through a local own-key comparator — never serialization, `planning_execution_index` = the last execution, the cursor binding, the accepted history carrying the planning state's accepted `plan_output` JSON output record, the run-input snapshot present); no plan output is read, no proposal/acceptance machinery runs (that belongs to the `resume-plan` controller). A verification failure propagates through the existing failure-finalization chain (no transition, the existing typed reason mapping — a damaged accepted output finalizes `accepted_output_modified`); malformed or hostile restore results never produce a suspension and never escape as a native `TypeError`. Planning transitions into control or terminal states are not affected (those flows never need the accepted plan, so they keep committing their transition and continue exactly as before — control and stage executions are untouched by the seam); a failed planning execution stays an ordinary worker failure. The suspension returns the exact plan-ready branch (no failure finalization, no signal cutoff, no further dispatch) and the boundary stays fully durable without any reducer command: status `active/running`, the settled-but-unbound planning execution (`executions.length === transitions.length + 1`, role `planning`, phase `cleanup_completed`, no `iteration_index`, on the cursor), no wait/generation/terminal/failure/run-outputs, no request manifest, no `run_waiting`, no transition. The initial plan-ready boundary is fully wired (this divergence is closed): the planning-run-plan handoff (`applyPipelineV2PlanningRunPlanHandoff`) gained the Initial A/B branches over the new initial stage transition controller (`openPipelineV2InitialStageTransition`, built on the shared transition-application kernel `applyStageTransitionCommit` that also serves the replanned transition controller), so `resume-plan` on the INITIAL plan-ready boundary of a fresh run accepts the first plan revision, opens the selected stage's first generation and iteration and commits the initial planning transition; the revise cycle (`revise-task` → planning execution → plan-ready → `resume-plan` → acceptance/handoff → stage execution → automatic `run_waiting`) stays fully wired. Failure policy (typed errors and context only, no message parsing): `PipelineV2RuntimeError` -> its reason; `PipelineExecutionError` -> its v7-compatible reason; worker result -> `worker_failed`/`worker_timeout`; sink/store failures -> `state_persist_failed`; a durably failed cleanup slot takes priority -> `session_cleanup_failed`; everything else `internal_error`; execution-level reasons outside the closed v7 subsets are recorded as `internal_error` on the execution while the run finalize keeps the true reason. An agent execution records `agent_failed` with the durable cleanup pair (not durably recorded -> `not_required`; durably recorded + confirmed cleanup -> `completed`; durably recorded + failed cleanup -> `failed`), a decision execution records `decision_failed`; a durably failed cleanup finalizes as `run_cleanup_failed`, everything else `run_failed`; no recursive finalization — one bounded `run_failed: state_persist_failed` attempt when the reducer still accepts it, and after `durability_unknown` the sink is poisoned, the visible snapshot is adopted, all writes and side effects stop (only the cleanups of the created Sessions still run) with `ok:false, reason:"state_persist_failed"`; an uncommitted `create_run` returns `state:null` with no fabricated document. Continuation from an existing clean active boundary is implemented as the separate production-neutral entrypoint `resumePipelineV2Run({pipeline, runId, runRoot, sink, runtime}, control) -> PipelineV2ResumeCoordinationResult` (the fresh API is not a mode with optional parameters; the resume accepts no project source, no input bindings, no caller-built snapshot/history, no new run id and no caller-supplied cursor or execution index). Both entrypoints meet in ONE internal continuation chain (`continuePipelineV2Coordination`): the chain owns the single engine invocation (`executePipelineV2Graph` for fresh, `executePipelineV2GraphResume` with the restored cursor for resume), the single transition hook, the single failure finalizer, the single session-cleanup sequence, the single terminal publisher and the single memoized signal cutoff — there is no second coordinator loop, no second budget model, no second accepted-output resolver and no re-read of any original user source. Resume ordering (fail-closed, every refusal before any side effect): pipeline provenance gate first (before sink/runtime/control reads and the filesystem; a Proxy pipeline causes no trap), then runtime/control capture, then the sink contract (non-poisoned with an existing snapshot — an opened existing run), then the run-id/root binding and the active-boundary precheck on the validated snapshot (only `status: "active"` + `phase: "running"` continues; an open waiting run — whose `wait_response_recorded` stays owned by the wait controller — a publishing phase, a final status and a reached terminal are refused before any filesystem read), then the read-only `restorePipelineV2RuntimeContext(pipeline, sink.snapshot, runRoot)` (durable-state coherence, exact pipeline identity, run layout, provenance-restored input snapshot and the full accepted-output history including old non-winning records), and only after the full restore do durable writes, Sessions and engine callbacks become possible: the engine starts from the restored cursor with the shared budget, the first execution index must be exactly the restored `next_execution_index` (asserted per execution against the durable snapshot; fresh runs establish the same chain), the accepted history starts from the restored `accepted_outputs` and is extended by new records, and the restored run-input snapshot is used without re-reading any original binding. Refusals are typed by structure and class only (`PipelineV2ResumeRefusalReason`: the existing restore reasons plus `missing_state`/`sink_poisoned`/`run_id_mismatch`/`internal_error`), carry the authoritative snapshot, and never finalize the state file — a damaged or incompatible run is not a new execution fact; post-restore failures keep the ordinary durable failure semantics. A signal accepted up to the post-restore checkpoint finalizes the existing run durably with the signal reason under the same priorities and exactly one cutoff. The two-phase decision adapter lives in `pipeline_v2_runtime.ts`: `prepareDecisionStateData(pipeline, runInputs, acceptedOutputs, stateId, executionIndex) -> PreparedDecisionStateData` (`state_id`/`execution_index`/`input_digest` — content-free; the digest is `SHA-256("pipeline-v2-decision-input\0" + length-framed bytes)` over the exact bytes read once through the existing O_NOFOLLOW path, registered with the trusted pipeline in a module-private WeakMap) and `evaluatePreparedDecisionState(pipeline, prepared) -> PipelineDecisionStateResult` (only the exact registered object for the same pipeline; parse + schema validation from the saved bytes, the file is never re-read; malformed JSON/schema stay `decision_input_invalid`, `invalid_facts` stays a normal result); the existing `evaluateDecisionStateFromData` is the unchanged compatibility wrapper over prepare + evaluate (no parallel implementation). `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_coordinator.test.ts` (45 tests) covers the provenance gate (forged cast/spread/`structuredClone`/Proxy pipelines rejected with the exact `internal_error` result, zero runtime/sink getter reads, zero Proxy trap hits, zero filesystem effects, zero commands, zero sessions and no durable document), the exact two-session durable command order for agent->decision->success (execution indexes 1/2, two transitions with exact engine indexes), two agent states with four unique session ids and the first activation fully cleaned plus its durable transition before the second Execution Session is created, decision and terminal states creating no sessions, the exact create/write/run/cleanup order with `runAgent` receiving the Tool Session object, Execution-Session create failure (no Tool Session, no worker), unconfirmed execution-session write (Execution cleaned once, Tool never created, slots `not_required`), Tool-Session create failure (Execution cleaned once), unconfirmed tool-session write (Tool then Execution cleaned once each, tool-first order), typed worker failures with the tool-first cleanup order, output-acceptance failure with the same cleanup, the cleanup outcome matrix (`{completed,failed}`, `{failed,completed}`, `{failed,failed}` -> `run_cleanup_failed`, first error never blocking the second), damaged session handles for both slots (missing/non-callable `cleanup`/`runAgent`, empty/non-string ids — no false `completed`), runtime/session method reassignment during Promise barriers, durability-unknown at both session-created writes and every failure-write level (adopted candidate, zero further dispatches), pre-rename failures, publication/terminal-write faults, typed data-plane reasons, `internal_error` for plain errors, snapshot/accepted-output re-verification through the existing data plane, the run-owned project copy (a project preparation failure reaching zero sink commands and zero sessions with no staging leftovers, a late run-input failure and a `create_run` store failure each keeping the published project with an untouched source, and a successful coordination keeping the source byte-identical with the source path and file bodies absent from the durable state and the result), a secrets/bearer/token/endpoint/prompts/facts/bodies/messages canary scan over the durable state and result, and the wrapper identity of `evaluateDecisionStateFromData` over the two-phase chain. Still unwired: `agent-smoke` (the production loader of v1 keeps rejecting schema version 2 before Launcher auth and before any Session), the default pipeline bundle, Docker Helper reality and Launcher auth. The coordinator's `create_run` `durability_unknown` completion path takes the shared memoized cutoff exactly once before returning the adopted candidate (the reason stays `state_persist_failed` — an accepted signal never masks the confirmed durability-unknown failure — and no further dispatch follows the poisoned sink), while a not-committed `create_run` returns `state:null` and requires no cutoff. The coordinator takes the required second parameter `control: PipelineV2CoordinatorControl` (`currentSignal`/`freezeSignal`, both captured exactly once after the provenance gate before any side effect — the production runner supplies them from the shared v1 `RunCauseGate`), gained the signal checkpoints listed in the runner bullet (before the project copy, after it, after the input snapshot and at its post-snapshot check — both inside the pre-`create_run` signal handling, so a signal noticed after a successful snapshot still yields the pre-`create_run` signal outcome with no state document and no escaping abort marker — after `create_run`, at every execution start, immediately before Session creates / `runAgent` / decision evaluation, and after the engine before the terminal record), and finalizes through the fixed priority `session_cleanup_failed` > accepted signal > typed failure > success with ONE memoized signal cutoff per run (`takeCutoff` — after the durable state exists every completion path freezes acceptance exactly once and reuses that snapshot; the cutoff sits inside `finalizeFailure` after cleanup and priority and in the terminal phase before the final write), so a late signal can never rewrite the recorded outcome; a failed terminal with published outputs finalizes only `terminal_failed`. The wait-entry proofs live in `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_wait_entry.test.ts` (17 tests): the live stage→planning entry through the coordinator's own commit hook (exact nine-command sequence, revision +9, request published from the trusted policy, no planning execution/session, no `run_failed`, waiting result with the authoritative snapshot identity, tool-first cleanup), the restart seam (only `run_waiting`, no repeated transition), the orphan-request adoption with byte-identical inode/mode/mtime/bytes, the durability-unknown adoption with the durable waiting state visible to a fresh reopen and the ordinary resume refusal afterwards, the derived-policy re-entry with zero dispatch and preserved file identity, the conflicting-policy conflict with zero writes, the action subsets and the reversed full order, stage→control keeping `invalid_graph`/`run_failed`, the stage→terminal edge keeping the ordinary success, a fresh planning run suspending plan-ready before any transition (no stage start, no wait, no `run_failed`), the two-generation historical flow where the current open generation is selected and the stage is bound by `generation.stage_id` (both stages sharing one template), the signal semantics before the boundary, the tampered/missing request and plan-artifact failures with zero dispatch and no `run_failed`, and the source-restriction scan (the private sentinel never exported, the single wait-controller/manifest imports, no reducer/store/publisher import, no second restore, the untouched failure-reason vocabulary, the runner checking waiting before refusal/failure and the CLI reporter checking waiting before the ordinary failure branch); the runner-level waiting outcomes for `orchestrator resume` and `orchestrator continue-stage` (the full continue intervention → successor stage execution → next auto wait, exit 0) and the CLI human/JSON waiting reports live in the same file and `tests/cli_main.test.ts`. The plan-ready suspension proofs live in `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_plan_ready.test.ts` (11 tests): the fresh planning run suspending plan-ready with the exact coordination keys/commands/state (no transition/wait/generation/terminal/failure, cleanup tool-first once each, the loader round-trip), the plan-ready signal checkpoint (a signal during the planning worker run finalizes `signal_sigint` with no transition), the planning worker failure unchanged, the planning→control and planning→terminal flows keeping their transitions and running to the terminal, the damaged accepted output failing the seam's restore verification as `accepted_output_modified` with no transition, the forged pipeline refused by the provenance gate, the exact runner planReady outcome (six keys, exit 0, no stage session, no request manifest), the ordinary resume refusal on the plan-ready boundary with an unchanged run-tree fingerprint, the full initial boundary E2E through the real runner (`runPipelineV2` suspends plan-ready → reopen → `resume-plan` accepts the first plan revision, opens generation 1/iteration 1 of the selected stage, commits the initial planning transition and the resumed successor stage execution fails the fake worker → `run_failed`; the lost-result retry on the waiting run refuses with exit 1, no reason field and an unchanged run tree), and the full revise cycle through the real entrypoints (`revise-task` → the planner2 planning execution → plan-ready → reopen → `resume-plan` → plan acceptance/handoff → the successor stage execution → automatic `run_waiting`, no duplicate planning execution).
- Public reason contract of the pipeline v2 commands (implemented, wired into every production runner entrypoint): every Pipeline V2 JSON outcome with a nonzero exit code carries a REQUIRED own `reason` property drawn from the one closed public vocabulary `PIPELINE_V2_PUBLIC_REASONS` in `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_public_reason.ts` (35 values: the 23 durable failure reasons of the run-state schema, the 9 coordinator pre-resume refusal reasons — overlapping — plus `run_already_exists`, `run_not_found`, `invalid_options`, `invalid_result`, `run_cleanup_failed`, `artifact_missing`, `artifact_modified`). Success, the controlled waiting suspension and the controlled plan-ready suspension carry exit code 0 and no `reason`; a successful inspect of a failed run is an inspect success (exit 0), not a command failure. The mapping is owned once by `publicReasonFor(cause, fallback)`: existing coordination results keep their already-public reasons byte-for-byte; typed error classes contribute their own closed `.reason` validated against the vocabulary or translated through one explicit internal table (`lifecycle_conflict`/`grant_conflict`/`intent_conflict`/`candidate_conflict`/`revision_conflict`/`plan_conflict`/`wait_conflict` → `invalid_state`; `invalid_intent`/`invalid_request`/`invalid_response`/`invalid_stage_id`/`stage_not_found` → `invalid_options`; `wait_storage_failed` → `state_persist_failed`; `artifact_mismatch` → `artifact_modified`; internal planning-chain invariants → `internal_error`); untyped causes fall back to the category the failure site owns (`invalid_options` for caller configuration, `internal_error` for unexpected failures). The fresh runner and the shared existing-run core (`resume`, `continue-stage`, `revise-task`, `resume-plan`) use the same mapping contract; composition controllers keep passing downstream errors by object identity, and the CLI only serializes the outcome. The reason is safe-identifier shaped, content-free (no paths, bodies, digests of artifacts, exception messages or stack traces) and never echoes an unknown internal value; hostile error objects (throwing getters, Proxies) can never break the mapper. Lost-result flow: the caller stores its run id before `run` → on a lost result `orchestrator inspect --run-id R` shows the actual boundary → the caller applies the boundary-specific command (`resume`, `continue-stage`, `revise-task`, `resume-plan`). A stale retry may return `invalid_state`; the caller then calls `inspect` — the reason is for machine handling, stderr diagnostics stay human-only. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_public_reason.test.ts` carries the shared outcome contract (exitCode 0 ⇒ success/waiting/planReady without reason; exitCode ≠ 0 ⇒ own `reason` in the closed vocabulary), the mapper battery (typed classes, the internal table, unknown errors → the fallback, hostile getters, canaries), and the source scans (one mapper, one vocabulary, no message parsing, no CLI-side classification).

- Inter-process run mutation lock (implemented, wired into every mutating entrypoint): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_mutation_lock.ts` serializes all mutating Pipeline V2 commands (`run`, `resume`, `respond`, `continue-stage`, `revise-task`, `resume-plan`) per run id across real OS processes — replacing the former one-mutating-caller-per-run convention. The primitive is the kernel advisory exclusive lock `flock(fd, LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` (Linux, via `bun:ffi` `dlopen` of libc with a cached candidate list `"libc.so.6"`, `"libc.so"`, `/lib/ld-musl-<arch>.so.1`, `/lib/libc.musl-<arch>.so.1` and `__errno_location` errno readback) taken on the **verified run-root directory descriptor itself** (`O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`): zero lock artifacts (fingerprints, layout and inspect are untouched — flock is on an open file description, the run directory's content is never modified, no chmod, no sentinel files), identity inherently bound to the verified state root + safe run id, native fail-closed on symlinked run roots, and `O_CLOEXEC` so docker-helper child processes never inherit the descriptor. `flock` is per-open-file-description, so even two descriptors in one process conflict (the in-process double-acquire test needs no seam), and the kernel releases the lock when the owning process exits or is killed — including SIGKILL — so there is no stale-lock metadata, no TTL/PID/mtime heuristic, no unlock command, and no crash-recovery protocol. The public API is `acquirePipelineV2RunMutationLock({runRoot, runId, mode: "fresh"|"existing"})` → `{busy:false, lock}`/`{busy:true}` or a typed `PipelineV2RunMutationLockError` (`run_layout_invalid` for a symlinked/file/FIFO run root, `state_document_missing`→`missing_state` for an existing-mode run root without a committed state document, `lock_infrastructure` for FFI failure; plus the pure `pipelineV2RunMutationLockPublicReason(cause)` mapper); acquisition is synchronous, the frozen lock handle releases idempotently, and the `fresh` mode additionally gates on state.json absence so an existing-run command that lost the fresh/existing race cannot steal the lock in the window between the fresh `mkdir` claim and the winner's lock (existing-run commands verify state.json existence before locking; if absent they refuse `missing_state` exactly like the previous `sink.open` refusal — the same reason and shape, zero lock contention). Three acquisition sites (one implementation body): the fresh runner after the exclusive run-root claim, the one shared existing-run core in `runExistingPipelineV2` after run-root verification (so `resume`, `continue-stage`, `revise-task`, `resume-plan` all serialize through it), and `respondPipelineV2Wait` after run-root verification; the lock is held to the end of the whole command — controller/intervention dispatch, coordinator sessions, automatic planReady continuation, terminal publication/finalization, cleanup, outcome construction — released via try/finally on every path including throws and signals, and never re-acquired by composed controllers. Busy outcome: `run_busy` joined `PIPELINE_V2_PUBLIC_REASONS` (36 values now); the loser returns the exact `{ok:false, exitCode:1, runId, runRoot, state:null, reason:"run_busy"}` (run root canonical and verified, zero state.json reads after the busy determination, zero `sink.open`, zero dispatch, zero sessions, zero durable writes) with the content-free human line `orchestrator: pipeline v2 <command> failed: run "<id>" is busy` (the reporter keeps waiting/planReady ahead of the failure branches); `inspect` stays read-only, imports no lock owner and never blocks writers. `run_already_exists` and the atomic claim are unchanged — a concurrent fresh `run` with the same id still reports the collision, never `run_busy`. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_mutation_lock.test.ts` (16 tests: acquire/release/reacquire, in-process double-OFD conflict, frozen handle, the existing-mode state-document gate, symlink/file/FIFO/absent → `run_layout_invalid`, unsafe run id, finally-release pattern, the reason mapper, a real cross-process busy probe and release probe, SIGKILL auto-release, different-run-id parallelism, the artifact-free/no-chmod proof, and the source-scan battery: one `libc.flock(` call site, one `run_busy` literal per entrypoint family, no message parsing, no stale-lock heuristics/registry/second serializer, no reducer/store/inspect imports); `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_mutation_busy.test.ts` (10 tests) proves the busy outcome and zero-effect contract at the runner and respond entrypoints in-process (gated winner holds the lock, `waitStart` loser refused), the busy retry after release, the non-busy cross-run parallelism, the CLI reporter busy line, the responder's pre-controller tree-identity, and the durability-unknown release path (the lock frees on a failed command; the next caller applies the ordinary durability semantics); `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_mutation_race.test.ts` (11 tests, real OS processes with stdin-gated fake-CLI children) covers the full concurrency matrix: resume/resume, continue-stage/continue-stage, revise-task/revise-task, continue-stage vs revise-task on one multi-action wait, resume-plan/resume-plan at planReady, respond/respond for one wait record (parent-held lock variant + zero-effects), fresh run vs existing-run command around the atomic claim (loser `run_busy` + unchanged collision reason for a second fresh `run`), SIGKILL of the winner (lock freed, existing recovery/refusal contract applies, no repair), different-run-id parallelism, and the loader-validity proof after every race. Still unwired: list-runs, event/log streams, HTTP API/T3, automatic wait-action policy, schema migrations.

- Control-plane subprocess substrate (implemented, not wired to HTTP/UI): `orchestrator/src/control_plane/routing.ts` is the single argv-routing owner of the closed typed command union (`run`/`inspect`/`resume`/`resume_plan`/`continue_stage`/`revise_task`/`respond`) mapped one-to-one onto the CLI grammar — every command validated per kind against exact own-key sets (hostile extras rejected without reading their values, one read per field via a normalized capture, own-`undefined` optional fields treated as absent), scalars through the existing shared predicates (`isPipelineV2SafeId`, `isPositiveSafeInteger`, `isCleanAbsolutePath`), `--json` always last, deterministic order pinned by tests, single argv elements without interpolation, `run` without an explicit pipeline root (command and config) keeping the bundled default-v2 behavior, `inspect` carrying no mutating flags, `revise_task` taking a `taskFile` (body materialization belongs to the future server layer). `orchestrator/src/control_plane/subprocess_client.ts` is the strict self-subprocess client: one `Bun.spawn` per call (no shell, no `shell:true`, no PATH search after capture), an immutable deep-frozen config captured once (Bun executable, entrypoint, working directory, config root, optional pipeline root, allow-listed `baseEnv` free of NUL, stdout/stderr byte limits, SIGINT grace interval — a bad config throws the typed `invalid_client_config` class error before any child), concurrent bounded stdout/stderr readers that keep draining past the limit (the child can never deadlock on a full pipe;
