stderr streams through to the parent's stderr while under the limit), stdin closed, and the single wire parser: exactly one JSON document plus one `\n` on stdout (empty/missing-newline/trailing-bytes/two-docs → `malformed_output`; non-object roots → `unexpected_output_shape`), exact own-key shapes per exit code and command kind (`__proto__` own keys rejected), document `exitCode` == process exit code (`exit_code_mismatch`), exit 1 reasons validated against `PIPELINE_V2_PUBLIC_REASONS` via the existing predicate (unknown → `unexpected_output_shape`; no stderr classification anywhere), 130/143 pinned to `signal_sigint`/`signal_sigterm`, exit 2 → `command_rejected`, other exits → `child_terminated`, and `inspect` accepting only `schemaVersion: 1` (anything else `unsupported_contract`) with the exact `{ok, exitCode, schemaVersion, runId, runRoot, revision, status, boundary, outputs}` projection (boundary/outputs passed through as opaque frozen JSON, never re-classified) plus the typed inspect-failure outcome. The raw durable `state` of a mutating outcome is verified as a permitted null-or-object runtime field and then dropped — never validated against schema v7, never copied, never returned, never stored, never used for routing; projections carry only run id, run root, exit code, the success/waiting/planReady/responded/failure discriminant and the public reason. Transport failures are one closed vocabulary disjoint from `PipelineV2PublicReason` (`invalid_client_config | invalid_command | spawn_failed | command_rejected | stdout_too_large | stderr_too_large | malformed_output | unexpected_output_shape | exit_code_mismatch | unsupported_contract | child_terminated | cancellation_forced`) carrying the expected run id when known. Cancellation is per call through an optional `AbortSignal`, isolated at the process boundary: abort before the spawn creates no child (listener installed before the pre-spawn re-check so a racing abort still reaches the child exactly once), the first abort delivers exactly one SIGINT to that call's child only, the grace interval ends in exactly one SIGKILL and `cancellation_forced` (recover through `inspect`), and a child that emits its 130/143 outcome inside the grace returns it as an ordinary projected failure; listeners and timers are removed on every path and no process-global signal handler exists. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_routing.test.ts` (17: exact argv for all seven commands, both pipeline-root forms, declaration-order inputs, special-character single elements, the unsafe scalar/path matrix, missing-field and hostile-extra rejection, the one-read Proxy battery, the immutable reason class) and `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_subprocess_client.test.ts` (44: the config battery incl. capture-once env isolation through the echo channel, the full wire-parser matrix over the real fixture child `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_child.ts` (success/waiting/planReady/responded/inspect boundaries, schemaVersion gate, reason vocabulary, signal outcomes, malformed shapes, oversized bounded streams that never hang, hostile stderr never classified, stdin closed, delayed children), the lifecycle battery (parallel children, per-call abort isolation, SIGINT-aware 130 within grace, SIGINT-ignoring → SIGKILL → `cancellation_forced`, pre-spawn abort with no child, double abort, external SIGKILL → `child_terminated` with a clean next invocation, exact argv through the real spawn), the source-scan battery (one `Bun.spawn` owner, no shell/exec strings, no orchestration-internal imports, no stderr classification, no process-local run registry, one wire parser, the public vocabulary imported from its owner and never copied), and the real-CLI smoke through `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_daemon.ts` (fake Docker Helper daemon) + `orchestrator/fixtures/control-plane/` (two-stage manual bundle + profiles): caller runId → planReady projection without state → inspect schemaVersion 1 with the `plan_ready` boundary → busy race (loser `run_busy`, winner `waiting`) → lost `continue_stage` projection recovered through inspect (`waiting` at wait index 2). Still unwired: no HTTP/`Bun.serve`, no REST surface, no project registry, no browser UI, no operation queue, no events/logs streaming, no automatic stage/wait policy — the CLI child remains the sole owner of orchestration semantics and the registry plus HTTP server are separate future layers.
- Control-plane project registry (implemented, not wired to HTTP/UI): `orchestrator/src/control_plane/project_registry.ts` is the single authority-mapping owner between the browser-facing world and the host — `loadControlPlaneProjectRegistry(registryPath)` loads a versioned JSON registry (schema version 1: `{schema_version, projects[] {id, project_root, config_root, pipeline_root: string|null, launcher_id: string|null, input_roots[] {id, root}}}`, exact own keys at every level, prototype-chain fields never satisfying the shape, unknown and `__proto__`-like own extras rejected, shared safe-id predicate for every id, unique project ids and per-project input-root ids, `pipeline_root`/`launcher_id` mandatory keys with null semantics — null pipeline root = bundled default-v2, null launcher id = the existing default launcher path, declaration order preserved verbatim, unknown future versions fail closed) read-only through `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` (leaf symlink, directory, FIFO, missing, unreadable and oversized registry paths are typed failures; one fixed 1 MiB size bound; one `JSON.parse` owner), canonicalizes every operator-configured root through real filesystem resolution (real non-symlink directory required at the leaf, symlinked ancestors canonicalize, duplicate canonical input roots within a project rejected) and returns a recursively frozen registry — loaded once per service lifetime, no hot reload, no watch, no discovery, no repair; a later change to or deletion of the file never alters an already-loaded registry. `resolveControlPlaneRunCommand(registry, request)` is the one composition boundary from `{runId, projectId, inputs[] {id, rootId, ref}}` (the transport-neutral public run request; every field a safe identifier, `ref` a single child name with no path syntax, non-empty unique inputs, exact own keys, one read per field, hostile Proxy extras unread) to the existing typed `OrchestratorCommand` (kind `run`) plus its `OrchestratorRoutingConfig`: per input it resolves `<canonical input root>/<ref>` immediately before the command is built (lstat, leaf symlink rejected, regular file required, directory/FIFO/socket/device rejected, canonical leaf must remain an immediate child of the canonical root, missing file typed `input_not_found`), never reads input contents (the existing runner stays the owner of input canonicalization/snapshot/modified detection; this layer only decides which paths the caller may name), takes every path exclusively from the registry (the caller can never override config root, pipeline root, launcher id or project path), proves the composition routable through the existing `routeOrchestratorCommand` (called, never duplicated; downstream routing errors pass through unchanged) and returns `{command, routingConfig}`. The closed failure vocabulary `invalid_registry_path | registry_missing | registry_unreadable | registry_too_large | invalid_registry | unsupported_registry_version | project_not_found | input_root_not_found | input_not_found | input_invalid | invalid_request` is disjoint from the public run reasons and the transport reasons; registry failures happen before any spawn, are not Pipeline V2 outcomes, create no run root and never call inspect, and diagnostics are content-free (no registry body, no unknown field names, no input/task contents, no host paths, no raw system error text — only already-validated safe ids). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_project_registry.test.ts` (29: the document battery over real directories with the shape/duplicate/version/bound/path-kind matrices, root canonicalization including a symlinked ancestor and duplicate canonical roots with paths never echoed, the request battery (project/root not found, duplicate ids, unsafe refs with `/`/`..`/backslash, missing/symlink/directory/FIFO/socket inputs, two inputs in declaration order, the hostile one-read Proxy, mutation after the call, file deletion after load), the exact composition and exact real-router argv with the bundled-default and explicit-pipeline forms, the zero-effect fingerprint proof, the source-scan battery (one `JSON.parse`, no spawn/HTTP/discovery/watch, no orchestration-internal imports, one file-read site, vocabulary not copied, routing reused), and the real composition smoke: registry → resolve → existing `OrchestratorSubprocessClient` → real CLI + fake Docker Helper daemon → planReady projection without state → inspect schemaVersion 1). Still unwired: no HTTP/REST, no UI, no authentication/RBAC, no registry hot reload, no API input upload/materialization, no taskBody temp files, no operation queue, no list-runs/events/logs, no automatic stage/wait policy — the subprocess client remains the sole execution substrate.
- Control-plane durable run binding store (implemented, not wired to HTTP/UI): `orchestrator/src/control_plane/run_binding_store.ts` (public facade) + `orchestrator/src/control_plane/run_binding_store_internal.ts` (internal core) durably bind one caller run id to one registry project id before the control-plane starts the first `run` for it, so after any restart every later operation resolves its project, config and pipeline authority exclusively from this binding — a caller-supplied project id is never accepted as ownership evidence and is not even a parameter of the resolution. The binding document is a versioned immutable canonical JSON record `{"project_id":"...","run_id":"...","schema_version":1}` (exact own keys, shared `isPipelineV2SafeId` predicate for both ids, no paths/config/launcher/inputs/timestamps/credentials) published as a 0600 regular file at `<store-root>/bindings/<runId>.json` inside a store-owned 0700 `bindings/` namespace (exclusive mkdir, identity fixation, adopted without modification), with a `sha256` digest over the new `control-plane-run-binding\0` domain carried only inside the substrate candidate. `ControlPlaneRunBindingStore.open(root)` binds the operator-configured clean absolute real non-symlink store root (canonical path + `dev`/`ino` fixation, re-verified on every operation — a vanished or identity-changed root fails closed); `claim({runId, projectId})` validates the scalars (`binding_invalid` — an invalid request never reserves a run id), pre-checks the leaf (a valid same-project binding is adopted with zero writes as `existing`; a different project is the typed `binding_conflict` whose diagnostics never name the existing owner; a defective object is `binding_invalid`), then publishes through the one shared immutable-document substrate (exclusive temp file, fsync, exclusive `link()` without overwrite, parent durability barrier, read-back verification of the published bytes) and returns `created` — the same-content race loser adopts byte-identical bytes through the substrate; `load(runId)` is strictly read-only (missing namespace or file → `binding_not_found`, nothing created or repaired). The closed failure union `invalid_binding_root | binding_store_unavailable | binding_not_found | binding_conflict | binding_invalid | binding_persist_failed | project_unavailable` is disjoint from the registry, transport and public-run vocabularies; diagnostics are content-free (no paths, no document bytes, no owner project ids, no raw system text). Crash windows converge without a second framework: pre-link faults leave nothing published (the owned temp is cleaned; a failed cleanup leaves a tolerated orphan temp — the next publication uses a fresh exclusive temp name), a post-link durability-unknown keeps the published binding and an exact retry confirms it through byte-equal adoption, and there is deliberately no hardlink-count rejection because the protocol itself makes the final inode a transient two-link object between publication and temp removal (the canonical byte verification is the integrity defense — any mutation through an external link fails the canonical comparison on every read). The store has no delete/update API, no process-local registry, no PID/TTL/mtime recovery, never touches the Pipeline V2 run root or the durable run state, and never spawns anything. The one read-only enumeration `listBindings()` (added for discovery) re-verifies the root and the `bindings/` namespace, enumerates the namespace entries exactly once through the substrate IO's `readdirNames` (the single readdir owner in the codebase), partitions them by the exact `<runId>.json` name grammar and the substrate-owned immutable-publication temp grammar (crash-orphan temps of that exact shape are ignored; any other unknown file, directory, symlink or FIFO is a typed `binding_invalid`), loads every final binding through the existing single leaf-verification path (mode 0600, canonical bytes, filename↔document run-id binding), sorts lexicographically by run id and returns a fresh deeply-frozen projection — no state/run-root reads, no writes, no timestamps, no in-memory index. `resolveBoundControlPlaneProject(registry, store, runId)` is the one composition boundary: the binding is loaded from the store and the project is looked up exclusively in the already-loaded frozen registry (a hostile registry or store argument is refused fail-closed; a project the current registry no longer declares is `project_unavailable` with zero writes — the binding is never deleted and no subprocess is started); CLI-created unbound runs fail closed as `binding_not_found` and are never adopted or guessed from raw state (operator import/adoption is a separate future increment). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_run_binding_store.test.ts` (50 tests: the open battery, the exact canonical bytes/layout/modes, created/existing/conflict semantics with content-free conflict diagnostics, the one-read options capture, the full hostile stored-object matrix for load and claim (malformed, noncanonical, oversized, symlink, directory, FIFO, wrong mode, hardlink mutation), the store-infrastructure defenses (symlink/file/FIFO/wrong-mode namespace, replaced/vanished root), the durability windows through per-call IO fault injection (pre-link matrix, orphan-temp tolerance, post-link durability-unknown adoption confirmation, EEXIST conflict classification, namespace mkdir/chmod faults), the authority resolution battery (frozen result, unbound run closed with the three-parameter signature, removed project with a zero-write fingerprint, hostile arguments, run-tree independence), the real multi-process race matrix through `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_run_binding_claim_child.ts` (same run+project: one publisher, identical bindings, stable inode; same run, different projects: one winner, typed conflicts, owner never revealed; different runs in parallel; a SIGKILLed claiming process converging on retry with exactly one final file), and the permanent composition smoke through the production layers (registry → `resolveControlPlaneRunCommand` → claim before the spawn → `OrchestratorSubprocessClient` → real CLI + fake Docker Helper daemon → planReady → fresh store open → load → `resolveBoundControlPlaneProject` → inspect through the bound authority with the conflicting project refused and both fingerprints unchanged). The store is production-wired to the HTTP discovery endpoints (`GET /v1/projects`, `GET /v1/runs`). Still unwired: UI, binding import/adoption, retention/deletion, operation queue, events/logs, run status aggregation.
- Control-plane HTTP service (implemented, production-wired): `orchestrator/src/control_plane/http_server.ts` is the single `Bun.serve` owner of the production tree and `orchestrator/src/control_plane/server_main.ts` is its runnable operator entrypoint (`bun server_main.ts --registry ABS --binding-store ABS --port N [--env KEY=VALUE ...]` — strict own flags, no ambient child-env inheritance, exit 2 with a content-free diagnostic, one safe listen line `control-plane: listening on 127.0.0.1:<port>` and no registry contents, credentials or absolute paths). The service composes the existing substrate only — HTTP → project registry → durable run binding → `OrchestratorSubprocessClient` → existing CLI subprocess → Pipeline V2 — and never imports runner/controller/reducer/state/store modules, never executes orchestration in-process, never reads `state.json`, run roots, proposals, ledgers or worker files, and adds no shell/exec and no second execution path. Exactly nine versioned routes: `POST /v1/projects/:projectId/runs` (fresh run body `{run_id, inputs[] {id, root_id, ref}}`), `GET /v1/runs/:runId` (inspect), the five mutation routes `POST /v1/runs/:runId/{resume,resume-plan,continue-stage,revise-task,respond}` with exact-key snake_case bodies (`{}`; `{stage_id, initial_budget}`; `{wait_index, additional_iterations}`; `{wait_index, action_id}`; `{wait_index, task_id, task {root_id, ref}}`), plus the two read-only discovery routes `GET /v1/projects` (declaration-order `{schema_version:1,kind:"projects",projects[] {id, input_roots[] {id}}}` — safe ids only, no roots/config/launcher/paths, no query parameters) and `GET /v1/runs` (`{schema_version:1,kind:"runs",runs[] {run_id, project_id, project_available},next_after}`) built exclusively from the durable binding-store enumeration with optional `limit` (positive safe integer, default 100, maximum 200) and `after` (safe run id, exclusive lexical cursor) — unknown/duplicate/empty query fields are request errors; `next_after` is non-null only when another binding follows the page, a removed registry project stays visible with `project_available: false` (which is never a run status), repeated responses are byte-identical, the listing takes no mutation lock, spawns no subprocess, never reads run roots or `state.json`, and offers no snapshot isolation under concurrent claims (a concurrent binding lands in the page or at the next refresh; the immutable publication makes a partial document unobservable). Configuration is loaded once and immutable: registry path, binding-store root, client runtime (bun executable, orchestrator entrypoint, working directory, explicit `--env` allow-list, output/SIGINT limits), listen port and the fixed body limit; the registry and binding store are opened once with no hot reload. The routing authority (config root and pipeline-root form) is derived exclusively from the loaded registry at startup — every declared project must share one authority (`registry_authority_mismatch` otherwise) — so the one immutable client's routing is exactly the bound project's routing. Authority flow: a fresh run validates the request document, resolves the registry command (`resolveControlPlaneRunCommand` — an invalid request or missing input never reserves the run id), claims the binding, then spawns; every existing-run route resolves `resolveBoundControlPlaneProject` first and never accepts a projectId from the HTTP caller — a CLI-created unbound run fails closed as `binding_not_found` before any subprocess, and a removed project is `project_unavailable`. The revise-task task file is resolved exclusively through the one reusable bound-project input resolver (`resolveControlPlaneBoundProjectInput` in the registry module, the same canonicalization policy as run inputs — the project must be the exact frozen registry entry; a caller-supplied task-file path is structurally impossible). Loopback single-user trust boundary (documented as NOT auth/RBAC): the server binds `127.0.0.1` only, the Host header must equal the actual listen authority exactly, Origin must be absent or the exact service origin and is refused before body parsing and before any effect, POST accepts exactly `application/json`, the body is read through a bounded stream loop (never unbounded `request.text()`) with the fixed byte limit, GET never accepts a body, OPTIONS/CORS are not supported, and percent-encoding, empty/dot segments, extra segments, unknown methods and unsafe path ids are refused; query strings are refused on every non-discovery route and accepted only on the two discovery routes in the exact documented grammar; no HTTP parameter ever becomes a host path, argv fragment or env name. Request validation is exact-own-keys with the shared safe-id/positive-safe-integer predicates, unique input ids, no normalization/sorting/defaults, and hostile Proxies never have extra fields read. Response contract: `schema_version: 1` with three exact shapes — `{"schema_version":1,"kind":"command_result","run_id","outcome":"succeeded|waiting|plan_ready|responded|failed","exit_code"[,"reason"]}` for a correctly received orchestrator outcome (ALWAYS HTTP 200, including failed outcomes — a Pipeline V2 public reason is never reclassified into an HTTP status; the CLI's own inspect failure is projected as a failed command result), `{"schema_version":1,"kind":"run","run_id","revision","status","boundary","outputs"}` for a verified inspect projection (boundary/outputs copied without new classification, runRoot dropped), and `{"schema_version":1,"kind":"error","layer":"request|registry|binding|transport","reason"}` with one closed status table (400/404/405/409/413/415, 502 transport, 500 only for genuine control-plane infrastructure failures) and no message/details/path. Request lifecycle: the request stays synchronous relative to the subprocess call, the request signal is never forwarded to the client — a client disconnect does not cancel the orchestration, a lost response is recovered through `GET /v1/runs/:runId`, and parallel mutators are not serialized by the HTTP layer (the kernel run mutation lock stays the single owner; the loser observes the ordinary `run_busy` outcome); no process-local queue, mutex, registry, retry machine or probing exists. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_http_server.test.ts` (52 tests over the full matrix — route/method/path parsing for all nine endpoints, exact-key/body validation with malformed/empty/trailing/oversized bodies, Host/Origin/content-type gates before effects with fingerprint proofs, fresh-run registry→claim→real-subprocess flow, service restart with byte-identical bindings and authoritative inspect, projectId never accepted, unbound CLI runs fail closed, removed-project conflicts, parallel mutators on the kernel lock, lost-response continuation, bound-input revise-task references end to end, zero-write inspect fingerprints, host-path canaries across every response, the unknown-inspect-schemaVersion transport failure, the entrypoint serve/flags/SIGTERM battery, and source scans pinning one `Bun.serve` owner, the import whitelist and every banned capability). Still unwired: auth/RBAC/TLS/remote bind, run status aggregation, list filtering/search, operation queue/202 jobs, event/log streaming, registry hot reload, input upload/materialization, retention/delete, and automatic stage/wait policy. The same-origin browser UI v1 (see the next bullet) is production-wired on top of this service.

- Browser UI v1 (implemented, production-wired): the loopback service additionally serves a same-origin, dependency-free browser UI — `GET /` (`orchestrator/ui/index.html`), `GET /ui/app.js` and `GET /ui/style.css` — so an operator can list registry projects, list bound runs with `next_after` pagination and refresh, inspect one run (status/revision/boundary/outputs) and create a fresh run with a caller-owned run id (browser `crypto.randomUUID()` or manual entry, displayed before the request) and registry-controlled input references `{id, root_id, ref}`. The static assets are owned once by `orchestrator/src/control_plane/ui_assets.ts`: they are captured from the shipped `orchestrator/ui/` tree exactly once at service startup before `Bun.serve`, validated against fixed per-asset size bounds and fixed content types (`text/html`, `text/javascript`, `text/css`, all `charset=utf-8`), never re-read per request (the request handler imports no `fs` and computes no filesystem path), and served with `Cache-Control: no-store` plus the fixed security header set on every UI response — `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; img-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self'; object-src 'none'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`. No inline script/style, no eval/Function, no external origins, no service workers, no WebSocket/EventSource, no CORS, and the existing exact Host/Origin gates apply unchanged. The UI consumes only the versioned API: `app.js` is one self-contained ES module whose exported pure model validates every document fail-closed (`schema_version: 1`, a known `kind` among `projects|runs|run|command_result|error`, exact field shapes — unknown versions/kinds/shapes are UI contract errors, never guessed), keeps run pagination state, builds the exact fresh-run body and describes lost-response recovery; the thin DOM adapter renders exclusively through `createElement`/`textContent` (no innerHTML/insertAdjacentHTML/document.write), carries an aria-live status and alert error region, semantic form/table/button markup, visible keyboard focus, no browser storage, and boots only where `document` exists. The browser API client is the one network owner: it checks the content type, reads bounded JSON, classifies only by `kind` (never by HTTP status or headers), shows failures as `layer: reason` and drops the raw response. The fresh-run POST is never retried automatically: on a lost response (network failure after send) the UI shows outcome unknown, keeps the run id displayed, performs exactly one safe GET inspect of the known run id and otherwise points at manual Refresh/Inspect; `run_already_exists` and every other failed command result are displayed, never re-POSTed. The UI never accepts or displays filesystem paths, launcher ids, env, argv, credentials, raw state, stderr or task bodies; the intervention flows (resume, resume-plan, continue-stage, revise-task, respond), automatic polling, logs/events, input upload and deletion stay CLI/API-only. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/ui_model.test.ts` (32 tests over the pure model, the one client and the source scans), `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_ui_assets.test.ts` (10 tests over the asset owner, the single-fs-importer scans and the image packaging proof) and `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_ui_http.test.ts` (12 tests over the routes, security headers, gates, grammar, captured-once serving and the leakage battery); the pre-existing `/`-path expectation in the route-parser battery moved into the UI suites. Still unwired: logs/events, input upload, deletion/retention and automatic action policy. The manual boundary-driven lifecycle controls (Browser UI v2) and the bounded read-only live observation (Browser UI v3) are production-wired — see the next bullets.- Docker Helper 2.2.0-rc.10 runtime adapter (implemented, wired into the production runner): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_docker_runtime.ts` implements the coordinator's `PipelineV2AgentRuntime` on the official docker-helper CLI — `createDockerHelperPipelineV2Runtime({pipeline, profiles, cli, helperConfig, operatorEnv, expectedLauncherId?, runRootProjection})`. Factory validation happens before the first helper/filesystem side effect: pipeline provenance, a loaded profile for every agent state, immutable per-state execution snapshots (profile name, image, OpenCode config content, destination-sorted env bindings — the source map and profile objects are never re-read during activations and are never frozen or modified), a captured `cli` function, validated helper config, a frozen operator-env copy, the optional expected launcher id, and the trusted `runRootProjection {localRoot, daemonRoot}` captured by value at factory creation (mutating the source object afterwards cannot influence execution; it is never part of the pipeline, profile, execution document, worker environment or durable state). Daemon-visible projection: `localRoot` is the canonical run root the data plane works on and `daemonRoot` the same directory by its daemon-visible absolute path (host mode passes the same string); before every `createChildSession` of an activation the adapter proves the projection fail-closed — `activation.run_root === localRoot`, both roots real non-symlink directories resolving exactly to their declared canonical paths with identical dev/ino, and the project/activation/data/inputs/outputs roots and the `.orchestrator` directory present as real non-symlink directories, the execution document as a real non-symlink regular file, each canonically resolving to its own declared path on both sides (a symlinked parent component redirects `realpath` and fails) with identical dev/ino under both roots (same-kind substitutions — symlinked, swapped file/directory, FIFO/socket pairs — are rejected) — any divergence throws the typed `PipelineV2ProjectionError` (stable reason `local_root_mismatch`/`projection_root_invalid`/`projection_pair_mismatch`/`projection_suffix_unclean`, never classified from message text) before any Session and before any helper CLI side effect; Session workspaces are only `daemonRoot + relative(localRoot, localPath)` translations (Execution → `daemonRoot`, Tool → the translated project root) and mount sources stay workspace-relative, so neither root path appears in worker argv/env, the execution document or durable state. `createExecutionSession` creates the child Session with workspace = the daemon-visible run root (Execution: its bearer authorizes every CLI call and is never handed to the worker), `createToolSession` with workspace = the daemon-visible project root (Tool: its bearer is the worker's only authority); both go through `createChildSession`/`deleteChildSession` with the launcher operator env only and the issuance-time filesystem policy below, verify `launcher_id` against `expectedLauncherId` (a mismatched known Session is deleted by the adapter itself with exactly one delete attempt before the error is thrown: a confirmed delete reports `wrong_authority` with the confirmed cleanup, a failed delete reports `cli_failure` naming the mismatch, the session id and the explicit `cleanup could not be confirmed` fact — such a session may remain and is never claimed to be gone; the Tool session additionally requires the uncleaned Execution Session of the same activation in this runtime), return opaque frozen handles carrying only `sessionId` + lifecycle methods, and keep bearer tokens in an instance-private WeakMap keyed by the exact handle object. Issuance-time Session filesystem policy (RC10 `--filesystem-root PATH=ACCESS` with daemon-visible absolute paths, the positional WORKSPACE always last, fixed order): the Execution Session request is exactly `<daemonRunRoot>=read_only`, `<daemonRunRoot>/project=read_write`, `<daemonRunRoot>/activations/<index>-<state>/data/outputs=read_write` (the run root read-only — the explicit workspace entry replaces the implicit workspace grant — the shared run-owned project writable, exactly the current activation's outputs RW; no separate inputs root is requested because the read-only run root already covers the activation inputs and the execution document; state.json/waits/other activation trees carry no writable entry; a revisit activation carries its own new global index), and the Tool Session request is exactly `<daemonRunRoot>/project=read_write` (the project workspace is the worker's only filesystem authority; run-root siblings stay unreachable and inputs/outputs reach the worker only through the Execution mounts). The daemon-normalized immutable snapshot is authoritative (consumed via `session show`/`self`) and is not a verbatim echo of the request — redundant same-access entries may collapse and a narrower nested read-only region stays; the production path adds no extra `session show` round-trip because the create contract does not return the snapshot. The policy transport is owned once by `createChildSession` in `orchestrator/src/launcher.ts` (`SessionFilesystemRoot`/`CreateChildSessionOptions`): roots are copied to argv in one synchronous pass before the first await (caller objects never frozen or modified, argv byte-identical without roots), and transport-shape validation — clean absolute host path via the shared `clean_path.ts` helper (no relative, empty, whitespace-padded, NUL, `.`/`..` or doubled/trailing-slash spellings), exact `read_only`/`read_write` access, values never in env — rejects invalid shapes before any CLI call; duplicate canonical roots, overlap and narrowing are daemon decisions and are never re-implemented client-side; the helper's authorization semantics (narrowing, ceiling, `invalid_filesystem_policy`/`read_only_root`) are never re-implemented — the daemon decides, and a daemon-side rejection surfaces as the ordinary `DockerHelperError("cli_failure")`. The root paths are computed from the provenance-checked `PreparedActivationData` through one shared `activationPolicyPaths` computation (the same clean-relative machinery and canonical cross-checks as the worker mount sources) and translated into the daemon namespace through the proven run-root projection, frozen inside the adapter before `createChildSession` is called, and travel only as argv values (never env, durable state, execution document or diagnostics); session policy is immutable after creation, only narrows the Launcher ceiling, no silent downgrade and no fallback creation without roots exists, and no filesystem-policy field exists in the pipeline schema, profiles, execution document or durable state. `runAgent(toolSession)` is a pure registry gate before any CLI call: the exact Tool handle of this runtime, linked to the exact Execution record of the same state id, activation index and `PreparedActivationData` object, both uncleaned, the worker not yet run; forged/hand-built/spread-copied/Proxy-wrapped handles and handles of another runtime instance are rejected with getters never invoked, and a repeated run is rejected. Worker execution is adapter-owned (implemented; `docker-helper run` is no longer the execution transport): the only helper CLI call left in the worker path is the image `pull` through the Execution Session (`pull --endpoint SOCKET IMAGE` with `DOCKER_HELPER_SESSION_TOKEN=<Execution bearer>` as the only env; a failed pull is `worker_failed` and never falls back to a cached image). The worker itself runs as one Docker Helper HTTP operation over the helper Unix socket owned by the internal `runWorkerOperation` state machine (`orchestrator/src/worker_operation.ts`): `POST /run` exactly once (a lost create response is never retried — the daemon may have accepted the operation, which can remain orphaned, the honest C1 window), then the single drain owner `GET /operations/:id/logs?offset=N` and the single status poll owner `GET /operations/:id` in one bounded loop (`WORKER_OPERATION_POLL_DELAY_MS` = 100ms), and exactly one `POST /operations/:id/cancel` on abort or deadline. The `POST /run` body preserves the exact request parity with the docker-helper CLI 2.3.1 (verified against the real CLI through a recording daemon): the same field set and field order (`image`, `entrypoint`, `workdir`, `command`, `environment`, `mounts`, `helper_socket`), the same mount order (project RW, activation inputs RO, activation outputs RW) with clean workspace-relative sources cross-checked against the prepared activation, and the same resolved environment values keyed by their destination names in sorted key order (matching the CLI's map marshaling); the only credential is the Execution Session bearer in the `Authorization` header. The operation outcome is classified only from the authoritative status (`succeeded` with a zero `exit_code`/`result_code` → `completed`; a contradictory `succeeded` with a non-zero code, `failed`, `cancelled`, an unreachable/malformed/unknown status → `worker_failed`; the deadline → `worker_timeout`); every log failure (404/500, malformed, oversized, timeout, daemon head eviction, local bound) is outcome-neutral and only degrades the internal bounded capture (`{status: captured|truncated|unavailable, byteCount, tailBytes ≤ 8 MiB UTF-8 bytes, truncatedDaemon, truncatedLocal}` — `captured` never means `complete`; no durable publication in this increment). Worker output bytes are forwarded to the orchestrator's stderr exactly once per chunk by the same drain owner; `--json` stdout stays one JSON document. Signals: the runner-owned `WorkerSignalHub` (`orchestrator/src/worker_signals.ts`) arbitrates first-wins between the operation deadline (`claimTimeout`) and user signals (`forwardUserSignal` — a signal losing to the deadline is neither delivered nor recorded), the hub forwards into the existing `RunCauseGate` composition so 130/143/`worker_timeout` outcomes and the single cutoff are unchanged, and a signal during a hung log fetch aborts the fetch and triggers the one cancel. The operation id lives only inside one operation call: never returned, never persisted, never in state/events/run tree/diagnostics; there is no process-local registry beyond the call lifetime and no recovery after a process crash (C1/C2 are honest loss windows — durable operation identity belongs to a later increment). The transport primitive is owned once by `orchestrator/src/helper_http.ts` (bounded fetch over the Unix socket, the explicit bearer header, the strict HTTP status handling, one JSON parser owner, no ambient credentials, no shell/PATH, the token never in diagnostics). Cleanup is a memoized Launcher-authority delete: the physical delete runs at most once per Session, repeated/concurrent cleanups share one promise, and Tool/Execution cleanups are separate operations. UID/GID boundary: the adapter cannot prove a default image UID through the CLI — the supported contract is the orchestrator image as `opencode` (1000:1000) and shipped agent images built on `Dockerfile_base` with the same UID/GID; an external profile image is accepted only as an operator-approved image compatible with this filesystem contract (a runtime compatibility requirement, not a verified guarantee; an incompatible image ends `worker_failed` or `activation_output_invalid`), and the pipeline schema gains no `user`/`uid`/`gid`. The environment values travel only inside the `POST /run` body (never in any process argv); the former `--env-from` argv-exposure risk of the CLI-mediated path is eliminated for pipeline v2 worker execution. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_docker_runtime.test.ts` (47 tests) covers the missing-profile factory rejection before side effects, snapshot immunity under source-map/profile mutation (user objects unfrozen), the projection battery (host-mode identity, run-root mismatch, missing/symlink/file daemon root, dev/ino root mismatch, all seven missing pairs named in the error, symlinked/FIFO/socket/swapped-kind pair substitutions, a symlinked parent with an external sentinel rejected by the realpath identity check, mutation of the projection object after factory, root paths absent from worker argv/env and the execution document, factory shape rejection, zero CLI calls on every failure), the exact session-create argv/env for both workspaces including the exact Execution three-root and Tool single-root policy (flags before the positional workspace), unique ids/tokens, launcher-mismatch delete for both session kinds plus the delete-failure regressions for both kinds (`cli_failure` observable, no "was deleted" claim, no tokens, exactly one delete attempt), exact pull argv/env, the exact `POST /run` body with fixed mount order and `read_only` only on inputs (request parity with the CLI 2.3.1, including the top-level field order and the sorted environment keys), authority separation (Tool bearer to the worker in the body, Execution bearer header-only, launcher credential absent), body/env secret canaries (secrets/config absent from any argv; prompt/input bodies absent from the body and env), the exact environment key set, the execution-document paths, the forged/spread/Proxy/cross-runtime/cross-activation handle battery with zero getter traps and zero CLI calls, forged prepared activations, tool-without-execution rejection, repeated-run rejection, memoized single-delete cleanup, separate Tool/Execution cleanups, pull-failure → `worker_failed` without run, timeout/nonzero/zero classification, per-state image/env/config, stable collision-safe env-from source names, and no absolute host paths in run argv; the policy battery adds the revisit index, mutation immunity of profile map/projection/activation objects over the policy argv, the state-id argv-injection rejection (hostile view + forged activation rejected before any CLI, trusted activation yielding exactly the canonical safe-id roots), daemon-visible absolute root values derived from the run-root projection, secret-free session-create argv (no bearer/credential/profile secret/config body), a session-create failure creating no handle/record/delete, a Tool create rejection leaving the Execution session cleanable exactly once, and the Tool-before-Execution cleanup order. `orchestrator/tests/launcher.test.ts` (12 tests) covers the transport layer itself: the byte-identical no-roots and empty-list argv, the exact repeatable flag order with the workspace last (never `--workspace`, no `--filesystem-entry`), a root path containing `=` staying one argv value, caller mutation isolation after the call, Proxy/throwing-getter capture behavior, invalid access, relative/empty/unclean/whitespace-padded/traversal paths, non-list/non-object shapes — every invalid shape with zero CLI calls — root values never entering env, and the typed create-failure contract. Still unwired: the default pipeline bundle; lifecycle/signals are wired through the production runner.

- Browser UI v2 manual lifecycle controls (implemented, production-wired): the UI extends the read-only surfaces with manual, boundary-driven run continuation over the existing versioned HTTP endpoints — no new endpoint, no schema version, no automatic policy, no reading of internal state documents. The single decision source is `GET /v1/runs/:runId`: `app.js` owns one inspect-to-action-model builder (`buildRunActionModel`) that maps the exact boundary union fail-closed — `running/in_flight` → observe-only, `running/resumable` → Resume, `running/plan_ready` with `continuation:"resume"` → Resume, with `continuation:"resume_plan"` → the resume-plan form (explicit operator-entered `stage_id` safe id and `initial_budget` positive safe integer; no default stage, no derivation from position/entry/template, no guessed budget, nothing persisted outside page memory), `waiting` → the declared actions in verbatim server order as the continue-stage form (`wait_index` from the boundary, non-editable; operator-entered positive safe integer `additional_iterations`) and the revise-task form (`wait_index` from the boundary; operator-entered `task_id`/`ref` safe ids; `root_id` a select over ONLY the bound project's `input_roots` from `GET /v1/projects`, with the run→project link taken exclusively from the binding-backed `GET /v1/runs` entry — a removed or `project_available:false` project disables revise-task fail-closed, no editable project id, no guessing), `succeeded`/`failed` → terminal/output projection only, and every unknown status/boundary/continuation/action or invalid shape fails closed into `unusable_boundary` with zero POST. One mutation request owner (`buildMutationRequest`) builds every mutating route path and exact snake_case body with exact own keys (`resume` `{}`; `resume-plan` `{stage_id, initial_budget}`; `continue-stage` `{wait_index, additional_iterations}`; `revise-task` `{wait_index, task_id, task {root_id, ref}}`); form values are read once at submit, validation completes before any fetch, the wait index can never come from a form value, and validation failures create zero network effects. Generic `respond` is never exposed: `continue_stage`/`revise_task` go only through their specialized intervention endpoints, any other declared action id fails closed. Responses are accepted endpoint-specifically (inspect → `run|error`, discovery → its own kind or `error`, mutation → `command_result|error`; a known but unsuitable kind is a contract error). A UI-wide mutation gate admits at most one mutating POST (all mutating controls disabled while it is in flight; double-click/second submit creates no second POST; GET discovery/inspect stay allowed; selecting another run does not cancel the mutator), and the shared mutation flow (`runMutationFlow`, one POST site) shows the outcome, performs exactly one authoritative inspect of the known run id, refreshes the runs discovery and never starts the next mutator automatically; `run_busy`/`invalid_state` are surfaced and never retried. Any network failure after a POST began means outcome unknown with the run id and the sent action kept visible and exactly one safe GET inspect — the POST is never retried, the request body never enters a URL or browser storage, and the UI asserts nothing about the effect until inspect confirms it. Async GET ordering uses generation semantics (`createGenerationGuard` for run-list pages; the still-selected-run rule for inspect rendering), so stale page responses after a Refresh and stale inspects after selecting another run are ignored; there is no POST cancellation and no unbounded background polling (the single automatic GET loop is the bounded read-only live observation of Browser UI v3 — see the next bullet). Tests: the v2 battery in `orchestrator/tests/ui_model.test.ts` (54 tests — the full boundary-to-control matrix, exact request bodies, endpoint-specific kinds, the gate, generation suppression, the mutation flow incl. the per-endpoint lost-response proofs, project/input-root authority and the extended source scans pinning one client/builder/action-model owner, no generic respond, no polling, no hardcoded stage/task/root identifiers), plus `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_ui_lifecycle.test.ts` (3 tests — the full lifecycle `run → plan_ready → resume-plan → waiting → continue-stage → waiting → revise-task → plan_ready → resume-plan → succeeded` driven through the real HTTP handlers, CLI subprocess and daemon with UI-built decisions and requests at every boundary, kernel `run_busy` authority under UI-built parallel requests, and zero-write discovery/inspect fingerprints with leakage checks). Still unwired: automatic stage/budget or wait-action selection, retries/backoff (the bounded read-only live observation is Browser UI v3 — see the next bullet), operation queue/202, events/log streaming, uploads or a task-body editor, delete/retention, auth/RBAC/TLS/remote bind, registry hot reload, and any Pipeline V2 reducer/controller/CLI or schema change.- Browser UI v3 bounded read-only live observation (implemented, production-wired): the run detail can repeat the existing read-only inspect GET of the one selected run automatically, driven by one pure scheduling owner (`createLiveObservationScheduler` in `app.js`) whose inputs are semantic events (run selected, inspect started/succeeded/failed/cancelled, timer fired, visibility changed, live toggle changed, mutation started/completed, disposed) and whose outputs are frozen declarative effects only (`schedule_timer` with the fixed `LIVE_OBSERVATION_POLL_DELAY_MS` = 2000, `cancel_timer`, `start_inspect`, `render_state`, `no_op`) — no second HTTP parser, no second action-model owner and no browser-side boundary validator: the scheduler consumes only the already validated `parseApiDocument`/inspect model (the snapshot is `status`, `boundary.kind`, `revision`). Polling is eligible exactly while the last applied authoritative inspect shows `status === "running"` with `boundary.kind === "in_flight"` and the tab is visible, the `Live updates` toggle (default enabled, page-memory only — no cookies/localStorage/query parameters) is on, no inspect of the run is in flight and no mutating POST is active; every other boundary (`resumable`/`plan_ready`/`waiting`/`succeeded`/`failed`/unusable), every inspect error, `run_not_found`, a project/binding error and every contract failure stop it permanently until an explicit user action (Refresh, re-enabling live updates, re-selecting the run) whose new authoritative inspect must again confirm `running/in_flight`. The period is a fixed 2000 ms measured from the completion of the previous successful inspect (never from the request start), implemented as one chained `setTimeout` behind the single `createSchedulerTimerAdapter` (the module's only setTimeout/clearTimeout sites; at most one pending timer; no `setInterval`); a slow GET never queues a second one, a stale timer callback is a no-op, and manual Refresh keeps the machine's single-flight semantics (`takeOverInspect` — the superseded in-flight response is dropped and never applied or scheduled from), while the one admission owner of every inspect GET is the module-level `createInspectTransport`: at most one unsettled inspect fetch exists at any instant — fulfil, reject and abort all settle it before anything new starts; a new desire supersedes the unsettled request (best-effort `AbortController.abort()`, which never frees the slot by itself) and coalesces as the single follow-up started exactly when the slot releases; `invalidate()` (before a mutating POST and a new-run submit) supersedes the unsettled request and withdraws a queued desire, and `dispose()` withdraws everything; every settled superseded response is dropped and never applied or scheduled from. Selecting another run cancels the timer and invalidates the outstanding response (it can never apply to the new run); hiding the document cancels the timer; returning to visibility triggers exactly one immediate inspect only when the last applied boundary was polling-eligible and never after an error; re-enabling live updates is an explicit user action that may also resume after an error, always through one inspect whose success re-confirms the boundary; dispose cancels the timer and invalidates everything. Mutations pause the observation: a mutating POST cancels the pending timer and invalidates the in-flight inspect (its response is dropped, the single-flight slot is released for the authoritative inspect), the live-updates toggle is untouched, polling is forbidden until the mutator completes, and the existing one authoritative v2 inspect then decides — `running/in_flight` restarts polling, any other boundary never does; `run_busy` is inspect-only and never retries the POST; the double-submit gate and the one-mutator rule are unchanged. The lost-response boundary stays: outcome unknown → exactly one recovery inspect → `running/in_flight` continues as GET-only polling, `run_not_found`/error/another boundary never starts it, and no polling result ever initiates a mutation. After a polling error the UI shows a safe `layer: reason` diagnostic, keeps manual Refresh, sets no new timer and performs no backoff/retry. The live-state line (`Live`/`Paused`/`Stopped after error`/`off` plus a browser-local refresh sequence number — presentation only, never a durable/API fact) is plain text outside the aria-live regions; announcements happen only for revision/boundary/status changes, polling errors and user-initiated pause/resume — an unchanged poll is silent and does not rebuild the detail DOM (focus and selection survive; the change key `liveObservationDocumentKey` covers run id, revision, status, boundary and outputs). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/ui_live_observation.test.ts` (32 tests — the full state-machine matrix including the single-flight takeover, the fake-clock timer adapter battery, the presentation helpers and the source scans), the single-flight transport battery in `orchestrator/tests/ui_inspect_transport.test.ts` (15 tests — the admission/coalescing matrix with deferred gates, the fulfil/reject/abort settlement matrix, the fail-closed desire validation and a 100-sequence stress battery), the real-wiring battery in `orchestrator/tests/ui_inspect_wiring.test.ts` (12 tests — manual takeover, selection change, the fixed period from completion, the mutation/recovery coalescing, `run_busy`, the lost POST and a 100-sequence stress battery through the real `app.js` wiring under a fake DOM) and the HTTP batteries in `orchestrator/tests/ui_inspect_http.test.ts` (3 tests — real deferred-response and real control-plane service fixtures proving at most one unsettled fetch across genuine HTTP round-trips, the superseded response never rendering and zero-write CLI-backed inspects under a synchronous takeover burst) and `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_ui_live.test.ts` (1 integration test — a fixture daemon holds the stage worker for eight honest seconds in `in_flight`, a continue-stage POST is lost through a client abort while the orchestration continues, the recovery inspect confirms `running/in_flight` and the machine-driven loop runs sequential non-overlapping GETs with zero-write proofs conditioned on an unchanged boundary plus an unconditional zero-write proof at the quiescent waiting boundary, until the first `waiting` boundary stops the scheduler with zero further GETs and zero mutating calls). Still unwired: polling beyond the one selected run (list aggregation, discovery sweeps), automatic mutations from any observation result, retries/backoff, durable worker-log streaming (the read-only lifecycle events feed is consumed by the Browser UI v4 timeline — see the next bullet), operation queue/202, and any HTTP schema or orchestrator CLI change.
- Browser UI v4 read-only lifecycle timeline (implemented, production-wired): the run detail shows the durable lifecycle events feed of the selected run in a `Lifecycle events` section over the existing `GET /v1/runs/:runId/events` — no new endpoint, no schema change, no CLI change and no mutation. One dedicated events contract owner in `app.js` (`parseEventsDocument`) accepts only `schema_version: 1` + `kind: "events"`, validates the exact own keys of the document and every event entry against the closed 28-kind vocabulary with the exact required reference fields per kind (`PIPELINE_V2_EVENT_KINDS`/`eventReferenceKeys` — the kinds are rendered verbatim, there is no second display-label set), checks `run_id` against the selected run, requires positive-safe-integer revisions/indices, rejects inherited/extra/missing keys and incompatible references, preserves the history order verbatim, and returns a frozen projection; the shared wire owner `requestJsonDocument` is the single `fetch` site, `requestApiJson` keeps rejecting the events kind for inspect/discovery/mutation, and `requestEventsJson` accepts only `events`/`error`/failed `command_result` for the events endpoint (the expected response kind stays bound to the endpoint). Cursor/pagination: the initial load at run selection is exactly `GET /v1/runs/<runId>/events?after=0&limit=100` (one URL builder `eventsRequestPath`, `EVENTS_PAGE_LIMIT` = 100); the merge rules are `after` exclusive, the first entry `after + 1`, strictly dense revisions, duplicate/gap/regression/out-of-order as contract errors, `next_after` equal to the last event's revision or null meaning caught up to the response revision, an empty page only at `after === revision`; the cursor moves only after a fully validated merge. Explicit actions: `Load more` (one click = one page, no auto-drain of a long history), `Reload timeline` (reset + repeat from after=0) and `Retry timeline` after a read/network/contract error; `invalid_cursor` stops the timeline fail-closed until an explicit Reload (no retry button, no auto-reset, no auto-retry). The meta line shows the detail revision (the last authoritative inspect), the events revision, the applied cursor and `caught up`/`more available`/`loading`/`stopped` — the detail and the event snapshot may briefly diverge under a concurrent append and reconcile at the next inspect. The inspect→tail rule: after every applied authoritative inspect (manual, scheduled live poll, mutation recovery) the timeline fires at most one tail `events?after=<cursor>` exactly when it is caught up, not stopped and behind the inspect revision (the cursor-0 initial load is the same rule at selection time); a partial page is continued only through `Load more`; there is no second scheduler and no new timer — the tail reuses the existing inspect cadence, and an events response never triggers an inspect, a recursive tail or a mutation and never affects the live-inspect polling eligibility. Single-flight: the events transport (`createEventsTransport`) admits at most one unsettled events GET; a repeated request is refused (not coalesced), a selection change and `dispose()` invalidate the active/pending result, a late response of a previous run never applies, aborts are best-effort and never free the slot themselves, and there are no retries/backoff. Error isolation: a timeline error renders only in the timeline region as a safe `layer: reason`/`contract: reason` diagnostic (an orchestrator failure presents as `orchestrator: <reason>`), never breaks the inspect detail, never disables the mutation controls and never POSTs; the automatic tail stops until an explicit user action. Rendering: a table with `Revision`/`Kind`/`References` columns (the kind verbatim; only present references in a fixed canonical order), append-only rows through `createElement`/`textContent` (focus and scroll survive every page), `aria-live` announcements only for added counts/caught-up/timeline errors (no per-row announcements), no forced auto-scroll, and no timestamps, interpretation, paths, contents or worker logs in any row. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/ui_events_model.test.ts` (19 tests — the parser/merge/stop/summary/announcement battery and the source scans), `orchestrator/tests/ui_events_transport.test.ts` (10 tests — admission/refusal/invalidate/abort/settlement and a 100-sequence stress battery), `orchestrator/tests/ui_events_wiring.test.ts` (14 tests — initial page, inspect-driven tails, manual pagination, `invalid_cursor` fail-closed, selection/dispose staleness, mutation recovery and append-only rendering through the real `app.js` wiring), `orchestrator/tests/ui_model.test.ts` (the extended export surface and the fail-closed boundary) and the HTTP integration `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_ui_events.test.ts` (the full 66-event lifecycle through the real endpoint with byte-exact 4×20 pagination and the 28-kind union via three synthetic coverage runs, caught-up/`invalid_cursor` shapes, unbound/missing/removed-project authority failures, a concurrent append between pages through the real sink, zero-write fingerprints, and the wiring lifecycle over the real service + CLI + fixture daemon: selection loads the first page, a lost resume-plan POST recovers through one inspect whose tails follow the in-flight worker, the waiting boundary catches up without further GETs, a timeline error never POSTs and re-selection recovers). Still unwired: events filtering/search/export, automatic mutations from any observation result, and any HTTP schema or orchestrator CLI change; the durable worker-log capture adds no event and stays invisible to this feed (see the worker-log bullet).
- Browser UI v5 bounded read-only worker-log viewer (implemented, production-wired; corrective semantics): the run detail gains a manual, pagination-only `Worker logs` section consuming ONLY the two existing versioned logs endpoints (`GET /v1/runs/:runId/logs` and `GET /v1/runs/:runId/logs/:executionIndex?offset&limit`) — no new endpoint, no schema change, no CLI change and no mutation. One pure model owner in `app.js` (`createWorkerLogViewer` returning a closed state machine `transitionToLogsView`/`fetchLogsList`/`inspectApplied`/`selectExecution`/`fetchChunk`/`retryLastChunkRequest` over the frozen state shape `{runId, snapshotRevision, logs, selectedExecutionIndex, content, requestState, error}` where `content` carries the accumulated decoded `text`) whose transitions accept only documents already validated by the dedicated logs endpoint parser chain (`worker_logs` metadata union and `worker_log` status/chunk shapes — exact own keys, run binding, lowercase-SHA-256 digests, positive-safe-integer indexes, and the raw-byte cursor coherence: the decoded chunk never exceeds `byte_count - offset`, `next_offset` is exactly the end of the slice whenever a tail exists and null only on the terminal chunk), never raw values or URLs; `parseApiDocument` (the general inspect/discovery/mutation client) still rejects both logs kinds, and the durable artifact envelope (`payload_base64`, `workerlog.json`) never enters the browser layer. A bounded single-flight logs transport (`createLogsTransport`) keeps exactly one unsettled logs GET and arbitrates two priority kinds: an automatic metadata desire never cancels an in-flight manual content request (it queues as the single coalesced follow-up), a content desire supersedes an active metadata refresh, equal kinds supersede and coalesce to the latest; `invalidate()`/`dispose()` withdraw everything, and there is no auto-retry, no retry loop and no streaming. The metadata list follows the applied authoritative inspects automatically: the first successful inspect of the selected run issues exactly one list GET, every later applied inspect issues one only when its revision is strictly greater than the last requested one, and a failed application never re-triggers for the same revision; the automatic metadata path never loads payload bytes and an explicit `Load logs` press stays a safe manual refresh. The viewer carries NO boundary classifier: after ANY successful authoritative inspect a captured/truncated chunk loads on the explicit `Load log` press of the selected execution on every run boundary, including succeeded, failed and resumable runs (the former capture gate was removed — the read-only viewer has no boundary vocabulary, no mutation/action-model dependency and no `capture_gate_refused` state). The immutable binding of a loaded execution is checked on every metadata application: an unchanged binding preserves the loaded content, the selection and the decoder state; a changed binding fails closed as the typed `viewer: invalid_log_binding` state instead of silently mixing bytes from two different artifacts. Stale responses of another run or execution are dropped before they become state (run-change invalidation plus run/execution guards in every transition); lost GETs settle as typed `transport: network_error` failures with exactly one manual Retry click per retry; selecting another run discards the viewer state and the wire. The one incremental UTF-8 decoder owner (`createLogChunkDecoder`): exactly one non-fatal decoder per selected execution, every non-final chunk decoded with `{stream: true}` and the terminal chunk with `{stream: false}`, so multi-byte sequences split across a chunk boundary decode correctly and a retried offset can never duplicate already applied text. The safe render contract: the accumulated decoded text renders through `textContent` only — NUL bytes, ANSI escapes, invalid UTF-8 sequences and payloads without a trailing newline can never become markup; no payload fragment ever enters a diagnostic, storage or URL; the section declares its read-only, secret-bearing, unredacted nature (no download, export, search, redaction or retention). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/ui_worker_logs.test.ts` (45 tests — the parser/URL-builder/transport-arbitration/viewer/decoder/render-contract battery plus the source scans), `orchestrator/tests/ui_worker_logs_wiring.test.ts` (20 tests — the inspect-driven automatic metadata refresh, terminal log access on every boundary, the binding-change fail-closed battery, the status-hint union, Retry, staleness and the safe render through the real `app.js` wiring under the fake DOM), `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_ui_worker_logs.test.ts` (7 tests — the real logs endpoints through the real service over runs with real schema-v9 artifacts: captured/truncated/pending/unavailable, the harness-driven flows on running and failed boundaries, the ghost-run failed command result, the query grammar, zero-write fingerprints and the leakage scan), plus the updated pins in `tests/ui_model.test.ts` (the general client still rejects the logs kinds; the export surface), `tests/ui_live_observation.test.ts` (three abort-controller owners; the inspect-transport region ends at the v5 header) and `tests/pipeline_v2_worker_log.test.ts` (the browser layer never touches the artifact envelope or filename). Still unwired: automatic payload loading (the metadata follows inspects; chunks stay explicit), payload streaming, download/export, search, redaction and retention.
- Browser UI v6 read-only Project changes panel (implemented, production-wired): the run detail gains a standalone `Project changes` section consuming ONLY the existing versioned changes endpoint (`GET /v1/runs/:runId/changes`) — no new endpoint, no schema change, no CLI change and no mutation; it is the read-only view of the **run workspace changes relative to the durable project baseline**, never a proven worker-authorship claim, and the registered operator project is never modified. One dedicated changes contract owner in `app.js` (`parseProjectChangesDocument`) accepts only `schema_version: 1` + `kind: "project_changes"` with exact own keys at every level: the closed 7-kind change vocabulary, the closed 3-relation source vocabulary, the exact node union (`regular_file` with `mode`/`byte_count`/`sha256` and the exact hardlink metadata of the actual API — `hardlink_group` travels exactly with `multiple_links` — plus `directory` and verbatim `symlink` targets), the per-entry variant coherence (`added`/`deleted` null-side rules, content kinds regular_file-only, `mode_modified` mode-bearing and kind-stable, `symlink_target_modified` symlink on both sides, `type_changed` different kinds), strictly increasing indexes, unique relative paths (no NUL, no absolute, no empty/`.`/`..` component), non-negative coherent summary counts (kind counts and relation counts each sum to `total`, `total >= page length`), a 64-hex observation and a positive-safe revision; unknown versions/kinds/shapes are never guessed, and the general `parseApiDocument` keeps rejecting the changes kind fail-closed for every other endpoint. The frozen state model (`createProjectChangesState` → `{runId, inspectRevision, observation, summary, changes, nextAfter, requestState, presentationState, error}`) carries the request states `idle|loading_initial|loading_more|reloading|ready|stale|error` and the derived presentations `unavailable|no_changes|pending|already_present|mixed|diverged` (`already_present` is deliberately NOT named `applied` — the API never claims how bytes arrived in the operator project). The one pure page-merge owner (`applyProjectChangesPage`) replaces the snapshot only after full validation: an initial page must start at index 1 (or be empty exactly when `total === 0`) and binds the run id; a continuation requires the requested `after` to equal the accepted entry count, the exact same observation, the same snapshot revision, dense indexes from the previous tail, no repeated paths across pages, a deep-equal summary and append-only entries; `next_after` null only on the fully loaded last page (loaded === total), non-null equal to the merged count, never regressing, and loaded never exceeding total — every violation is a typed UI contract failure that never mutates the state and never applies a partial page. The automatic path (`viewer.inspectApplied`) is exactly ONE `GET /v1/runs/<id>/changes?after=0&limit=100` per applied authoritative terminal inspect (status `succeeded`/`failed` — the stable external terminal mapping of the inspect model, not a phase ladder) whose revision was not yet requested; running/plan-ready/waiting/resumable boundaries, inspect failures and re-inspects of the same revision issue nothing, a changed terminal revision issues exactly one new initial request (the previous snapshot stays visible until a fully valid page replaces it), and events/live polling never trigger changes GETs. Explicit actions only: `Reload changes` (one after=0 request without an observation, keeping the old snapshot visible as `reloading`; hidden only after a contract-invalid document) and `Load more` (only when ready with a continuation; sends `after=<nextAfter>&limit=100&observation=<current>`; refused while a request is in flight). The URL builder (`projectChangesRequestPath`) owns the two exact forms and never puts a project path, change path, symlink target, file digest, node metadata, project id or run root in a URL. The kind-bound client `requestProjectChangesJson` reuses the one bounded JSON response primitive and accepts only the changes document of the selected run plus the endpoint's `error`/failed-`command_result` envelopes; the single-flight transport `createProjectChangesTransport` keeps at most one unsettled changes GET (an initial/reload supersedes and coalesces; a continuation while busy is declined without a second fetch; selection change and `dispose` invalidate; aborts are best-effort and never free the slot; no timer, polling or retry loop of any kind). `stale_observation` (the HTTP 409 envelope) keeps the loaded coherent snapshot, shows `Stale. The run workspace or registered project changed after this view was created. Reload the changes.` (never attributing the change to the worker), hides Load more and offers only the explicit Reload; `changes_not_ready`/`artifact_missing`/`artifact_modified` render a safe `layer: reason` failure without hiding the terminal execution outcome and without any automatic repair; network failures settle as `transport: network_error` with manual Reload only; contract/parser failures fail closed and render no raw document. The DOM panel shows the snapshot revision, the presentation status, the loaded/total count, the full summary counts line and one row per change (path, change kind, source relation, baseline metadata, result metadata) — modes in octal, byte counts, the full SHA-256 as text, hardlink indicators, directory modes and verbatim symlink targets, all exclusively through `createElement`/`textContent` (no innerHTML, no clickable paths, no automatic links, no ANSI/markdown interpretation, no code execution, no payload fetch, no localStorage/cookies); the panel is fully isolated from the inspect detail, live observation, timeline, worker logs, mutation controls and fresh-run form (a changes failure never POSTs, never calls inspect/events/logs and never affects the mutation gate or polling eligibility; a run selection change resets the viewer and drops a late response of the previous run). No content contract in the v6 layer itself: the changes panel fetches no `data_base64` and has no content endpoint, `/diff`, `/apply`, File System Access API, clipboard/copy or download route; digests and metadata are not a content preview — the bounded read-only content preview is the separate Browser UI v7 panel (next bullet), and the changes owner itself still carries none of it. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/ui_project_changes.test.ts` (15 tests — the exact parser schemas, node variants and coherence matrix, summary coherence, presentation derivation, the full page-merge battery including duplicate/gap/regression/observation mismatch, the URL builder and the source scans), `orchestrator/tests/ui_project_changes_transport.test.ts` (7 tests — the single-flight matrix with the supersede/decline arbitration, abort-ignoring fetches, invalidate/dispose, stale fulfilment/rejection and a 100-sequence stress), `orchestrator/tests/ui_project_changes_wiring.test.ts` (18 tests — the automatic request matrix through the real `app.js` wiring under the fake DOM, pagination, stale recovery, selection changes, hostile path/symlink-target rendering, zero POSTs and failure isolation), `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_ui_project_changes.test.ts` (5 tests over the real HTTP service + production CLI subprocess + durable schema-v10 runs with a fixture daemon whose stage workers write into the shared project: the succeeded run's automatic one-shot GET with the exact added/modified projection and zero side effects, the 153-change paginated run with the stale-observation battery and the explicit reload, the failed terminal run's partial changes, the tampered-provenance refusal with the terminal outcome still visible, and the restart identity with zero writes), plus the updated pins in `tests/ui_model.test.ts` (the extended export surface), `tests/ui_live_observation.test.ts` (four abort-controller owners), `tests/ui_worker_logs.test.ts` (the extended inspectApplied count) and `tests/pipeline_v2_project_changes.test.ts` (the v6 panel pins replacing the unwired-UI pins). Still unwired: any Apply or write path, polling/automatic retries, and any HTTP schema or orchestrator CLI change.
- Browser UI v7 bounded read-only Project Change Content viewer (implemented, production-wired): the run detail gains a manual, per-entry `Review content` action in the Project changes table and one inline review panel consuming ONLY the existing versioned content endpoint (`GET /v1/runs/:runId/changes/:changeIndex/content`) — no new endpoint, no schema change, no CLI change, no mutation and no automatic content reading. Dedicated model owners in `app.js`: the one URL builder `projectChangeContentRequestPath` (only run id, change index, fixed side, observation identity and raw-byte cursor travel in the URL — never a project path, change path, digest, node metadata, project id or run root), the dedicated parser `parseProjectChangeContentDocument(document, expected)` (exact own keys per status variant, the closed status/side/unavailable-reason vocabularies, canonical safe integers, lowercase SHA-256, strict base64 with decoded-length verification, full byteCount/offset/nextOffset coherence — a terminal empty chunk only at the exact end, `next_offset === offset + decoded length` and below `byte_count` — `implicit_empty` carrying no synthetic digest, `metadata_only`/`unavailable` carrying no content or cursor keys, and the exact response binding to the requested run id/observation/change index/side), the endpoint classifier accepting the content kind only on the content endpoint plus the endpoint's `error`/failed-`command_result` envelopes (the general `parseApiDocument` keeps rejecting `project_change_content`), and the kind-bound client `requestProjectChangeContentJson` over the one bounded JSON response primitive. Constants: `CONTENT_PREVIEW_CHUNK_LIMIT` 65 536, `CONTENT_PREVIEW_MAX_BYTES` 262 144 retained raw bytes per side, `CONTENT_HEX_DISPLAY_BYTES` 4096 rendered hex bytes, `DIFF_MAX_LINES_PER_SIDE` 2000, `DIFF_MAX_WORK_CELLS` 250 000. The single-flight transport `createProjectChangeContentTransport` keeps at most one unsettled content GET (a new explicit Review supersedes and coalesces to the last desire; a chunk continuation while busy is declined without a second fetch; invalidate/dispose withdraw everything; superseded fulfill/reject never applies; aborts are best-effort and never free the slot themselves; no timer, polling or retry loop). The closed viewer state machine `createProjectChangeContentViewer` is the single state owner: `review(entry)` is the ONLY path that starts content GETs — up to two sequential requests (baseline, then result) for one explicit user action, each bound to the current run/observation/change index/side — and every response is verified against the selected change entry's metadata (regular-file digest and byte count, the exact side node, status/node coherence) and fails closed with zero rendered bytes on any mismatch. Side states explicitly distinguish `unrequested|loading|available|implicit_empty|metadata_only|unavailable|error`; an `available` side accumulates its chunks at the raw-byte cursor (loadedBytes) and honestly reports `truncated preview` once the 262 144-byte cap is reached, after which further Load more is refused even though the file is longer. A `stale_observation` response discards the loaded bytes of BOTH sides, marks the review stale and offers only the explicit Project Changes reload (no automatic after=0). Run/observation changes, Reload of the changes metadata, review close and run selection invalidate the transport, make active responses superseded and clear the loaded secret-bearing bytes; a late response never applies to a new row or observation. Chunk continuations and retries stay explicit clicks (retry only after a transport-layer failure, re-requesting the same offset); a declined continuation never strands the side in loading. Presentation: raw bytes are the source of truth — `decodePreviewText` classifies each side (NUL or invalid UTF-8 → binary; an incomplete trailing sequence is invalid only for a complete file, tolerated and excluded in an unfinished preview; ANSI and markup-looking text stay plain text) and the render path is `textContent` only; the binary mode is the bounded hex view `hexPreviewRows` (fixed raw-byte offsets, hex pairs, safe ASCII column, display-limited to the first 4096 retained bytes). The bounded client-side diff `computeBoundedLineDiff` (one deterministic owner, no dependency): it runs only when both sides are fully loaded, uncapped, UTF-8 text or an explicit implicit-empty side, with the observation/index/run binding intact; added/deleted implicit-empty sides participate as honest empty sides; the missing final newline participates in the comparison key; identical content short-circuits (`Content identical; metadata changed.` for mode-only changes); over 2000 lines per side or an LCS table above 250 000 cells refuses as a typed `diff_too_large`-class reason (`too_many_lines`/`work_budget`) with no hang and no partial misleading diff; rows render through `textContent` with `+ `/`- `/context prefixes and the `\ No newline at end of file` marker. The DOM panel shows path, change kind, observation, per-side status, byte counts, digests, loaded/total, full/truncated preview, the human unavailable text (no internal diagnostics echo) and the buttons Load more baseline/result (only when allowed), Retry (only after a recoverable network failure), Close and Reload Project Changes (only when stale), always with the fixed warning that content may contain secrets, is not redacted, and that the registered project is not modified; no Copy button exists in this increment. The panel is fully isolated from inspect/live observation/timeline/worker logs/mutation controls: a content failure never POSTs, never calls other endpoints and never changes the revision or observation, and the content viewer never initiates a changes reload itself. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/ui_project_change_content.test.ts` (25 tests — the parser battery over all four statuses with exact own keys/unknown-key/version/kind/status/reason refusals, the exact response binding, cursor/base64/byte-count coherence, the no-fake-digest and no-content-on-metadata rules, hostile-string canaries, the URL builder, the text/binary classification with the chunk-boundary code point and the unfinished-preview tail, the hex view, the full bounded-diff battery with the no-EOL distinction, empty sides, identical content, line-limit and work-budget refusals
