and the pinned constants), `orchestrator/tests/ui_project_change_content_transport.test.ts` (10 tests — the single-flight matrix, supersede/coalesce to the last desire, the declined continuation, abort-ignoring fetches, invalidate/dispose, dropped stale fulfillments/rejections, the exactly-once lifecycle and the 100-sequence stress at max active GET 1), `orchestrator/tests/ui_project_change_content_viewer.test.ts` (19 tests — zero GET before Review, the exact sequential baseline/result requests, added/deleted implicit-empty, unavailable baseline, metadata-only directory/symlink, mode-only identical content, digest/byte-count/binding mismatches failing closed, the raw-byte cursor with prefix accumulation, the preview cap, the declined continuation, retry-only-by-click, stale discarding both sides, observation changes clearing bytes, selection A→B dropping late A and close/run-selection resets), `orchestrator/tests/ui_project_change_content_wiring.test.ts` (12 tests through the real `app.js` under the fake DOM — zero content GETs on the terminal inspect/Reload, the exact request matrix, text/hex/diff/mode-only rendering, the cap battery, stale + Reload-without-content, retry-only-by-click, supersede and failure isolation with zero POSTs), `orchestrator/tests/control_plane_ui_project_change_content.test.ts` (14 tests over the real HTTP service + registry + binding store + CLI subprocess + schema-v10 fixtures with a CONTENT-mode daemon fixture: modified/added-binary/deleted/symlink/directory/mode-only/large-file changes, byte-exact chunk reassembly against a one-shot full read, the stale mid-file continuation after an operator change, the diverged baseline unavailability, the service-restart determinism, the invalid cursor 400, the provenance-tamper refusal, the zero-write fingerprints and the leakage scan), plus the updated pins in `tests/ui_model.test.ts` (the extended export surface; the general client still rejects the content kind), `tests/ui_project_changes.test.ts` (the v6 region boundary ending at the v7 marker), `tests/ui_live_observation.test.ts` (five abort-controller owners), `tests/ui_worker_logs.test.ts` (the logs region boundary), `tests/ui_project_changes_wiring.test.ts` (the six-column rows) and `tests/control_plane_ui_project_changes.test.ts` (the six-column rows). Still unwired: any Apply or write path, download/export, content redaction policy, final-workspace durable sealing, payload streaming beyond the retained bounds, and any HTTP schema or orchestrator CLI change.
- Versioned read-only Project Change Content API (implemented, wired into the CLI as `orchestrator project-change-content`, the control-plane routing/client and the loopback HTTP service; consumed by the Browser UI v7 review panel): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_project_change_content.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_project_change_content_internal.ts` (internal core: the pure side-availability resolver and the single-pass verified reader) + `pipeline_v2_project_stream_internal.ts` (the ONE streaming regular-file observation owner) serves one bounded raw-byte slice of one side of one verified Project Changes entry for one finalized run. Authority is never a path: the request names run id, change index, side, observation, offset and limit; the observed path is derived exclusively from the freshly recomputed verified change entry (full observation recompute → exact observation match → index resolve → side resolution → derived trusted path → one verified stream). Four distinct success statuses — `available` (regular-file side, bytes proven against the expected digest, bounded raw-byte slice in `dataBase64`), `implicit_empty` (the baseline of an added path / the result of a deleted path: a full logical empty diff side with `byteCount: 0`, never an error and never a fabricated digest), `metadata_only` (directory/symlink side; metadata already lives in the change node; a symlink is never dereferenced — `O_NOFOLLOW` open ⇒ `ELOOP`) and `unavailable` (baseline regular-file bytes no longer provable; the ONLY reason is `source_no_longer_matches_baseline`) — with exact own keys per variant and no content/cursor keys on the non-`available` statuses. Baseline availability is conditional by construction: `matches_baseline` reads the current operator project (digest re-verified), a mode-only regular-file change may serve BOTH sides from the verified result workspace bytes because the classifier proves digest equality (independent of the operator relation), everything else is `unavailable`; diverged third-party bytes are never served. Streaming observation owner: one `O_NOFOLLOW` open, fstat before, one sequential 128 KiB-chunk pass from offset 0 to EOF hashing the actual stream while an optional bounded slice (≤ the requested limit) is copied from that same stream, fstat after with the full identity comparison (dev/ino/kind/mode/size/nlink/mtime/ctime), and the full digest/byte-count check against the expected node — the result (including captured bytes) leaves the owner only after every check passed; a slice can therefore never be paired with a digest of different bytes, which closes the audited V1 A→B→A hole (slice from B accepted by a separate rehash of A) by construction — there is no second read. The Project Changes scanner now observes every regular file through the same streaming owner (memory bounded by one 128 KiB chunk plus metadata; no whole-file buffer, no all-chunk concat), keeping the observation digest and every wire document byte-identical for unchanged trees; the copy-time provenance traversal is untouched (no second source scan, no schema change). Cursor contract (raw bytes): offset default 0; `offset > byte_count` → `invalid_cursor`; `offset == byte_count` → empty data + `nextOffset: null`; `nextOffset = offset + returnedByteCount` only when a tail exists; `implicit_empty`/`metadata_only`/`unavailable` serve offset 0 only; default limit 65 536, maximum 262 144 raw bytes (both verified against the 4 MB subprocess stdout limit and the 1 MiB UI document bound with ≥11×/3× headroom). Per-request cost is honest O(tree + file): every content request recomputes the FULL Project Changes observation (both trees) and streams the requested file once. TOCTOU classification: a tree change between the metadata page and the content request is `stale_observation` before any byte; an instability during the recompute scan or during the target stream is `workspace_unstable` (run workspace) / `project_unstable` (operator project) with zero bytes; the provenance manifest keeps `artifact_missing`/`artifact_modified`. The residual trusted-writer window is the same millisecond-granularity class the provenance scanner documents (a same-ms same-size in-place write between the scan read and the stream open defeats the ctime check; the stream digest still binds the served slice to the bytes actually read). The content is secret-bearing and unredacted by design: bytes travel ONLY in `dataBase64` and never in diagnostics, errors, headers or URLs; paths never enter content documents; the caller never controls a path. Zero-write semantics: no mutation lock, no project lock, no reducer/store, no Docker Helper/Launcher/Session, no temp file, no repair; content/mode/kind fingerprints are byte-identical across reads, and atime is explicitly OUTSIDE the zero-write guarantee (reads may move it under the kernel's read policy; fingerprints exclude it and nothing restores it). CLI: `orchestrator project-change-content --run-id SAFE_ID --config-root ABS --project ABS --change-index N --side baseline|result --observation SHA256 [--offset N] [--limit N] [--json]` (both flag forms; strict parser: duplicates/unknowns/positionals/empty exit 2 with empty stdout; canonical decimals; observation 64-hex exact; every auth/helper/internal/state-root flag rejected; the CLI success document carries `runRoot` — the control-plane client drops it; failures the existing Project Changes 14-reason envelope, no new failure vocabulary). Control-plane: the closed `OrchestratorCommand` kind `project_change_content {runId, projectPath, changeIndex, side, observation, offset?, limit?}` routes to the exact argv with the project path exclusively from the bound frozen registry project; the subprocess client parses the status-dispatched exact own-key shapes (all four statuses, strict cursor/value validation, `runRoot` dropped, only `dataBase64` carries bytes, exit-code agreement, stderr never classified). HTTP: `GET /v1/runs/:runId/changes/:changeIndex/content?side=...&observation=HEX[&offset=N&limit=N]` flows the same authority chain; strict query parser (side/observation required, canonical decimals, bounds, no percent-encoding/bare keys/duplicates/unknowns); `invalid_cursor` → request 400, `stale_observation` → request 409, `binding_not_found` → 404, removed project → 409, every other correctly received content failure → failed command result at 200, transport → 502, all success statuses 200 with `Cache-Control: no-store`, no Range semantics, no content disposition, GET-only, body forbidden, Host/Origin gates unchanged. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_project_change_content.test.ts` (38 tests — the full availability matrix, cursor boundaries, 256 KiB multi-chunk reassembly, binary/NUL/ANSI/no-EOL/chunk-boundary canaries with bytes only in `dataBase64`, stale/invalid-cursor binding, the A→B→A closed-hole proof, deterministic IO-seam stress (same-size overwrite inside/outside the captured range, truncate, inode replacement, mode change, symlink substitution, one-byte reads, premature zero-byte read, mid-stream read error, a 3 MB file streamed in bounded chunks), zero-write fingerprints, vocabulary pins, the CLI parser/routing/client batteries and the real-CLI subprocess E2E with the dropped run root), the HTTP battery in `tests/control_plane_http_server.test.ts` (route grammar, all four snake_case shapes, the strict query matrix, 400/409/200/404/409/502 mapping, no-store, body/Host/Origin gates, leakage scan), the dispatcher battery in `tests/cli_main.test.ts`, the fixture-child scenarios in `tests/control_plane_child.ts`, and the UI fail-closed pin in `tests/ui_model.test.ts` (the general client keeps rejecting `kind: "project_change_content"`). Still unwired: Apply, writes to the operator project, final-workspace durable sealing, and any state schema change.
- Durable Project Apply preparation package (implemented, foundation layer, no public surface yet): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_project_apply.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_project_apply_internal.ts` (core) turn one reviewed, finalized pipeline v2 run plus one exact Project Changes observation into a fully self-contained prepared operation package — an immutable canonical plan, sealed payload blobs for every future regular-file publication and a crash-safe revisioned operation journal — **without writing a single byte into the registered project**. A prepared package does NOT mean applied; the next increment owns the per-path filesystem writer, the project lock and recovery on top of it. Authority: the facade takes only trusted internal parameters (`applyStoreRoot`, caller-owned `applyId` safe id, `runId`, `projectId`, the canonical `projectRoot` for the current read-only observation, the requested `observation` digest, the deployment `stateRoot`); the stored package binds the project through an opaque root-binding digest over the canonical root and its `dev`/`ino` (`pipeline-v2-project-apply-root-binding\0`), the canonical path is never persisted and recovery must re-resolve the root through `run binding → frozen registry project → canonical project root` and re-verify the binding before any future use. Layout: `<applyStoreRoot>/operations/<applyId>/` (0700 tree) with the immutable canonical `plan.json` (0600), `payloads/` (0700) of immutable blobs `${payload_index}.payload` (0600) and the revisioned `state.json` (0600) through the shared `RunSnapshotStore` substrate (now with an optional `pathLayout` override). The single streaming immutable-blob publication owner `pipeline_v2_immutable_blob_internal.ts` reuses — never duplicates — the immutable-document substrate protocol: exclusive owned temp 0600 with identity fixation, per-chunk write-all at a running file position, file fsync, exclusive `link()` publication, ownership-checked temp unlink, parent fsync, `EEXIST` adoption only on exact digest/byte-count match, `durability_unknown` with a frozen candidate, and the read side (real non-symlink regular file mode 0600, one bounded-chunk pass whose digest must equal the expectation). The immutable plan (`pipeline_v2_apply_plan_internal.ts`, one schema/digest owner, digest over the closed domain `pipeline-v2-project-apply-plan\0` plus the canonical JSON, no self-reference) carries: schema version, kind, apply/run/project ids, the root-binding digest, observation, terminal revision, the provenance binding, the policy version, ordered steps, payload bindings, total payload byte count — and never an absolute project path, run root, apply-store root, operation/session ids, credentials, file contents, `dev`/`ino`, timestamps or diagnostics. The low-level step model decouples execution progress from the change list: every step carries a dense 1..N `step_index` (the journal progress cursor), its `change_index` reference (never a cursor), a safe relative path, one closed kind of `remove_leaf | remove_directory | create_directory | publish_regular_file | publish_symlink | set_mode`, the exact `expected_before`/`desired_after` node states and the payload binding; one path carries several steps only for an explicit type substitution (exactly one removal then one creation — crash positions are unambiguous), a rename stays exactly one deletion plus one addition and case-only changes get no rename semantics. The deterministic apply-all planner (`pipeline_v2_apply_planner_internal.ts`) consumes exactly the production observation (`observeProjectChangesTreesWithIo`, the changes core extended to also return the raw observed trees and the durable baseline — no second scanner): preflight load durable run → terminal status only → provenance load + binding verification (inside the shared load chain) → full observation recompute → exact observation match → every affected path must match its baseline or result node (`diverged_project` otherwise) → `matches_result` becomes a durable no-op recorded with its expected-after state → plan-level projected-state simulation (a `file→directory` substitution with added children is judged against each path's own current object, and the final projected tree on every affected path must equal the reviewed result) → directory containment (every operator descendant of a removed/replaced directory must be covered by the plan's own removals; any operator-only descendant is a `diverged_project` conflict — no blind recursive delete, every removal is an explicit per-path step) → hardlink policy (a reviewed result or touched baseline with a hardlink group is `unsupported_metadata`; hardlink recreation is out of v1 scope) → deterministic ordering (removals deepest-first, directory creations shallowest-first, regular-file publications, symlink publications, mode-only steps last; lexical within a phase; identical verified input yields the byte-identical canonical plan). The single metadata probe owner `pipeline_v2_apply_metadata_probe_internal.ts` (read-only libc FFI `llistxattr`/`lgetxattr` over the proven `dlopen` + `__errno_location` + `memcpy` discipline) runs before the plan over every touched object, every existing ancestor directory and every desired-after workspace object, fail-closed: setuid/setgid/sticky, ownership the orchestrator could not reproduce (uid != euid, gid outside the supplementary groups), more than one hard link on a touched regular file, ANY listed xattr (user/trusted/security/…) and present POSIX ACLs (`system.posix_acl_access`, `system.posix_acl_default`) are `unsupported_metadata`; FIFO/socket/device is `unsupported_project_entry`; an unreachable object is `project_unstable`/`workspace_unstable`. Timestamps, sparse layout and reflink/compression layout are deliberately NOT part of the logical project contract (documented physical properties, not silently lost claims). Payload sealing: for every `publish_regular_file` the core opens the result file through the single streaming observation owner with a new `sink` capability — `O_NOFOLLOW`, fstat before, one sequential 128 KiB-chunk pass computing the content digest while streaming every chunk exactly once into the owned immutable payload temp, fstat after with the full identity check, digest/byte-count match against the plan's content contract, fsync, immutable publication, parent fsync, read-back verification at package completeness; no whole-file buffer, no slice-then-rehash, no bytes from the browser/API, no publication before the source is fully verified, no payload without a plan binding; symlink targets are stored canonical in the plan (no payload) and a mode-only step creates no payload. After the prepared marker the run workspace can change or disappear — the loader restores every desired regular-file byte from the sealed payloads alone. The journal (`pipeline_v2_apply_state_internal.ts`) is revisioned and crash-safe with the v1 statuses exactly `preparing | prepared | failed` (a future writer extends the vocabulary with its own version bump), binding apply id, run id, project id, observation, terminal revision, root binding, plan digest (plus step/payload/byte-count projections present exactly when the digest is), sealed payload count, status and a closed content-free failure reason; `prepared` is written only after plan and every payload were published, re-read and verified, completeness confirmed and directory fsyncs completed (or a `durability_unknown` adopted). Idempotency: the apply id is the caller-owned identity — the first call creates it, an exact retry continues `preparing` or returns the byte-identical prepared package (no journal growth), a retry with a different run/project/observation/root binding is a typed `identity_conflict` refused BEFORE the other run is even looked up, concurrent identical calls converge through store create/adoption and the `isAdvance` reconciliation, foreign bytes under the operation path are never overwritten (exact-artifact adoption only after digest/shape verification; the substrate's own publication-temp orphans are tolerated by the loader, foreign-named artifacts are `artifact_modified`). The crash matrix P0–P17 (before identity … after prepared) is proven by permanent fault-injection tests: zero project writes in every window, retries execute only the missing durable suffix, owned temps are cleaned or safely adopted, published artifacts keep inode/mode/bytes across adoption, a fresh components instance finishes the preparation and a failed package never looks prepared. The read-only loader verifies operation layout/modes, exact state schema, canonical plan bytes, plan digest and identity binding, dense indexes, valid change references, the closed step vocabulary, step-order coherence, safe relative paths, payload existence/type/mode/absence of symlinks, exact byte counts/digests, the absence of extra payload artifacts and prepared completeness (`artifact_missing`/`artifact_modified`, content-free diagnostics, never repairs, never needs the run workspace, never uses a stored path as authority, never starts a worker/helper/session). Failure vocabulary: `invalid_options | run_not_found | run_not_terminal | run_state_invalid | pipeline_mismatch | artifact_missing | artifact_modified | stale_observation | diverged_project | unsupported_metadata | unsupported_project_entry | identity_conflict | preparation_busy | project_unstable | workspace_unstable | state_persist_failed | internal_error`, classified by typed causes only (`preparation_busy` is reserved for the writer layer's project-lock conflicts). There is deliberately NO HTTP route, no CLI command, no Browser UI surface, no control-plane command, no deployment flag and no schema v11: the facade (`preparePipelineV2ProjectApply` / `loadPreparedPipelineV2ProjectApplyPackage` / `inspectPipelineV2ProjectApplyOperation`) is consumed by the permanent integration tests and is the explicit handoff surface for the next writer increment. Payloads are secret-bearing and unredacted; payload bytes never enter plan, journal, diagnostics or filenames. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_apply_plan.test.ts` (14), `pipeline_v2_apply_planner.test.ts` (22), `pipeline_v2_apply_state.test.ts` (9), `pipeline_v2_apply_metadata.test.ts` (14), `pipeline_v2_apply_payload.test.ts` (9), `pipeline_v2_project_apply.test.ts` (28) and `pipeline_v2_project_apply_crash.test.ts` (21), over the shared fixture `orchestrator/tests/apply_fixtures.ts` (real finalized schema-v10 runs through the real reducer/provenance copy, the read-only zero-write tree fingerprint that ignores atime, and the per-op nth fault-injection seams). The journal schema is now v2 (the writer extension, below); `preparing`/`failed`/`prepared` carry `execution: null`. Still unwired: HTTP/UI/CLI/routing surfaces, the deployment RW mount, schema v11, selective apply and rollback.

- Production pipeline v2 runner (implemented, wired into the production runner and invoked exactly once per `orchestrator run`): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runner.ts` assembles the whole v2 stack into one entry point — `runPipelineV2(options: PipelineV2RunOptions, deps: PipelineV2RunnerDeps): Promise<PipelineV2RunOutcome>` with `PipelineV2RunOptions {pipelineRoot, configRoot, projectSourcePath, inputBindings: readonly RunInputBinding[], runId?, launcherId?}` — the optional `runId` is the caller-supplied run identifier, read exactly once during the protected preflight before the first await (a hostile Proxy counts exactly one getter read; caller mutation after the capture cannot change the identity). When present the exact captured value (never normalized, never re-generated) becomes the authoritative identity of the run root, durable state, execution documents, output paths and outcome and bypasses the built-in generator entirely (one `crypto.randomUUID` site remains, reached only when the flag is absent); when absent the established generation path and every outcome shape are unchanged. This is caller correlation, not an idempotency key: the run root `<state-root>/pipeline-runs/<run-id>` is claimed exclusively by the single authoritative `mkdir` (pre-checks are fast paths only, the exclusive create is the authority), an existing object of any kind — a valid run, an incomplete directory, a symlink, a regular file, any other layout — is never reused, followed, repaired or removed, and the collision maps to the typed outcome `{ok: false, exitCode: 1, runId, runRoot, state: null, reason: "run_already_exists"}` (the closed `PipelineV2RunCollisionReason`; the existing run's state is never opened, read or returned — `inspect` is the next step; the human reporter prints the dedicated collision line). The claim sits after the whole pipeline/project/input/config/Launcher preflight and before the sink, the runtime adapter and any Session, so no session is ever created before the claim, a preflight failure (including a signal) reserves nothing (`inspect` reports `run_not_found`, a corrected retry with the same id can create the run), and a failure after the claim keeps the root for its owner (re-run → collision; no automatic removal). Two racing fresh-run calls with the same id are decided solely by the exclusive create: exactly one winner runs the ordinary lifecycle while the loser returns the collision outcome with zero dispatches and zero sessions. Not implemented: list-runs, replaying an existing run through `run`, automatic resume on collision, idempotency envelopes; concurrent mutating commands are serialized by the inter-process run mutation lock bullet., the trusted runtime config `PipelineV2StateRootProjection {localRoot, daemonRoot}` (host mode passes the same absolute state root twice; a split projection is operator-supplied), `PipelineV2RunnerDeps {cli, fetchAuth, helperConfig, baseEnv, stateRootProjection, onSignal?, now?, randomId?}`, and the content-free deep-frozen **discriminated union** `PipelineV2RunOutcome` (branches: success `{ok:true, exitCode:0, runId, runRoot, state}`; waiting `{ok:false, waiting:true, exitCode:0, runId, runRoot, state}` — the controlled suspension at the trusted stage-wait boundary; plan-ready `{ok:false, planReady:true, exitCode:0, runId, runRoot, state}` — the controlled suspension at the settled-but-unbound planning acceptance boundary, both checked before the failure shape, no `reason`, no `refused`, no envelope; failure `{ok:false, exitCode:1|130|143, runId, runRoot: string|null, state: PipelineV2RunState|null, reason: PipelineV2PublicReason}` — `reason` is a REQUIRED own property of every nonzero-exit outcome). The machine-readable reason contract is owned once by `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_public_reason.ts`: one exported closed vocabulary `PIPELINE_V2_PUBLIC_REASONS` (the durable failure reasons of the run-state schema, the coordinator's pre-resume refusal reasons, the collision reason, and the runner-boundary categories `run_not_found`/`invalid_options`/`invalid_result`/`artifact_missing`/`artifact_modified`/`run_cleanup_failed`) and ONE mapper `publicReasonFor(cause, fallback)` — typed error classes contribute their own closed `.reason` (validated against the vocabulary or translated through one explicit internal-reason table, e.g. `lifecycle_conflict`/`grant_conflict`/`intent_conflict`/`candidate_conflict`/`revision_conflict`/`plan_conflict`/`wait_conflict` → `invalid_state`, `invalid_intent`/`invalid_request`/`invalid_response`/`invalid_stage_id`/`stage_not_found` → `invalid_options`, `wait_storage_failed` → `state_persist_failed`, `artifact_mismatch` → `artifact_modified`), and untyped causes fall back to the category the failure site owns (`invalid_options` for caller configuration problems, `internal_error` for unexpected failures). Classification never reads `error.message`, never uses substring/regex matching, and never leaks an unknown internal reason; the CLI only serializes the outcome (no CLI-side classification); human stderr diagnostics stay unchanged. The existing-run core's run-root verification throws the typed carrier `PipelineV2PublicReasonError`: a missing run is `run_not_found`, every run-layout divergence is the established `run_layout_invalid`, and an open-time store error is `missing_state`. The options/deps shape validation and the single `onSignal` read live inside one protected preflight region (the seam is read exactly once, checked as `undefined | function`, and only the captured local value reaches the gate; the original getter is never read again), and the `RunCauseGate` is constructed only after both succeeded (a null/primitive deps, a Proxy or throwing getter on a validated field, or a throwing `onSignal(handler)` registration resolves with the generic preflight outcome, never a rejected promise, and registers no handler); the preflight order continues: contract shape validation (never reading binding items or secret env values) -> `loadPipelineV2(pipelineRoot)` -> unique agent-state profile names in declaration order -> `loadProfile(configRoot, name, baseEnv)` exactly once each -> one shared `lifecycleAuthority` Launcher auth (`lifecycleAuthority` lost its dead `workspace` parameter; the v1 callers pass `{launcherId}`) -> launcher-id check -> run id (`deps.randomId?.() ?? crypto.randomUUID()`, safe-id validated) -> run root -> `PipelineV2RunStateSink({stateRoot: projection.localRoot, runId, now})` -> `createDockerHelperPipelineV2Runtime` exactly once with `operatorEnv: authority.baseOperatorEnv` and `expectedLauncherId: authority.auth.launcher_id` -> `coordinatePipelineV2Run(params, control)` — a successful coordination that suspends plan-ready (or, via the existing-run core, waiting) flows through the uniform plan-ready/waiting hook: when the trusted automatic plan-ready continuation bullet's routing helper admits the boundary, the composition runs exactly once before the outcome is mapped (a `refused` outcome branch exists only on that auto path); a broken pipeline, profile or credential produces `ok:false, exitCode:1, state:null, runRoot:null, reason:"invalid_options"` with zero sessions, zero runtime factory calls, zero sink commands and no state root creation (`runId: ""`, `state: null`, `runRoot: null`). Run-root ownership: `<local-state-root>/pipeline-runs/<run-id>/` is created exclusively (mkdir 0700, EEXIST rejects any pre-existing directory/file/symlink without touching it), doubles as the state store run directory (`state.json` inside) and the projection `localRoot`; the daemon path is only `<daemon-state-root>/pipeline-runs/<run-id>`; both state roots must be real canonical non-symlink directories that are the same object (dev/ino), `pipeline-runs` is created 0700 when missing or accepted only as an existing real non-symlink directory with exactly mode 0700 (an unsafe existing directory is rejected unchanged — no `chmod`, no run leaf, no Session, no state; the mode is verified explicitly for created directories too, never trusted to the umask), and the created run directory (verified 0700) must exist on the daemon side as the same object before the coordinator starts — all through the new neutral helper `orchestrator/src/projection_fs.ts` (`inspectProjectionObject`, `inspectProjectionPair`, `translateProjectionPath` with clean-suffix-only translation), whose object/pair checks the Docker adapter now delegates to with byte-identical error messages (its 38-test suite passes unchanged); a successfully created run root is never auto-removed. Signal outcomes align with the preflight: a signal accepted during an operation that then fails before `create_run` outranks that failure (e.g. an auth callback delivering SIGINT before a failing authority check yields 130/`signal_sigint`, not exit 1). Failure boundaries follow the run-root creation: before the exclusive run-leaf creation every failure (and signal) yields `runId:""`/`runRoot:null` and nothing is left behind; from the successful exclusive creation on, any later failure — including a runtime-factory rejection between the run root and the coordinator start — reports the actual run id, the canonical run root and the last authoritative sink snapshot (`state:null` until `create_run`), and the created run root is never removed. Signals: the runner builds the coordinator control from the existing v1 `RunCauseGate` (new synchronous `freezeSignalAcceptance()` snapshots and closes acceptance in one step; `freezeFinalStatus()` and all v1 semantics unchanged) and reads the recorded signal only through a function accessor so no control-flow narrowing can hide a signal accepted between awaits. Checkpoints sit before the project copy, after it, after the input snapshot, after `create_run`, at every agent/decision execution start, immediately before `createExecutionSession`/`createToolSession`/`runAgent` (no await in between), before decision evaluation, and after the engine before the terminal record/output publication — never between a settled execution and its `transition_committed`. Finalization priorities: durable `session_cleanup_failed` (exit 1) > accepted SIGINT/SIGTERM (`signal_sigint` 130 / `signal_sigterm` 143, durable `run_failed`/`agent_failed`/`decision_failed` with honest cleanup pairs) > typed failure (exit 1) > terminal success (exit 0); after the durable state exists every completion path calls the coordinator's memoized cutoff exactly once (inside `finalizeFailure` after cleanup+priority, or in the terminal phase before the final write — one snapshot reused everywhere), so a signal delivered during or after the final write — including `run_succeeded`/`run_failed` — changes nothing, and a failed terminal with published outputs can only finalize `terminal_failed`; the result object is deep-frozen and content-free. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_runner.test.ts` (38 tests) covers the happy path agent->decision->success through the real sink, runtime adapter and data plane with a fake CLI (session counts and tool-before-execution delete order, run/pull argv shape, state v7 success, atomic outputs publication, untouched project source), two-profile preflight loading in declaration order with zero auth/sessions/run-root on broken first/second profiles, pipeline-load/credential/authority/launcher-mismatch preflight zeros, the run-root layout/modes/dev-ino battery, pre-existing run-id objects of every kind untouched, symlinked/wrong-kind `pipeline-runs` and split-root projection mismatch rejection, late failure keeping the run root, durability-unknown at `create_run` (adopted candidate visible, zero further dispatches, zero sessions), the full deterministic signal battery (during auth, during binding parsing, at agent start, inside each Session create, during the worker run, SIGTERM, between states, during the decision execution, cleanup-failure priority, post-cutoff final-write signal ignored, late post-return signal no-op, timeout-vs-signal first-wins) with exact exit codes and durable reasons, and secret canaries over outcome/state/diagnostics. Still unwired: `agent-smoke` stays the v1 diagnostic path; the bundled default-v2 is selectable by `run` without `--pipeline-root` and carries the automatic plan-ready continuation (see the trusted automatic plan-ready continuation bullet).
- Production recovery of the loader-valid post-commit crash windows through `orchestrator resume` (implemented, wired into the production resume chain): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_recovery.ts` is the single catch-free authoritative classifier `classifyPipelineV2ResumeRecovery(pipeline, state)` over a loader-validated state — it checks only the exact bindings needed to choose one recovery suffix (never a second state validator) and returns `none` (the existing chains own the boundary), `pipeline-mismatch` (the settled execution's compiled transition does not resolve through the engine's own `compiledTransitionFor` — the resume refuses `pipeline_mismatch` before any dispatch), `transition` (a settled stage/control execution awaiting its exactly one `transition_committed`; the outcome comes from the execution's own durable contract — the fixed agent lifecycle outcome `completed`, or the durable decision record's outcome — never re-derived from facts, rules or worker output), `run-finalization` (a durably failed execution awaiting exactly one run-level finalization carrying the durable failure reason, or `run_cleanup_failed` when the durable cleanup pair failed), or `terminal` (the incomplete terminal lifecycle: the missing `terminal_reached`, the missing output publication, or the missing final run status derived from the durable terminal result). A settled-but-unbound **planning** execution is deliberately NOT a recovery window (the plan-ready chains own it); genuinely in-flight executions, waiting and finalized runs stay refusals. The recovery belongs to the coordinator: `resumePipelineV2Run` classifies after the run-id binding, bypasses the active-boundary precheck only for classified windows, runs the same single restore (its resumable-boundary policy now accepts exactly the classified windows through the same classifier — one authority, no second validator; a failed agent execution without accepted outputs is restorable for F0), and passes the classified suffix into the continuation chain, which executes it before the engine with the SAME machinery the live coordination uses: the extracted transition kernel `applyTransitionCommit` (one `transition_committed` dispatch + the post-commit signal checkpoint + the trusted stage-wait attempt — shared by the live engine hook and the recovery, so a recovered stage→planning transition enters the automatic `run_waiting` and a recovered stage→terminal transition reaches the ordinary terminal chain), the extracted terminal owner `runTerminalPhase` (record/publication/cutoff/final status — the recovery passes `recordTerminal`/`publication: adopt-or-fresh|skip` flags; the live path is byte-identical), and the outcome-aware finalization dispatch. The run-output publication gained the recovery adoption path `collectOrAdoptRunOutputs` in `pipeline_v2_runtime.ts`: an already published `<runRoot>/outputs` tree is verified byte-exactly against the publication the trusted sources determine (same declared outputs, same kinds, same `pipeline-v2-run-output` digests over the shared hashing primitives, no extra entries, JSON schema revalidated) and adopted without touching a file — a foreign, damaged, incomplete or extra-entry tree fails closed with the typed `run_output_invalid`/`run_output_publish_failed` reasons; the fresh publication contract of `collectRunOutputs` is unchanged. Signal semantics: a pending SIGINT/SIGTERM before the first recovery effect returns the existing signal failure result (130/143 at the runner) with zero durable writes; after a durable linearization the existing coordinator semantics apply (the recovered transition's post-commit checkpoint finalizes durably); no checkpoint inside the atomic dispatch/publish contracts and no Session on any recovery path. Results stay the existing union only (success, waiting, planReady, refusal, ordinary failure, `state_persist_failed`); runner and CLI display them unchanged. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_recovery.test.ts` (32 tests — the S0/S1/S1→planning-wait/S2/S3/S4/F0/F0-cleanup/F0-decision/T0/T1/T1-failed-terminal/T2/T3/T4/E0/P0/W0/I0 matrix through the real substrate with reopen + durable bundle-root reload, exact suffixes, no worker replay, prefix-preserving fingerprints, loader round-trips, the forged-outcome `pipeline_mismatch` refusal, foreign pipeline identity, damaged accepted outputs refusing before publication, damaged/extra/missing published artifacts failing the adoption without rewriting, not-committed publication orphans with the existing failed-run finalization, `run_succeeded` durability-unknown adopting the succeeded candidate, zero-write signal refusals for F0/T3, the post-transition signal linearization, classifier units, content-free frozen results) plus runner-level pass-throughs (S3/F0/T1/T3 in `pipeline_v2_runner_resume.test.ts` tests 19–22). Still not recoverable in this increment: in-flight executions (no worker replay), list-runs, multi-process locking, wait/plan policy changes. The read-only external projection of every boundary, including these windows, is `orchestrator inspect` (next bullet).
- Versioned read-only `orchestrator inspect` (implemented, wired into the CLI): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_inspect.ts` is the single owner of the external boundary classification and the stable `schemaVersion: 1` projection of one durable pipeline v2 run. The whole path is strictly read-only: the run state is loaded only through the proven read-only snapshot store (`PipelineV2RunStateStore.load()` - never `PipelineV2RunStateSink.open`, never a dispatch, never a publisher, never a candidate adoption, never a filesystem repair), the pipeline is reloaded from the durable `state.pipeline.bundle_root` through the trusted loader and verified against the durable identity with the single shared comparator, and the boundary classification is the single authoritative one - the recovery classifier `classifyPipelineV2ResumeRecovery` (exactly one call; a second recovery classifier is forbidden) for every post-commit crash window, the authoritative `restorePipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext` for the planning-acceptance candidate (pre-selected structurally, then verified - a restore failure after the pre-selection is a typed inspect failure, never a branch change) and the authoritative `restorePipelineV2RuntimeContext` for the ordinary and recovery boundaries. There is no worker/runtime, no Launcher credential, no Docker Helper call, no Session and no durable change on any path: a missing run is never created, a damaged run is never repaired, a repeated inspect over an unchanged run returns byte-identical JSON, and one mutating caller per run is safe alongside it (no snapshot isolation against an active writer is claimed). CLI grammar: `inspect --run-id SAFE_ID --config-root ABS [--json]` (both flag forms; the run id against the shared safe-id grammar; `--config-root` required by the shared grammar but unused - no profile is loaded; every fresh-run, auth, intervention, state-root and internal index/digest flag rejected; positional arguments rejected; parse/configuration failures exit 2 before any filesystem read; the dispatcher resolves only the state-root projection and builds no `SubprocessCliRunner` and no mutating command deps object). The successful JSON document carries exactly the own keys `ok`/`exitCode`/`schemaVersion`/`runId`/`runRoot`/`revision`/`status`/`boundary`/`outputs` - the internal durable state document is deliberately NOT part of the contract. `status` is the stable external mapping (`running|waiting|succeeded|failed`; a finished failed run inspects successfully with exit 0 - exit 1 means only that the inspect itself could not be performed). `boundary` is a closed discriminated union with no `unknown`/`other`/`stalled` slot: `plan_ready {planningExecutionIndex, continuation}` (the continuation is derived exclusively from the provenance-verified compiled planning role - the trusted `plan_ready` auto policy present means `resume`, absent means the manual `resume_plan` contract), `waiting {waitIndex, reason, actions}` (the open wait record's declared actions in verbatim order), `resumable {stateId, transitionCount}` (the ordinary runtime boundary AND every recovery window - settled transition, run finalization, terminal lifecycle/publication - because the one safe external action is always `orchestrator resume`; the internal recovery subtype is never exported; a terminal window with an already published tree additionally verifies that tree read-only through the shared published-output verifier - an exact T2 orphan stays resumable, a damaged/missing/extra one is a typed inspect failure, and a durable `run_outputs` record obliges the tree to exist and match it exactly), `in_flight {executionIndex, stateId, executionRole, phase}` (only a genuinely unfinished execution; a settled execution awaiting its recovery suffix is never in-flight), and the final `succeeded {terminalStateId}` / `failed {reason}` boundaries. `outputs` projects the durable run-output records in declaration order with the exact own-key shapes `{id, type, required, present}` plus `digest` only for present outputs - no output content, no filesystem paths, no planning proposal, and an empty array until the terminal publication (a crashed publication with no durable record is invisible to the projection). The `succeeded` boundary verifies the published tree against the durable record through the same shared read-only verifier before it is reported: a finalized succeeded run whose published artifacts are missing, modified, substituted or schema-violating is a typed inspect failure (`artifact_missing`/`artifact_modified`), never a false success. The failure documents carry the closed inspect-only reason vocabulary - before the run root is verified `run_not_found|invalid_options|internal_error` with `runRoot: null` (an unvalidated run id is never echoed), after it `run_state_invalid|pipeline_mismatch|artifact_missing|artifact_modified|internal_error` with the canonical run root (the restore reasons map by phase: `invalid_state` to `run_state_invalid`, `run_layout_invalid` to `artifact_missing`, `run_input_modified`/`accepted_output_modified` to `artifact_modified`; a changed bundle after the run is `pipeline_mismatch`; the published-output defects map by the shared verifier's typed defect kind - `missing` to `artifact_missing`, `modified`/`layout` to `artifact_modified`) - and diagnostics are content-free. Human mode prints one stderr summary line (`orchestrator: inspect <status>/<boundary-kind> (run <id>, revision <n>)`, optionally with the wait index or the terminal state id) and exits 0; JSON mode prints exactly one JSON document plus a newline on stdout with errors and diagnostics on stderr. The provenance boundary is inherited: the inspect loads the pipeline itself (always genuine), and the delegated restores reject forged pipelines with zero state traps. The published-output integrity is owned by the one shared read-only verifier extracted from the recovery adoption (`verifyPublishedRunOutputs`/`verifyPublishedRunOutputsAgainstRecord` with the typed `PipelineV2RunOutputTreeDefectError` in `pipeline_v2_runtime.ts`): the adoption rebuilds its exact existing runtime errors from the verifier's defect classification (byte-identical reasons and messages, artifact identity preserved), and both inspect faces - the terminal-recovery window and the finalized succeeded/recorded-failed boundary - consume the same verifier without a second publisher, serializer, schema validator or digest implementation. Before the publication has begun (no durable record, no tree) a missing tree is the normal resumable terminal chain, never a failure. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_inspect.test.ts` (26 tests - the full proof matrix A-Q built through the real facades/reducer/sink with in-memory objects dropped before the read-only inspect and full run-tree fingerprints before/after: ordinary resumable, initial plan-ready with the auto policy (`resume`), policy-less plan-ready (`resume_plan`), replanned plan-ready after a real revise cycle, waiting with both declared actions verbatim, genuinely in-flight agent executions in the `data_prepared`/`execution_session_created`/`running` phases (never a settled recovery execution), settled stage and settled control recovery transitions as `resumable`, the run-finalization window, all four terminal lifecycle windows (cursor-at-terminal, `terminal_reached`, the crashed publication, `run_outputs_published`), succeeded with a present required output digest and with an absent optional output without a digest, a finalized failed run, the entry-terminal pipeline with zero executions, a missing run creating nothing, damaged/missing state documents never repaired, a changed bundle as `pipeline_mismatch`, modified accepted outputs detected by the delegated restores as `artifact_modified` at both the ordinary and the plan-ready boundary, and a modified published artifact at the terminal window staying `resumable` while the corresponding recovery verification detects the damage at the mutating step (`run_output_invalid`)); plus the CLI parser/dispatcher batteries and the defensive battery (deep freeze, deterministic repeated JSON, exact key shapes, the one-time ordered Proxy options capture with hostile extras unread, invalid run ids never echoed, the forged-pipeline rejection of the delegated restores with zero state traps, the export surface, and the source scan pinning exactly one classifier call, the read-only store load and the absence of sink/dispatch/reducer/publisher/runtime/launcher imports and of every mutation primitive). Still unwired: list-runs, caller-supplied run ids/idempotency keys, worker-log capture and general log streaming (the read-only lifecycle events feed over the durable commit history is implemented — see the next bullet), HTTP API/T3 beyond the versioned routes, multi-process locking, automatic wait-action selection, and recovery of genuine in-flight executions.
- Versioned read-only lifecycle events API (implemented, wired into the CLI, the subprocess substrate, the loopback HTTP service and the Browser UI as the read-only lifecycle timeline): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_events.ts` is the single projection owner that exports the schema-v9 `commit_history` as the stable public event schema 1. `projectPipelineV2Events(state, {after, limit})` accepts only a loader-validated schema-v9 state (the coherence replay stays owned by the state loader; the events layer creates no second validator), reads only `commit_history`, never reconstructs order from executions/transitions/controllers, never copies the 28-kind vocabulary (the public event entry union IS the provenance union passed through verbatim — `{revision, kind}` plus the minimal content-free references `execution_index`/`state_id`/`transition_index`/`wait_index`/`generation_index`/`iteration_index`/`task_index`/`plan_index`/`grant_index`), preserves history order (no normalization, no reordering, no merging of phase events, no timestamps, no derived descriptions), and returns the exact deep-frozen page `{revision, events, nextAfter}`. Cursor contract: `after` is an exclusive durable revision cursor (default 0 starts at revision 1; an event is included iff `event.revision > after`; the limit applies after the filter; `after === state.revision` succeeds with an empty page and `nextAfter: null`; `after > state.revision` is the typed `PipelineV2EventsError` `invalid_cursor` — one closed owner, never classified from message text — with no partial response; negative/fractional/unsafe values are rejected by the CLI/HTTP parsers before the loader; `nextAfter` is the last returned event's revision only when further events exist in the snapshot). Concurrency: one call reads one atomic snapshot (old-complete or new-complete, never partial — the store's atomic rename guarantees it), takes no mutation lock, writes nothing, and a restart reproduces the same cursors and the same event bytes. CLI: `orchestrator events --run-id SAFE_ID --config-root ABS [--after N] [--limit N] [--json]` (both flag forms; `after` default 0, `limit` default 100, maximum 200; signed/fractional/exponent/overflow/empty/duplicate values are parser exit 2; every fresh-run/auth/intervention/state-root/internal flag rejected; no Docker Helper auth, no mutation lock, no run-tree change). The CLI success document carries exactly `ok`/`exitCode`/`schemaVersion: 1`/`runId`/`runRoot`/`revision`/`events`/`nextAfter` (one JSON document + newline on stdout; exit code === document exit code; human mode one content-free stderr line with run id, observed revision, event count and next cursor); the failure document carries exactly `ok`/`exitCode: 1`/`runId`/`runRoot: string|null`/`reason` over the closed events vocabulary (the inspect vocabulary plus `invalid_cursor`; legacy schema v7 is typed `run_state_invalid` with no synthetic history and no rewrite; exit 2 stays the parser/usage failure with empty stdout). The read-only load chain (option validation → run-root verification → store load → pipeline reload + identity compare) is now the shared `loadDurablePipelineV2Run` in `pipeline_v2_inspect.ts` — one read-only loader for both `inspect` and `events`, no second loader. Subprocess substrate: `routing.ts` owns the exact `events` argv (`events --run-id X --config-root C [--after N] [--limit N] --json`) with exact own-key capture; `subprocess_client.ts` adds the versioned events projection (`schemaVersion === 1` required, else `unsupported_contract`), drops the raw `runRoot` before the HTTP layer, never accepts raw state, and never classifies stderr; cancellation semantics match inspect. HTTP: `GET /v1/runs/:runId/events[?after=N&limit=M]` always flows `runId → durable binding → frozen registry project → subprocess client` (no caller project id/config root; unbound CLI-created runs fail closed `binding_not_found` with a byte-identical run tree and no adoption attempt; a removed project stays `project_unavailable`); the query parser accepts only `after`/`limit` with the strict grammar (no percent-encoding, bare keys, duplicates, empty values, sign, fraction, exponent or overflow; order semantically irrelevant; defaults 0/100, limit maximum 200) and everything else is a request failure; the success body is exactly `{schema_version: 1, kind: "events", run_id, revision, events, next_after}` (snake_case envelope, no `run_root`/`state`/project id/host path); a typed `invalid_cursor` is the one explicit events-layer mapping — the request-layer 400 envelope, never a 502 transport failure — while every other events failure follows the inspect precedent (a correctly received failed outcome as a 200 command result). Browser UI boundary: the read-only lifecycle timeline (Browser UI v4) is the only consumer of `kind: "events"` — `ui/app.js` owns one dedicated events parser/transport (`parseEventsDocument`/`createEventsTransport`), the general JSON client keeps rejecting the events kind for every other endpoint, and `tests/ui_model.test.ts` pins the fail-closed boundary. Worker stdout/stderr and durable log capture stay out of scope. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_events.test.ts` (50 tests — the projection-owner battery over the real 66-command lifecycle, cursor/pagination matrix, exact entry pass-through and 28-kind coverage across reducer-built states, typed invalid_cursor, zero-write fingerprints, failure shapes over the real store, the durability-unknown adopted candidate read exactly once, the concurrent-append matrix, every durable window ending its feed at the exact current revision, the leakage matrix, the source scans, and the 66-event paginated E2E through the real CLI subprocess wire), the CLI parser/dispatcher batteries (`tests/cli.test.ts`, `tests/cli_main.test.ts`), the routing/client batteries (`tests/control_plane_routing.test.ts`, `tests/control_plane_subprocess_client.test.ts` with the fixture-child events scenarios), the HTTP authority/pagination battery (`tests/control_plane_http_server.test.ts`), and the UI fail-closed boundary (`tests/ui_model.test.ts`). The worker-log capture is durable since schema v9 (see the worker-log bullet), but it adds no event and stays invisible to this feed; worker stdout/stderr remain ephemeral outside the run-root artifacts.
- Production pipeline v2 resume runner (implemented, wired into the production runner and invoked exactly once per `orchestrator resume`): the same module `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runner.ts` adds `resumePipelineV2(options: PipelineV2ResumeOptions, deps: PipelineV2RunnerDeps): Promise<PipelineV2RunOutcome>` with `PipelineV2ResumeOptions {runId, configRoot, launcherId?}` — `PipelineV2RunOutcome.reason` widened to `PipelineV2FailureReason | PipelineV2ResumeRefusalReason` (a pre-resume refusal is not a durable failure: the state document is untouched). The pinned preflight order, all before any side effect beyond read-only verification: shape validation (`options`/`deps`; the run id against the shared v7 safe-id grammar `expectSafeId` before any path is built; the state-root projection arrives already resolved from the CLI's shared env resolver — the runner reads no environment) -> exactly one `onSignal` read/capture inside the protected preflight region -> the single `RunCauseGate` -> read-only verification of the existing state roots, the fixed `<state-root>/pipeline-runs/<run-id>` layout and its local/daemon projection (`verifyExistingRunRoot`: both state roots real non-symlink directories that are the same object (dev/ino), `pipeline-runs` an existing real non-symlink 0700 directory on both sides, the run root an existing real non-symlink 0700 directory that the daemon sees as the same canonical object; NOTHING is created, chmodded, repaired or removed — a missing/unsafe/mismatched layout is a refusal, never a fix) -> read-only `PipelineV2RunStateSink.open` -> the normalized durable snapshot -> `loadPipelineV2(state.pipeline.bundle_root)` only from the validated durable bundle root -> one `loadProfile(options.configRoot, name, baseEnv)` per unique agent-state profile name in declaration order -> one shared `lifecycleAuthority` Launcher auth + launcher-id check -> `createDockerHelperPipelineV2Runtime` exactly once with the verified run-root projection -> the trusted automatic plan-ready continuation's routing helper on the durable snapshot (`pipelineV2PlanReadyPolicyFor`; when it admits the boundary the composed call is the auto continuation instead of the plain resume, and a `refused` outcome branch exists only on that auto path) -> `resumePipelineV2Run(params, control)`. No second restore, no second state validator, no second coordinator, no own cursor/history reconstruction. Outcome mapping: pre-run-root-verification failures (projection/layout) and a signal recorded before the verification keep the generic preflight shape (`runId:""`, `runRoot:null`, `state:null`, no durable write — the run can be resumed again later); from the verified run root on every outcome carries the actual `runId`, the canonical `runRoot` and the last authoritative `state` — a coordinator refusal maps to `{ok:false, exitCode:1, state, reason: <refusal reason>}` (zero dispatches/sessions/callbacks, the state document byte-identical), post-restore failures map like `run` (signal reasons 130/143, `session_cleanup_failed` > signal > typed failure > success, one memoized cutoff, durability-unknown adopts the candidate and poisons the sink), and success maps `{ok:true, exitCode:0}`. Profile boundary (honest): pipeline execution identity is verified by the durable digest, run-owned inputs/project/accepted outputs by the restore context, and profile definitions remain trusted operator configuration reloaded at resume from the explicitly passed `configRoot` — durable profile epochs/replacements are not implemented (no profile manifest). `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_runner_resume.test.ts` (22 tests) covers resume-after-`create_run` success through the real transport, agent->decision->agent with the global next execution index and gapped activation leaves, deleted original sources never re-read with source-path-free diagnostics, old+winning accepted outputs restored with exact digests, a terminal cursor finalizing with zero executor callbacks, the shared transition budget, missing-state read-only refusals (zero auth/CLI/creations, generic shape for an absent run root), waiting/in-flight/unbound-planning/final refusals with a full state-root fingerprint (the publishing and settled stage/control shapes moved to the recovery suite), damaged input/output/layout/pipeline-identity refusals (`run_input_modified`/`accepted_output_modified`/`run_layout_invalid`/`pipeline_mismatch`) without Sessions or durable writes, the pre-existing run root never re-created or chmodded (unsafe 0755 rejected unchanged; same dev/ino on success), symlinked/file/split projections rejected, one `onSignal` read with throwing getter/registration ordinary outcomes, pre-restore and during-auth signals with the generic/actual shapes, durability-unknown adopting the exact candidate and stopping everything, profile loaded exactly once (env-source Proxy counter) with missing/broken profiles refusing before any Session, launcher mismatch refusing before any Session, hostile Proxy options validated read-only with the ordinary refusal, and the full state-root fingerprint identical across every pre-resume refusal. The durable plan-ready routing battery (A0–A5 windows auto-continuing through the plain `resume` entry, the A6 in-flight refusal and the policy-less plan-ready refusal unchanged) lives in `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_plan_ready_auto.test.ts`.

- Production continue-stage runner API (implemented, **wired into the CLI as `orchestrator continue-stage`**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runner.ts` adds `continuePipelineV2Stage(options: PipelineV2ContinueStageOptions, deps: PipelineV2RunnerDeps): Promise<PipelineV2RunOutcome>` with `PipelineV2ContinueStageOptions {runId, waitIndex, additionalIterations, configRoot, launcherId?}` — the external parameters are exactly the run id, the wait journal index, the one caller policy scalar `additionalIterations` (a positive safe integer with no default) and the standard resume configuration; every internal intervention parameter — the stage id, the expected plan digest, the initial budget, the prepared intent, the pipeline and the compiled plan — is derived from the authoritative durable state after the reopen and is never a caller field (`pipeline`/`pipelineRoot`/`runRoot`/`actionId`/target/`stageId`/`expectedPlanSha256`/`initialBudget`/prepared intent/compiled plan are forbidden; a Proxy capture test proves the five validated fields are read in contract order and no forbidden extra field is ever read). The action is fixed by the entrypoint as the reserved `continue_stage`. No parallel resume runner exists: the two existing-run entrypoints share one private internal core (`runExistingPipelineV2` + `ExistingRunFlow`) — one shared capture boundary before the first await (`captureExistingRunContract`: every top-level options/deps field read exactly once in the fixed order and validated as captured locals — continue options `runId,waitIndex,additionalIterations,configRoot,launcherId`, resume options `runId,configRoot,launcherId`; deps `cli,fetchAuth,helperConfig,baseEnv,stateRootProjection(localRoot/daemonRoot pinned as captured scalars),now,randomId,onSignal`; validation operates only over the captured locals and the original caller objects are never read again — `helperConfig` is captured as a NEW frozen record rebuilt once from the validated `socketPath`/`credentialFile` scalars (exactly one read of each nested field at the capture; a mutating nested getter can no longer swap the socket or the credential between validation and the authority/runtime, which both consume only the rebuilt record) — a hostile getter or any later mutation cannot change a validated value, redirect the run selection, the policy, the configuration, the authority or the runtime; `baseEnv` stays an opaque captured reference, never enumerated/cloned/frozen; hostile extra fields never read; the per-entrypoint wording keeps the resume messages byte-identical and the `onSignal` diagnostic entrypoint-specific), then the single `RunCauseGate`, the read-only `verifyExistingRunRoot` projection, the read-only `PipelineV2RunStateSink.open`, the continue-only policy derivation (`deriveContinueStagePolicy`: the run-id binding; the target wait located by one FULL journal pass with exactly one record at the caller index, which must be the LAST record and declare exactly one exact `continue_stage` action — an already-answered target wait is accepted as the crash-retry boundary, because the facade owns the exact-answer recognition), exactly one open generation that is also the current last generation (the sole source of `stage_id`/`plan_sha256`/`initial_budget`), the intent built only through the public `prepareWaitIntent` with a mandatory digest match against the durable accepted intent when one exists, and no direct manifest read and no compiled-plan restore — then the unchanged pipeline load from `state.pipeline.bundle_root`, the profiles from the caller `configRoot`, one Launcher authority check, one runtime adapter, and the unified coordinator-result -> `PipelineV2RunOutcome` mapping (success, refusal, worker failure, persistence failure, SIGINT 130, SIGTERM 143; no new reason, no outcome widening). The single composed call is exactly `resumePipelineV2RunAfterContinueStageIntervention({pipeline, runRoot, sink, runtime, control, intent, initialBudget})` — no separate intent acceptance, restore or continued-stage composition; thrown errors of the composed facade follow the same runner-boundary policy as the resume's; a signal accepted before the facade cannot allow any intervention write (the post-open and post-derivation signal checks precede every write; after the facade starts, signal ownership stays coordinator-owned through the captured control functions). `resumePipelineV2` keeps its observable behavior, side-effect order, signal semantics, outcome shape and tests byte-identical. Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_runner_continue.test.ts` (11 tests) cover the capture battery (every continue option field read exactly once in the contract order; a mutating `additionalIterations` getter leaves the grant at the validated 2; a mutating `runId` getter continues only the first validated run with the second run byte-untouched; a mutating `deps.now` getter cannot change the run clock and is read exactly once; one shared table-driven test over BOTH entrypoints proves a Proxy `helperConfig` whose `socketPath`/`credentialFile` getters return the validated path first and a replacement path afterwards can no longer swap them — pre-fix the authority authenticated with the replacement socket and token and the runtime created both Sessions on the replacement endpoint — post-fix each nested field is read exactly once, the authority and the runtime use only the validated values, and the replacement is never read, called or published), the C0 after the real reopen (only the four external scalars; the full intervention suffix + the successor execution `dev_entry`/role `stage`/iteration 2/global index 3; the ordinary `worker_failed` — never a refusal or `pipeline_mismatch`; revision +12 exactly; the loader round-trip), the C5/crash retry (the completed intervention boundary built by the existing public facades; the runner reconstructs the exact intent digest itself; only the seven resume commands; the durable projection up to the successor execution never rewritten), the derivation refusals before any pipeline/profile/auth/runtime work (a wrong/missing wait index, the target wait not the last, no exact `continue_stage` declaration, no open generation on the bare waiting boundary; several simultaneously open generations are impossible loader-valid and skipped with that reason; the existing durable intent with a different digest — zero durable writes on every refusal), the contract battery (malformed options/deps shapes, `additionalIterations` 0/negative/fraction/overflow/undefined, the Proxy capture order with hostile extras never read), the pre-facade signal battery (exit 130/143, zero intervention commands, zero Session/runtime effects, the durable state untouched), the export surface (exactly one new runtime function key) and the source scan (no second parser, layout verifier, compiler, plan restore, reducer call or intervention-suffix implementation in the runner). CLI wiring (implemented): `orchestrator continue-stage` routes only through the existing `continuePipelineV2Stage` — `cli_args.ts` parses `continue-stage --run-id SAFE_ID --wait-index N --additional-iterations N --config-root ABS [--launcher-id dhl_...] [--json]` into `ParsedPipelineContinueStageArgs` (both numeric flags share the one canonical `parsePositiveDecimalSafeInteger` grammar — no sign, leading zeros, fraction, exponent, whitespace or overflow; no default for the count; `--json` flag-only singleton), and every fresh-run, respond, state-root and internal-policy flag (`--action`, `--pipeline-root`, `--project`, `--input`, `--workspace`, `--image`, `--profile`, `--task`, `--state-root`, `--daemon-state-root`, target/stage/plan/budget/intent/plan-digest flags) plus every unknown flag or positional argument is rejected. `main.ts` routes the command through the SAME protected CLI-configuration boundary as `run`/`resume` (`resolvePipelineV2CliConfiguration` — the state-root projection first, then the helper configuration; any failure is exit 2 with the usage text and zero facade/auth/subprocess/signal side effects), assembles the deps over the single `SubprocessCliRunner` and the shared v1 signal wiring, invokes `continuePipelineV2Stage` exactly once with the exact options mapping, and reports through the shared `reportPipelineV2Outcome` with the label union widened to `"run" | "resume" | "continue-stage"`. The generic `orchestrator respond` still fail-closes on `continue_stage`/`revise_task` before any response publication (never redirected to the intervention commands); the `revise_task` intervention runs via `orchestrator revise-task` (next bullet).
- Production revise-task runner API (implemented, **wired into the CLI as `orchestrator revise-task`**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_runner.ts` adds `revisePipelineV2Task(options: PipelineV2ReviseTaskOptions, deps: PipelineV2RunnerDeps)` with `PipelineV2ReviseTaskOptions {runId, waitIndex, taskId, taskBody, configRoot, launcherId?}` — the external parameters are exactly the run id, the wait journal index, the task id and the caller's revised task body (a non-empty string, the only place the new body exists) plus the standard resume configuration; the action is fixed by the entrypoint as the reserved `revise_task`, and every internal intervention parameter — stage id, expected plan digest, budget, prepared intent/candidate, pipeline, compiled plan — is derived by the existing handoff controller from the authoritative durable state after the reopen and is never a caller field (a Proxy options test proves the six fields are read in contract order and no forbidden field — `pipelineRoot`/`runRoot`/`actionId`/intent/candidate/manifests/digests/stage/plan/generation/iteration identifiers/budget/runtime/control — is ever read). The current boolean `continueStage` flag of the shared private existing-run core was replaced by a minimal discriminated flow/caller-policy model (`ExistingRunCallerPolicy`: `resume` | `continue_stage` with runId/waitIndex/additionalIterations | `revise_task` with runId/waitIndex/taskId/taskBody; `ExistingRunFlow.callerPolicyKind` drives the option-field capture and the post-open derivation), and all shared stages stay single: capture + shape validation, the `RunCauseGate`, read-only run-root verification, `PipelineV2RunStateSink.open`, the pipeline load from durable `state.pipeline.bundle_root`, profile loading, Launcher authority, the runtime adapter, and the coordinator-result → outcome mapping; `resumePipelineV2` and `continuePipelineV2Stage` keep their observable behavior, texts, read orders, signal semantics and outcome mapping unchanged (their suites are unmodified beyond the export-surface pin gaining the fourth key). Revise capture order: `runId, waitIndex, taskId, taskBody, configRoot, launcherId` — each exactly once; the deps order is unchanged. After the sink open and strictly before any pipeline/profile/auth/runtime work the read-only routing gate (`gateReviseTaskRouting`) verifies the durable `run_id` binding, locates the target wait by one full journal pass (exactly one record with the caller index, and it must be the LAST record), requires exactly one `revise_task` declaration in its actions, and admits exactly the two runner-routing boundaries — C0 (the wait open, no accepted intent: the intervention not started) and R4 (the wait answered with exactly the `revise_task` action: the crash/retry boundary after the completed intervention) — refusing every other shape (a mid-intervention intent-without-response window, a foreign answer, malformed shapes) as an ordinary post-run-root refusal with zero durable writes, zero Sessions and zero downstream calls; loader-invalid wait journals (a duplicate wait index, two `revise_task` declarations, malformed actions) are refused earlier at the sink open. `taskId` and `taskBody` stay caller policy (never derived from the ledger); the runner reads no manifests, prepares no intent and builds no delta/retry machine — the handoff controller owns the deep exact-boundary verification and the wrong-body refusal, its typed errors pass through by identity (exit 1 without a reason field). The single composed call is exactly `resumePipelineV2RunAfterReviseTaskIntervention({pipeline, runRoot, sink, runtime, control, runId, waitIndex, taskId, taskBody})` — the pipeline is obtained only through `loadPipelineV2(state.pipeline.bundle_root)` and no in-memory compiled plan, prepared intent or prior-process result is passed. Signals: a signal accepted before the facade yields 130/143 with the actual run id/root/state and zero intervention/session dispatch (post-cutoff late signals change nothing, shared with resume/continue-stage). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_runner_revise.test.ts` (12 tests) cover the C0 end-to-end (the honest waiting prefix through the real facades, reopen, the runner; the durable projection of the exact eleven-command suffix `plan_intent_accepted → task_revision_accepted → stage_iteration_closed → wait_response_recorded → start_agent_execution {architect, planning, no iterationIndex} → agent_data_prepared → agent_execution_session_created → agent_tool_session_created → agent_running → agent_failed → run_failed`, revision +11, the ordinary `worker_failed` — never a refusal —, execution 3 planning with one session pair cleaned once tool-first, the loader round-trip, `state.pipeline.bundle_root` equal to the bundle directory — the no-in-memory-reuse proof, since the prefix helper returns only filesystem coordinates), the R4 crash retry (the completed intervention built by the existing public facade, reopen, zero intervention dispatch, only the seven resume commands, the wait/task/plan/generation/closure projection byte-identical), the invalid-options matrix (non-record, bad runId/waitIndex/taskId, empty/non-string taskBody, bad configRoot/launcherId — every refusal before any run-root access with the durable tree untouched), the routing-derivation matrix (a missing/not-last/foreign-answer/mid-intervention window each refusing before any pipeline/profile/auth/runtime work with zero auth calls and zero Sessions; loader-invalid journals refused at the sink open with state null and the raw document never repaired), the wrong-body R4 (the handoff refuses with its own typed error — no reason field, no resume start, the durable state byte-identical), the signal battery (SIGINT 130 / SIGTERM 143 at the authority boundary with the actual run id/root/state and zero durable writes), the capture battery (the Proxy options reading the six fields exactly once in contract order with hostile extras unread, a mutating `taskId` getter revising only the first validated task, caller mutation after the pending auth leaving the captured policy in force, and the nested helper fields read exactly once with replacement deps never used), and the export-surface pin (exactly four runtime keys).

- CLI wiring of the production pipeline v2 runner (implemented): the five production commands `orchestrator run`, `orchestrator resume`, `orchestrator continue-stage`, `orchestrator revise-task` and `orchestrator resume-plan` route only through the existing `runPipelineV2`/`resumePipelineV2`/`continuePipelineV2Stage`/`revisePipelineV2Task`/`resumePipelineV2PlanningRunPlan` respectively; `orchestrator/src/cli_args.ts` parses `run [--run-id SAFE_ID] [--pipeline-root ABS] --config-root ABS --project ABS [--input SAFE_ID=ABS_PATH]... [--launcher-id dhl_...] [--json]` into the public `ParsedPipelineRunArgs` (added to the `ParsedCommand` union): all three paths are required absolute paths; `--input` is repeatable with the form split at the first `=` (so `=` inside the path is allowed); zero inputs are valid; empty/unsafe/duplicate input ids and empty/relative input paths are rejected; duplicate singleton flags (including `--launcher-id`) reject instead of silently last-wins; `--json` takes no value and cannot repeat; `--workspace`/`--image`/`--profile`/`--task`/`--state-root`/`--daemon-state-root`, output-path flags and every unknown flag are rejected. The optional singleton `--run-id SAFE_ID` is the caller-supplied run identifier validated against the shared safe-id grammar (both `--run-id value` and `--run-id=value`; duplicates reject; empty/unsafe values reject at parse time with exit 2 and no filesystem access): the caller must durably store it before the call, a lost result is then recovered through `orchestrator inspect --run-id R`, and a repeated `run` with the same id is caller correlation, never a retry or resume. Parse failures exit 2 with the usage text before any auth or filesystem side effect. There is no default pipeline for `run` — `--pipeline-root` is required and the bundled v1 pipeline is never selected by `run`. The trusted state-root projection is runtime configuration, not a flag: `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_state_root.ts` implements `resolvePipelineV2StateRootProjection(env)` — local root `ORCHESTRATOR_STATE_ROOT`, else `${XDG_STATE_HOME}/orchestrator`, else `${HOME}/.local/state/orchestrator` (no source at all is a CLI configuration error, exit 2), daemon root `ORCHESTRATOR_DAEMON_STATE_ROOT` else the local root; values must satisfy the shared clean absolute path contract (`orchestrator/src/clean_path.ts`, the single lexical validator used by both the resolver and the runner preflight: non-empty, absolute, no NUL, no surrounding whitespace, no empty/`.`/`..` segments, no trailing slash except the root `/`, never normalized or rewritten), offending values are never echoed in errors, the resolver creates and canonicalizes nothing (kind/canonical/dev/ino/mode checks stay exclusively in `runPipelineV2`), and the resolved paths never become pipeline fields or reach a worker. `main.ts` keeps a single `SubprocessCliRunner` per invocation and the exact v1 signal wiring (`killActive` first-wins forwarding; the handler runs only when the signal may still count as a user abort; one registration, no second `RunCauseGate` — the gate lives inside `runPipelineV2`); the dispatcher is testable through per-call DI (`runCli(argv, io?)` with a `CliIo` supplying runner/fetchAuth/helper-config/resolver/runner-function/writer fakes; no module-global mutable setters; the entrypoint body runs only under `import.meta.main`). The `run` branch is taken before the v1 helper-config resolution: inside `runPipelineV2Command` the state-root projection is resolved first and the helper configuration second, both inside one protected CLI-configuration boundary — any failure (including the real `resolveHelperConfig` with no `HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`) is a CLI configuration error (exit 2 with usage) with no `runPipelineV2` call, no auth, no filesystem, no runner subprocess, no signal registration, and `runCli` never rejects; the shared v1 `resolveHelperConfig` call still runs only for `smoke`/`agent-smoke` with unchanged behavior.
