The shared reporter checks the waiting and plan-ready branches before the ordinary `ok:false` shapes and prints exactly one stderr summary line — success `orchestrator: run ok (run <RUN_ID>, state <RUN_ROOT>/state.json, outputs <RUN_ROOT>/outputs)`, waiting `orchestrator: <command> waiting (run <RUN_ID>, state <RUN_ROOT>/state.json)`, plan-ready `orchestrator: <command> plan-ready (run <RUN_ID>, state <RUN_ROOT>/state.json)` (one line each, no `failed`/`ok`/`reason`/`outputs`, exit 0; JSON mode prints the exact outcome object) — post-run-root failure `orchestrator: run failed (run <RUN_ID>, reason <REASON>, state <RUN_ROOT>/state.json)` (no summary line until the run root exists; the runner's content-free diagnostics carry pre-root failures), with the fresh-run collision branch before it — `orchestrator: pipeline v2 run failed: run "R" already exists; use inspect` (exit 1; the existing run's state is never opened or disclosed, `inspect` is the one safe next step) — and returns the outcome's `exitCode`. `--json` writes exactly one JSON `PipelineV2RunOutcome` document plus a trailing newline to stdout (no extra wrapper, machine-readable for success and failure, content-free), keeps stdout free of progress lines by switching only the inherit-asked CLI calls (image pull, worker run) to the streaming `stderr` `CliStdio` mode — the child's stdout and stderr flow directly onto the orchestrator's stderr at kernel level (`Bun.spawn` fd 2), with no buffering, no accumulation and no post-hoc forwarding; `capture` calls (structured session answers) stay captured and human mode keeps the adapter's `inherit` (argv, env, options and every runtime decision untouched), and the CLI exits with the outcome's `exitCode`. Run outputs stay at the fixed `<runRoot>/outputs`; the CLI reports but never relocates them. `usage()` documents the run command (required flags, repeatable `--input`, run-owned project copy, fixed state/outputs locations, the two state-root env variables, profiles-selected-by-pipeline-states, no user mounts/paths/credentials/helper transport) and no longer claims `agent-smoke` is one-step only — its production v1 runner is multi-state; `docs/orchestration-workflow.md` was synced to the implemented state. The CLI also exposes `orchestrator resume` — the production pipeline v2 continuation command wired through `runCli`: it parses `resume --run-id SAFE_ID --config-root ABS [--launcher-id dhl_...] [--json]` into `ParsedPipelineResumeArgs` (run id against the shared safe-id grammar; config root absolute; singleton flags reject; `--json` flag-only; every fresh-run flag — `--pipeline-root`/`--project`/`--input`/`--workspace`/`--image`/`--profile`/`--task`/state-root/output-path flags — and every unknown flag or positional argument is rejected), takes the state-root projection through the same protected CLI-configuration boundary and the same env resolver as `run` (resolution failure is exit 2 with the runner never called), invokes `io.resumePipelineV2` exactly once, prints one content-free human summary line (`orchestrator: resume ok|failed (...)`, none before the verified run root) or exactly one JSON `PipelineV2RunOutcome` + newline on stdout with helper output streamed to stderr, and exits with the outcome's exit code; the pipeline comes only from the durable `state.pipeline.bundle_root` and the data only from the run-owned layout. Tests: `tests/cli.test.ts` (run + resume parser batteries + updated usage pins; smoke/agent-smoke parsing unchanged), `tests/pipeline_v2_state_root.test.ts` (11 resolver tests incl. every unclean form through all four env sources), `tests/cli_main.test.ts` (26 dispatcher tests with per-call fakes: exact single dispatch and options mapping for run and resume, resolver-failure exit 2, resume grammar/forbidden-flag battery, exit-code passthrough 0/1/130/143, pre-run-root silence, the JSON single-document contract with canary scans, JSON capture-to-stderr plumbing, unchanged v1 routing for `smoke`/`agent-smoke`). The CLI also exposes `orchestrator respond` — the production pipeline v2 wait-response command wired through `runCli`: it parses `respond --run-id SAFE_ID --wait-index N --action SAFE_ID [--json]` into `ParsedPipelineRespondArgs` (all three values required singletons; run id and action against the shared safe-id grammar; `--wait-index` a canonical positive decimal safe integer — no sign, leading zeros, fraction, exponent, whitespace or overflow; `--json` flag-only; every run/resume flag, target/digest/body flags and every unknown flag or positional argument rejected), resolves ONLY the state-root projection through the same protected CLI-configuration boundary (deliberately no helper configuration, no auth, no subprocess, no signal registration; resolution failure is exit 2 with the production API never called), invokes `io.respondPipelineV2Wait` exactly once, prints one content-free human summary line or exactly one JSON `PipelineV2WaitResponseOutcome` + newline on stdout, and exits with the outcome's exit code. The CLI also exposes `orchestrator resume-plan` — the production pipeline v2 planning continuation command wired through `runCli`: it parses `resume-plan --run-id SAFE_ID --stage-id SAFE_ID --initial-budget N --config-root ABS [--launcher-id dhl_...] [--json]` into `ParsedPipelineResumePlanArgs` (all four value options required singletons supporting both `--flag value` and `--flag=value`; `--run-id`/`--stage-id` against the shared safe-id grammar; `--initial-budget` the canonical `parsePositiveDecimalSafeInteger` grammar with `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` accepted and no default; `--config-root` a non-empty absolute path; `--launcher-id` the `dhl_...` grammar; `--json` flag-only singleton), rejects `--action` (the command is not a respond action and accepts no action id), `--wait-index`/`--additional-iterations`/`--task-id`/`--task-file`/`--task-body`, every fresh-run flag (`--pipeline-root`/`--project`/`--input`/`--workspace`/`--image`/`--profile`/`--task`), the state-root flags, every internal proposal/plan/digest/intent/generation/iteration/transition/compiled-stage flag, and every unknown flag or positional argument; `stageId` and `initialBudget` stay explicit operator policy of every invocation (never derived from the durable state, no body/file reads). `main.ts` routes the command through the SAME protected CLI-configuration boundary (`resolvePipelineV2CliConfiguration` — the state-root projection first, then the helper configuration; any failure exit 2 with usage and the runner never called), assembles the deps over the single `SubprocessCliRunner` (`pipelineV2CommandDeps`; JSON mode keeps the inherit→stderr adapter) with `CliIo` extended by `resumePipelineV2PlanningRunPlan` (production default = the exact runner export; no module-global mutable seam), invokes it exactly once with the exact five-field options mapping, and reports through the shared `reportPipelineV2Outcome` with the label union widened to `"run" | "resume" | "continue-stage" | "revise-task" | "resume-plan"`; the pipeline comes only from the durable `state.pipeline.bundle_root`, and there is no automatic planning loop and no automatic stage/budget selection (tests: `tests/cli.test.ts` parser battery + usage pins; `tests/cli_main.test.ts` dispatcher battery incl. the no-other-API, error-precedence, reporting/exit-code, command-list pins and the end-to-end proof through the real production runner over the two-cycle planning prefix — exact seven-command handoff suffix + seven resume commands, revision +14, successor execution `review_entry`/role `stage`/iteration 1, ordinary `worker_failed`, one session pair cleaned once each tool-first, loader round-trip — plus the initial plan-ready boundary E2E (the fresh run's first planning execution settles unbound → `resume-plan` → the exact six-command initial handoff suffix `task-a:1 → task-b:1 → plan:1 → stage_generation_opened → stage_iteration_opened → transition_committed` + seven resume commands, revision +13, generation 1 bound to `stage-1`/`development`/budget on the zero anchor, successor execution `dev_entry`/role `stage`/iteration 1, ordinary `worker_failed`, one session pair cleaned once each tool-first, loader round-trip). Still unwired: the default pipeline bundle (no v2 migration yet), automatic resume, the automatic stage/budget selection policy.

- Production wait-response command (implemented, wired into the CLI as `orchestrator respond`): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_wait_respond.ts` adds `respondPipelineV2Wait(options: PipelineV2WaitResponseOptions, deps: PipelineV2WaitResponseDeps): Promise<PipelineV2WaitResponseOutcome>` with `PipelineV2WaitResponseOptions {runId, waitIndex, actionId}` and the deliberately minimal `PipelineV2WaitResponseDeps {stateRootProjection, now?, io?}` — no fetchAuth, no Docker Helper CLI, no helper config, no profiles, no baseEnv, no signal lifecycle (proven by a banned-import source-scan test). The outcome is a discriminated union: success `{ok:true, exitCode:0, runId, runRoot, waitIndex, actionId, actionTo, requestSha256, responseSha256, state}` (recording a response does not complete the pipeline; continuation is the separate `orchestrator resume`), failure `{ok:false, exitCode:1, runId, runRoot: string|null, state, reason}` with the closed reason set `invalid_options` (hostile shapes; an unvalidated run id is never echoed), `missing_state` (sink open), `run_layout_invalid` (read-only layout/projection verification), the controller's own reasons verbatim, and `internal_error` for unexpected causes. Fixed production ordering, all read-only before the controller: shape validation → state-root projection → read-only state-root verification (same object dev/ino) → fixed `<state-root>/pipeline-runs/<run-id>` → run-root projection as one real canonical object with exactly mode 0700 (never created or chmodded) → read-only `PipelineV2RunStateSink.open` → normalized snapshot → **the early fail-closed reserved-action guard** (the two confirmed intervention action ids `continue_stage`/`revise_task` are rejected as an ordinary `invalid_state` failure carrying the authoritative snapshot, strictly before any response publication or dispatch — their durable interventions must run before the response and this command carries no policy parameters to run them; the reducer's own schema-v7 invariant rejects such a response after the publication, leaving an orphan manifest, so the guard moves the rejection before the side effect; ordinary action ids keep the unchanged generic controller path; the set is a local implementation detail with no export) → `recordPipelineV2WaitAction` (the single internal response chain shared with the raw API). Pre-controller refusals leave the run tree byte-identical (fingerprint test); durability semantics are the controller's own (`not_committed` → waiting state + orphan response with an exact-retry commit; `durability_unknown` → adopted candidate with no further dispatch and an idempotent recognition by a fresh call; exact same answer → idempotent success with identical inode/mtime/bytes and zero commits; different action → conflict without writes). `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_wait_respond.test.ts` (21 tests) covers the happy path with round-trip, exact retry, conflict, both durability faults via the store's own IO seam, missing state/layout, active/publishing states, wrong/stale indexes, undeclared action, tampered/noncanonical/missing/restored/symlinked/wrong-mode/conflicting manifests, projection and mode refusals, tree-identical pre-controller refusals, the reserved-action battery (both ids: `invalid_state` with the authoritative snapshot, unchanged revision, no durable response, no response file, identical repeat, zero store commits via the counting IO), the look-alike ids (`continue_stage_x`, `CONTINUE_STAGE`, `revise_task2` — not reserved, ordinary `invalid_response`), the missing-state/layout precedence over the reserved guard, the banned-dependency source scan, deep-freeze/content-free diagnostics, hostile options, the export surface, and the no-message-parsing/no-second-serializer source scan. Still unwired: production wait entry, automatic resume after response, P01, TASK revision, budget grants, profile replacements, schema changes, migrations, run listing/API/T3, multi-process locking, Docker Helper/Launcher changes, v1.

## Hard invariants

- Never mount `docker.sock` into the orchestrator or agent containers; docker-helper is the only Docker access path.
- Images must not bake in provider credentials or OpenCode model config.
- The agent worker env is built from **exact bindings declared by the selected trusted profile** (`orchestrator/src/profile.ts`), plus explicit orchestrator-owned run params (`OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` from the profile's OpenCode config, the child session bearer, `AGENT_SMOKE_*` run identity). No ambient inheritance. Control variables (`DOCKER_HELPER_*`, `AGENT_SMOKE_*`, `ORCHESTRATOR_*`, `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`) and operator path variables (`HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`) can be neither profile destinations nor sources; launcher credentials, admin tokens, and the orchestrator state path are never forwarded. Keep `orchestrator/src/main.ts` usage text and tests in sync when touching the contract.
- For `agent-smoke`, exit code 0 or agent JSON events alone never mean success: validate the per-activation `result.json` schema (exact fields only, schema version 2), the `run_id`/`state_id`/`activation_index`/`attempt` identity, artifact existence and workspace confinement (including symlinks), unchanged protected inputs, and the outcome transition to a success terminal before accepting the result.
- Every created child Session must be cleaned up on all controlled exit paths, including errors and cancellation. Cleanup failure makes the overall run unsuccessful, even when the worker succeeded.
- Trusted execution profiles: implementation, deterministic tests and the end-to-end profile-backed `agent-smoke` UAT (real launcher credential, real docker-helper, real OpenCode/LLM run, `local/glm53-flash`, report from 2026-09-11) are complete; the UAT verified the full v1 path including durable state, cleanup and secret containment.
- `base`/`go26`/`dotnet10`/`orchestrator` run as non-root `opencode` user (uid/gid 1000); `gdk` follows its upstream GDK image user model. `Dockerfile_base` pins `ENV HOME=/home/opencode`: the upstream `ghcr.io/anomalyco/opencode` image sets no `HOME` and no passwd entry, so the container runtime would derive `HOME=/` and OpenCode fails with `EACCES` on `/.local` (the passwd home matches, one `ENV` keeps the contract independent of upstream config).

## Docs pointers

- `docs/manifesto.md` — design principles (OpenCode is an execution engine, not state authority; the orchestrator owns state).
- Run plan manifest substrate (pure, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_manifests.ts` fixes the canonical content-free format of the three manifest kinds a future user-intervention layer will exchange: the plan revision (`kind: "plan_revision"` — the full immutable plan snapshot with one or more sequentially ordered `stages`, each carrying at least one task pointer; `previous_sha256 === null` iff revision 1, lowercase SHA-256 from revision 2; `root_task {input_id: "task", sha256}` protected binding; positive-safe-integer `origin_execution`; `stage.template` is a reference to a static pipeline stage template and the pipeline schema is unchanged), the task revision (the only place a task `body` exists; `origin` is the content-free class `planning_proposal|user_response` — `planning_proposal` only at revision 1, `user_response` for revisions above 1, a planning system may still create new tasks at revision 1 later), and the two wait intents (`continue_stage_intent` with `additional_iterations > 0` — no upper limit is baked into the parser; `revise_task_intent` carrying two task digests, never a task body). Normalization semantics: stage array order is semantic (sequential execution; reordering stages changes the digest), task pointers inside a stage are sorted by task id and every `depends_on` list is sorted by dependency id by the single preparer (permuting equivalent tasks or dependencies never changes the canonical JSON or the digest; the only task-order semantics is `depends_on`), stage ids are plan-unique, task ids are globally plan-unique, dependencies are same-stage only with no self/duplicate/unknown edges and no cycle, and the flat task pointer form is `{id, revision, sha256, depends_on}`. Digests: `SHA-256("pipeline-v2-plan-revision\0"+canonicalJson(manifest))`, `SHA-256("pipeline-v2-task-revision\0"+...)` and `SHA-256("pipeline-v2-wait-intent\0"+...)` (both intent kinds share the intent domain; their `kind` fields differ in the payload) — three new domains separated from each other and from every existing pipeline-v2 domain, built on the one shared `canonicalJson` (no second serializer) and the shared `pipeline_v2_scalar.ts` predicates (no local copies). Public surface is exactly `PipelineV2RunPlanManifestError`, `prepareTaskRevisionManifest`/`parseTaskRevisionManifest`, `preparePlanRevisionManifest`/`parsePlanRevisionManifest`, `prepareWaitIntent`/`parseWaitIntent`; one validation/normalization chain per manifest type with `parse*` running the same chain after `JSON.parse`; results are deep-frozen `{manifest, canonical_json, sha256}` snapshots registered in the shared module-private provenance registry (`pipeline_v2_run_plan_provenance.ts`, internal; registry, digest builders and freezing helpers are not exported); diagnostics are content-free (malformed JSON is a stable message without parser fragments; unknown user property names, values and task bodies are never echoed). Binding validators live in `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_bindings.ts` — public surface exactly `PipelineV2RunPlanBindingError`, `validatePlanTaskBindings` (exactly one revision per plan task pointer — missing/extra/duplicate rejected — plus `task_id`/`revision`/`sha256` identity and run-id equality), `validatePlanRevisionChain` (revision 1 with no predecessor; successor = same run, `revision === previous.revision + 1`, `previous_sha256 === previous.sha256`, unchanged protected root-task binding — stage/task content may change), `validateTaskRevisionChain` (same chain for two prepared task revisions: revision 1 with no predecessor and a null previous digest; successor = same run, same task id, `revision === previous.revision + 1`, `previous_sha256 === previous.sha256`; the content-free `origin` class stays owned by the manifest chain — no second origin validator), `validateRootTaskBinding` (exact equality of `root_task.sha256` with a caller-computed protected-input digest; the TASK object/body is never received), `validateContinueIntentBinding` (same run, `expected_plan_sha256 === plan.sha256`, declared stage exists; no template maximum grant — future policy) and `validateReviseIntentBinding` (same run/task, candidate = exact successor of the CURRENT accepted revision, both intent digests naming current/candidate exactly, candidate origin `user_response`); every validator gates on the shared provenance registry before any field is read (hand-built/spread/`structuredClone`/Proxy rejected with zero getter/trap hits; caller objects never frozen or modified). `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_manifests.test.ts` and `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_bindings.test.ts` cover exact shapes/unknown fields at every level, zero-stage/zero-task rejection, stage-order digest sensitivity, task/dependency permutation invariance, edge-change sensitivity, id uniqueness, dependency batteries (unknown/cross-stage/self/duplicate/cycle), scalar/digest/integer boundaries, determinism/mutation isolation/deep-freeze, the three-domain separation battery, body digest sensitivity, all binding positive/negative cases, the chain battery, the provenance battery, malformed-JSON and canary-free/value-free diagnostics, both export surfaces (no registry/minter/digest-builder/test seam), and source scans (no second canonical serializer, no local safe-id/SHA pattern copies). The originally listed blockers are since resolved: the durable task ledger is the schema v7 `task_revision_accepted` command, the `plan acceptance ↔ transition` boundary is the planning-run-plan handoff, and the `stage_iteration_opened ↔ start_agent_execution` boundary is the coordinator's global execution-index chain. Production-reachable transitively through the planning chain (construction → acceptance → handoff → runner → CLI). Still unwired: P01, migrations/API/T3, the default pipeline bundle.
- Agent-authored run plan proposal substrate (pure, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_proposal.ts` is the snapshot substrate for the semantic document a planning execution hands the orchestrator to describe the next run plan — deliberately NOT a durable manifest and carrying **no digest of its own** (the accepted execution output that carries it is already bound by its own durable output digest; a second proposal digest would create a parallel identity without adding authority). Runtime export surface is exactly `PipelineV2RunPlanProposalError`, `preparePipelineV2RunPlanProposal(value)` and `parsePipelineV2RunPlanProposal(raw)` (one `JSON.parse`, then the same validation/snapshot chain — no second compiler); both return the DIRECT deep-frozen proposal document `{schema_version: 1, kind: "run_plan_proposal", stages[] {id, template, tasks[] {id, depends_on}}, new_tasks[] {id, body}}` — no `{manifest, canonical_json, sha256}` envelope. Responsibility boundary (owned HERE, only): the document representation with exact OWN enumerable fields at every level (required fields must be own enumerable properties — an inherited property never satisfies the shape — and unknown own fields are rejected without echoing their names), `schema_version === 1`, the exact `kind`, array/record/string shapes, the shared `pipeline_v2_scalar.ts` safe-id grammar for every stage id/template reference/task id/dependency entry, `body` as a non-empty string, and duplicate `new_tasks[].id` rejection (locally ambiguous ownership of one content-bearing entry). Deliberately NOT validated here (other layers own these rules): plan-wide unique stage/task ids, non-empty stage/plan policy, and every dependency graph semantic — self/duplicate/unknown/cross-stage edges and cycles are rejected later by `preparePlanRevisionManifest` (which also owns task/dependency order normalization); whether a reference naming an id that also appears in `new_tasks` denotes a durable task (a silent rewrite attempt, rejected there) or a genuinely new task is a construction-layer question against the durable task ledger; template existence is the compiler's. DECLARED ORDER IS PRESERVED VERBATIM at all four array levels (stages, tasks, depends_on, new_tasks) — the proposal is a safe independent copy of the agent-authored document in declared order, never a canonical manifest; a shape-valid proposal prepared by this layer is not therefore a valid plan. Provenance: the exact frozen object returned by `prepare` AND by `parse` is registered in the SHARED internal registry (`pipeline_v2_run_plan_provenance.ts` — the union carries the additive `run_plan_proposal` kind; no second registry); hand-built/spread/`structuredClone`/Proxy look-alikes are unregistered and rejected with zero getter/trap hits; other prepared kinds are never recognized as proposals. Diagnostics are content-free (malformed JSON is a stable message without parser fragments; unknown property names, values and bodies never echoed). `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_proposal.test.ts` (19 tests) covers the direct four-key shape, prepare/parse structural equality with distinct registered identities, one `JSON.parse`, exact fields at every level, inherited-field rejection (root + nested, own `PipelineV2RunPlanProposalError`, never a `TypeError`), scalar/safe-id/body validation, duplicate `new_tasks` rejection, verbatim order preservation at all four array levels, permutation sensitivity, mutation isolation/deep-freeze, the responsibility-boundary matrix (duplicate pointers/cycle/unknown/cross-stage deps/empty stages/empty tasks/unused new task/undeclared pointer task all ACCEPTED here), the provenance battery (both registrations, kind separation, look-alikes/clones/Proxy with zero traps), content-free diagnostics, the exact three-key export surface, and source scans (no `canonicalJson`/`CryptoHasher`/digest domain/canonical_json/sha256 strings, no sorting, no graph traversal machinery, no fs/state/store/coordinator/runner/CLI imports, no registry/minter/seam exports). Production-reachable transitively: the trusted read happens in the planning-output composition (`readAcceptedJsonOutput` → `preparePipelineV2RunPlanProposal`), the construction/acceptance layers consume the prepared proposal, and the planning transition is the handoff's committed planning transition. Still unwired: migrations/API/T3.
- Read-only proposal → candidate construction layer (production-neutral, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**; anchored/retry-aware): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_construction.ts` (public facade) + `pipeline_v2_run_plan_construction_internal.ts` (internal core + frozen `productionRunPlanConstructionOps` over exactly the two store loaders) is the single layer that builds one provenance-backed `PreparedPipelineV2RunPlanCandidate` from an agent-authored proposal and the durable state, using only the existing manifest/store/candidate chains: public runtime surface exactly `PipelineV2RunPlanConstructionError` + `constructPipelineV2RunPlanCandidateFromProposal({runRoot, state, proposal})` (types erased). One construction call returns the same exact candidate on a fresh, a partial and a completed plan-acceptance boundary. The caller passes no pipeline, no sink, no compiled plan, no digests, no revisions, no execution index and no prepared manifests — every durable field is derived from the validated state and the proposal: `run_id` from the validated state; the run-root binding `basename(runRoot) === state.run_id` before any store load (the canonical store contract stays with the loaders); `origin_execution` = the index of the last durable execution (the planning/settled/unbound acceptance boundary belongs to the acceptance controller and is deliberately NOT re-checked here; the calling production chain runs `restorePipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext` before the construction); the root task is exactly one durable input named `task` with `protected: true` (its digest is the only root-task digest source); stages/templates/task ids/dependencies are declared only by the proposal (graph/normalization semantics stay with the single existing `preparePlanRevisionManifest`). Anchored target resolution (zero loads): plan records whose `origin_execution` names this origin execution are the acceptance evidence of exactly this boundary — 0 records target the next revision (`1` on an empty plan ledger, else last+1) with the last plan record (or none) as the base; exactly 1 record is the completed target (it must be the last plan record; the base is its immediate predecessor); more than 1, or a non-last single record, is `invalid_state`. The FIRST store load is the target plan artifact by the computed revision. Absent artifact: a completed ledger record → `artifact_missing`; durable task evidence intersecting `new_tasks` → `artifact_missing` (partial ownership is never recognized without the commit-marker); otherwise the fresh construction runs the previous chain unchanged (the pointer resolution against the latest durable revisions, the unused-`new_tasks` rule, the new task manifests re-prepared from the bodies, the plan manifest preparation, the loads: the base plan then the pointered existing tasks in proposal traversal order, each with its immediate durable predecessor above revision 1). Present artifact: the single retry anchor — the loader binds run id/revision; the origin execution and the exact base predecessor digest are bound here; the proposal's normalized stage/template/task/dependency projection must equal the artifact's; every new task re-prepares the expected revision-1 manifest from the proposal body and binds it to the target pointer, the loaded artifact and a possible durable ledger record exactly (an exact durable record is a partial/completed retry; a mismatch is a conflict, never a rewrite); every pointered existing task resolves ONLY in the durable ledger exactly at the pointer's revision (current artifact + predecessor as before); every `new_tasks` entry must be referenced by the artifact's pointers. The candidate is assembled only by the existing `preparePipelineV2RunPlanCandidate` and returned as its exact object (never copied, never wrapped, no new provenance); the acceptance controller stays the owner of dispatch reconciliation and publication adoption. Anchored load order: target plan → base predecessor plan → target tasks in the artifact's semantic order, each with its predecessor; orphan artifacts at non-target revisions stay ignored, a foreign orphan exactly at the target revision is an anchor conflict. Loaded prepared plan/task objects are opaque — the internal layer checks only the safe form of the loader-result wrapper (null → `artifact_missing`; non-record wrapper/missing prepared/missing manifest record/wrong kind → `malformed_loader_result`, never a `TypeError`); their provenance and every run/task/revision/digest/chain correspondence is verified by the existing `preparePipelineV2RunPlanCandidate`. Store/manifest/binding typed errors pass through by object identity. Capture boundary: options shape → `runRoot`/`state`/`proposal` each read exactly once in fixed order (hostile extras never read) → ops shape/methods each once → the non-empty run-root scalar → the proposal provenance gate (the shared registry lookup, zero traps) → the single state validation → the derivation and the anchored target search — all before the first await; caller mutation during pending loads cannot change the construction. Failure contract: closed reasons `invalid_options | invalid_state | run_root_mismatch | construction_conflict | artifact_missing | malformed_loader_result` with the last validated durable state; existing typed manifest/binding/store errors are never re-classified; diagnostics are content-free. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_construction.test.ts` (22 tests) covers the initial-plan bridge (real accepted planning JSON output → reader → proposal → construction → exact candidate r1 with two new tasks, nothing published, acceptance-verifier acceptance, exact `toEqual` against the hand-built public-chain candidate), the replanning r2 construction after a restart with the exact load order (the absent target artifact first) and the acceptance-verifier proof at the second settled planning execution, the honest post-revise replanning boundary (the revise response closes the wait, the second planning execution's pointer-only proposal selects the latest durable task-a@2, loads r2 then predecessor r1, and the new plan r2 points task-a@2), the anchored digest-mismatch rewrite conflict, the unused-`new_tasks` and unresolvable-pointer conflicts, the manifest-graph pass-through battery (duplicate pointers/unknown/cross-stage/cycle/empty stages/tasks keep `PipelineV2RunPlanManifestError` by identity), the missing-artifact matrix (the completed ledger record without its artifact; the pointered current task; the anchor-bound task artifact), the malformed loader-result matrix (own typed failures, never a `TypeError`, no hostile value in diagnostics; an unbound orphan-shaped fake is refused as the anchor's foreign origin; a fully anchor-bound fake reaches the candidate provenance gate and is rejected there — the deep verification is delegated, never duplicated), the orphan battery (a foreign orphan at the target revision is an anchor conflict; orphans at non-target revisions stay ignored with the ledger-named revisions still selected), the anchored/retry-aware matrix through the production chain (`PipelineV2RunStateSink.open` → `loadPipelineV2(durable bundle root)` → `restorePipelineV2PlanningAcceptanceContext` → `compiledExecutionRoleFor` → `readAcceptedJsonOutput` → proposal → construction → acceptance: fresh (full suffix after publication), W1 all artifacts orphan (task-a → task-b → plan), W2 task-a durable (task-b → plan), W3 both tasks durable (plan), W4 plan durable (zero dispatch) with the exact candidate identity (`toEqual` against the first attempt's candidate, same plan/task digests), the anchored load log, the exact artifact inode/mode/mtime/bytes preservation, the revision deltas and the loader round-trip after every scenario; and the pointer-only completed control recognizing the accepted r2 with zero dispatch instead of building r3), the anchored negative battery (durable task evidence without the commit-marker; two same-origin plan records → `invalid_state`; a forged base record digest → the predecessor conflict; a forged durable task digest → the pointer conflict — each with the exact load log and zero construction), the capture/provenance/mutation batteries (Proxy options read exactly once in contract order, Proxy/clone proposals rejected with zero traps, mutation-after-await immunity, malformed options/ops matrices at the capture boundary), the state/run-root/root-task boundary matrix, the derivation naming the last durable execution regardless of role (no acceptance-boundary re-check), the exact export surfaces, and the source scan (one manifest preparer path, one candidate preparer, two store loaders; no parser/digest/serializer/registry/minter/state-writer/reducer/controller/runner/CLI). Production-reachable transitively through the planning-output composition (the trusted proposal read and the compile + acceptance happen there). Still unwired: migrations/API/T3.
- Run-plan candidate layer (pure substrate + publication, implemented, **production-reachable transitively**): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_run_plan_candidate.ts` (public) + `pipeline_v2_run_plan_candidate_internal.ts` (internal core) assembles one coherent candidate from the prepared manifests — public runtime surface exactly `preparePipelineV2RunPlanCandidate(options)` (sync, no filesystem) and `publishPipelineV2RunPlanCandidate(runRoot, candidate)` (types erased), with `PreparePipelineV2RunPlanCandidateOptions {plan, taskRevisions, previousPlan, previousTaskRevisions, protectedInputDigest}` and the deep-frozen two-key candidate `PreparedPipelineV2RunPlanCandidate {plan, task_revisions}`. Preparation order is fixed: capture each option field exactly once (no spread, no re-destructuring, no later reads — a getter that throws on a second read never fires; later mutations of the caller's options/arrays cannot influence the result; caller objects are never frozen) → provenance gates before any manifest field is read (plan → both task values checked with `Array.isArray` → `taskRevisions` materialized exactly once into a snapshot (one `Symbol.iterator` read) → the current snapshot entries' provenance gates → `previousPlan` provenance gate if non-null → `previousTaskRevisions` materialized exactly once into a snapshot (one `Symbol.iterator` read) → the previous snapshot entries' provenance gates; the single-snapshot boundary — every gate, binding validator, predecessor map and the deterministic ordering below reads only the snapshots, so a later iterator read of a mutating Proxy array can never swap the bound objects; hand-built/spread/`structuredClone`/Proxy rejected with zero getter/trap hits through the shared manifest registry) → `validateRootTaskBinding` → `validatePlanRevisionChain` → `validatePlanTaskBindings` → the task predecessor set (previous entries keyed by task id: duplicate predecessors rejected, an entry for a task the candidate does not declare rejected, a predecessor for a revision-1 task rejected; every current revision > 1 requires exactly one predecessor and calls `validateTaskRevisionChain` — missing/gap/wrong digest/foreign run rejected; sparse arrays and explicit `undefined` entries are rejected by the gates; caller array order is never semantic) → the candidate's `task_revisions` are the exact prepared objects in stage declaration order × the plan's normalized task order (no second sort, no global flat sort, caller order never preserved) → deep-freeze → registration in a module-private `WeakSet`. Publication (internal core with the structural `PipelineV2RunPlanCandidatePublicationOps {publishTaskRevision, publishPlanRevision}` capability over the public store functions, one frozen `realPipelineV2RunPlanCandidatePublicationOps`, no mutable module-global ops/installer; tests may inject per-call ops): candidate provenance gate before any field read and before any store call → broken/missing ops functions fail before any store call (each ops method is read exactly once into a captured local before its type check; the caller's ops object is never read again — a getter that throws on a second read never fires and mid-run method reassignment cannot change dispatch) → tasks published strictly sequentially in candidate order (never a joined parallel batch) with a structural result check (kind/run/task id/revision/canonical JSON/digest match the candidate task; hostile injected results fail as a typed store error `not_published`/`io_failure`) → only after the last task publication succeeds the plan revision is published — the plan artifact is the filesystem commit marker of the whole candidate, never durable acceptance. The exact store errors pass through unchanged (`not_published`/`durability_unknown`, no re-tagging, no retry loop, no cleanup/rollback — a partial failure leaves immutable orphan task artifacts that only a caller retry reuses through the store's exact-retry adoption, and the candidate layer never reads state or dispatches reducer commands). The public wrapper always runs production ops; an injected call cannot affect a parallel production call. `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_run_plan_candidate.test.ts` (45 tests) covers revision-1/revision-2 happy paths, multi-stage order, task/previous permutations, protected-digest mismatch, plan/task chain failures, missing/extra/duplicate/sparse/undefined current arrays, missing/extra/duplicate/foreign-run/wrong-task predecessors, revision-1 predecessors, exact shape/deep-freeze/caller mutation isolation, one-time option capture with read-order and throw-on-second-read proofs, manifest+candidate provenance batteries with zero store calls and zero proxy traps, exact export surfaces, task-before-plan ordering, plan never called after a task failure, all four store failure modes with original-error identity, hostile injected result mismatches, the real-filesystem happy path with load round-trips, exact-retry inode/mode/mtime/bytes idempotency, partial-publication retry adoption, task/plan conflicts, identical and conflicting concurrency (orphan boundary documented), per-call isolation, canary-free diagnostics, and a source scan (no message parsing, no second serializer/digest builder/scalar predicates, no mutable global seam, no reducer/sink/coordinator imports). The candidate layer never reads durable state and never dispatches a reducer command; the authoritative task/plan ledgers, the schema v7 reducer commands and the graph-transition acceptance boundary exist (schema v7), and the run plan acceptance controller is the production consumer of this publication (via the planning-output composition).
- `docs/pipeline-model.md` — target user-facing pipeline model: responsibility boundaries, roles and profiles, declarative process/data contracts, validation, defaults, and migration of the established workflow.
- `docs/orchestration-workflow.md` — design sketch: only `smoke`/`agent-smoke` are implemented; don't treat described future interfaces as existing.
- agent-smoke result contract: the worker writes a structured `result.json` per activation (schema version 2 with `run_id`/`state_id`/`activation_index`/`attempt` identity, `status` `completed`, `summary`, `artifacts`); protected input digests must be unchanged; fixture in `orchestrator/fixtures/agent-smoke/`.
- Durable Project Apply filesystem writer (implemented, foundation+writer layer, no public surface yet): `orchestrator/src/pipeline_v2_project_apply_writer_internal.ts` (orchestration) composes the new owner modules `pipeline_v2_apply_fd_internal.ts` (the single fd-relative syscall adapter: libc FFI `openat/mkdirat/unlinkat/symlinkat/renameat/renameat2/readlinkat/fstatvfs/faccessat/flistxattr` plus `fstat`/`read`/`write`/`fchmod`/`fsync` on held fds and `getdents64` through `syscall()` — every project mutation is fd-relative against the locked root descriptor, no absolute target path ever reaches a mutation primitive, `O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC` everywhere, errno through `__errno_location`+`memcpy`), `pipeline_v2_apply_project_lock_internal.ts` (the project lock owner: `flock(fd, LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on the `O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC` root descriptor — zero lock artifacts, kernel-released on process death, `busy` → typed `project_busy` result, the locked fd doubles as the writer's root dirfd, re-verified `dev`/`ino` + canonical realpath under the lock), `pipeline_v2_apply_verify_internal.ts` (the fd-based observation + walk engine: per-path node observation through openat with ELOOP→O_PATH+readlinkat for symlinks and the fd-based metadata gate — the execution twin of the preparation probe, without path joins), `pipeline_v2_apply_exec_internal.ts` (the step executor + temp ownership + publication) and `pipeline_v2_apply_crash_internal.ts` (the crash-marker helpers the fault-injection tests use to simulate abrupt process death). The public facade gained `applyPreparedPipelineV2Project({applyStoreRoot, applyId, projectId, projectRoot})` returning a deep-frozen discriminated union — `{ok:true, outcome:"applied"|"already_applied", apply_id, run_id, project_id, revision, completed_step_count}` or `{ok:false, reason, apply_id, run_id, project_id, revision, status, completed_step_count}` over the closed 14-reason execution vocabulary `invalid_options | apply_not_prepared | artifact_missing | artifact_modified | project_binding_mismatch | project_busy | project_read_only | unsupported_filesystem | unsupported_metadata | unsupported_project_entry | project_conflict | recovery_required | state_persist_failed | internal_error`; the writer never transitions the journal to `failed` and never rolls anything back. The journal schema is v2: the writer statuses `applying | applied | conflict | recovery_required` join the preparation statuses, with the mandatory `execution {completed_step_count, active_step: null | {step_index, temp_name}}` (null exactly for preparing/prepared/failed; `applied` requires completed == plan step count and no active step; `conflict` requires completed 0). Fixed attempt flow: whole-package load + verify (run workspace is never read — the payloads are the only byte source; a tampered payload/plan is `artifact_modified`/`state_persist_failed`), terminal-result projection for applied/conflict (retry returns the stored refusal), the realpath + root-binding re-verification, the lock, the read-only gates (`fstatvfs ST_RDONLY` + `faccessat W_OK` on the root and every existing unplanned ancestor), then the recovery walk: at `prepared` the whole plan is verified against the untouched project before any mutation (planned ancestors are verified by their own steps, every unplanned ancestor must exist as a gated real directory, removal containment through the real child listing; any divergence with zero durable progress is a clean `project_conflict` with a byte-identical project and a terminal `conflict` status), at `applying`/`recovery_required` the positional recovery walk requires every completed step to sit exactly at its recorded postcondition and every pending path to sit at a plan-consistent state (precondition of the first pending step, an adopted intermediate postcondition, or the final planned state — planned substitution ancestors may legitimately be non-directories in the current project and are resolved through the plan); a divergence with progress is a `recovery_required` refusal that keeps the frontier intent and leaves the state retryable, a passing walk re-enters `applying` and rolls forward. Steps run strictly sequentially from `completed_step_count + 1` with one durable progress commit after each verified mutation; every `publish_regular_file` owns its temp through the journal (the intent `{step_index, temp_name}` is committed BEFORE `O_CREAT|O_EXCL`, the name is `.apply-tmp-<32hex>`, and the recovery rules are: complete temp (digest match, mode 0600 or the desired mode) → adopt and publish, incomplete → clean and recreate under a fresh committed intent, shape mismatch (foreign object) → `recovery_required`, the foreign object is never removed, the intent stays); publication is `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` for absent targets (EEXIST → adopt-if-exact or conflict; EINVAL/ENOSYS/ENOTSUP → typed `unsupported_filesystem` leaving the state retryable) or `renameat` for replacements after a fresh precondition re-verification (the non-cooperative verify→rename race is a documented limitation); directories/symlinks/removals follow the same observe→mutate→fsync→verify→commit discipline, each step re-gates metadata through the fd probe before mutating. Temp cleanup is ownership-proved only (recorded name + shape), crash-marked faults rethrow without cleanup or journal writes (the fault windows simulate process death), and unexpected errors leave the state `applying` for retry with the cleaned own temp. Durability follows the shared journal substrate (`not_committed` → previous snapshot authoritative, `durability_unknown` → adopted candidate; both mapped to `state_persist_failed` results while the on-disk state stays recoverable). The deep-frozen results and diagnostics are content-free (no project paths, plan paths, payload bytes or temp names in results). The race matrix proves the lock across real OS processes (busy loser with zero effects, serialization of two executions, crash-then-retry, inspect during a mid-execution pause, prepare alongside an execution, independent projects). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/pipeline_v2_apply_project_lock.test.ts` (7 + a real cross-process probe), `pipeline_v2_apply_writer.test.ts` (25 — every step kind against the real fd adapter, all six type substitutions, large binary payload, adoption, zero-step plans, the conflict/RO/unsupported/foreign-temp battery, the canary scans and the exact end-state proofs), `pipeline_v2_apply_writer_recovery.test.ts` (20 — the W0–W20 crash windows, temp-ownership recovery, restart-at-every-prefix, healing of a removed foreign temp, `apply_not_prepared` edges) and `pipeline_v2_apply_writer_race.test.ts` (7 cross-process races). Still unwired: HTTP/UI/CLI/routing surfaces for apply, the deployment RW mount, schema v11, selective apply and rollback.
